## I / 第一章

### I / I

===Original===
He was riding the hundred miles from T’o Tlakai to Tsé Lani to attend a dance, or rather, for the horse-racing that would come afterwards. The sun was hot and his belly was empty, but life moved in rhythm with his pony loping steadily as an engine down the miles. He was lax in the saddle, leaning back, arm swinging the rope’s end in time to the horse’s lope. His new red headband was a bright colour among the embers of the sun-struck desert, undulating like a moving graph of the pony’s lope, or the music of his song﻿—

===Chinese===
他骑马赶着一百英里的路，从特奥·特莱凯（T’o Tlakai）到特塞拉尼（Tsé Lani）去参加一场舞会——或者不如说，是为着舞会之后的那场赛马。日头毒，肚里空，可生命正随着他的小马合着节拍涌动——那小马像一台机器，平稳地一英里又一英里大步跑下去。他松松地坐在鞍上，身子后仰，一条胳膊甩着绳头，跟马的步子合着拍。他那条崭新的红头带，在被烈日烤成余烬色的荒漠里一点鲜亮，随着小马的跑动一起一伏，像一道描出马步的活动波形线，又像他歌里的乐线——

===Original===
“*Nashdui bik’é dinni, eya-a, eyo-o﻿ ﻿…*
						

						Wildcat’s feet hurt, *eya-a, eyo-o﻿ ﻿…*”

===Chinese===
“Nashdui bik’é dinni，哎呀-啊，哎哟-哦……

						野猫的脚疼啊，哎呀-啊，哎哟-哦……”

===Original===
Rope’s end, shoulders, song, all moved together, and life flowed in one stream. He threw his head back to sing louder, and listened to the echo from the cliffs on his right. He was thinking about a bracelet he should make, with four smooth bars running together, and a turquoise in the middle﻿—if he could get the silver. He wished he could work while riding; everything was so perfect then, like the prayers, *hozoji nashad*, travelling in beauty. His hands, his feet, his head, his insides all were *hozoji*, all were very much alive. He whooped and struck up the Magpie Song till the empty desert resounded﻿—

===Chinese===
绳头、肩膀、歌，全都一起动，生命流成一道。他把头往后一仰，唱得更响，听那歌声从右边的峭壁上撞回的回声。他心里正盘算着要打的一只手镯：四道光滑的银条并排奔流，当中嵌一颗绿松石——只要他弄得到银子。他巴不得能一边骑马一边做活计；那时候样样都那么圆满，就像那些祷词，霍卓吉·纳沙德（hozoji nashad），在美之中行进。他的手、他的脚、他的脑袋、他的五脏六腑，全都霍卓吉，全都活得旺旺的。他高声呼哨，唱起喜鹊歌，唱得空荡荡的沙漠四处回响——

===Original===
“A-a-a-iné, a-a-a-iné,
						

						Ya-a-iné-ainé, ko-ya-ainé﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
“啊-啊-啊-伊内，啊-啊-啊-伊内，

						呀-啊-伊内-伊内，阔-呀-伊内……”

===Original===
He was lean, slender, tall, and handsome, Laughing Boy, with a new cheap headband and a borrowed silver belt to make ragged clothes look fine.

===Chinese===
他精瘦，细条，高挑，俊朗，这就是笑娃（Laughing Boy）；一条崭新的廉价头带，一条借来的银腰带，让一身破衣裳显出体面。

===Original===
At noon, having no money, he begged coffee from a trader at Chinlee and went on, treasuring his hunger because of the feasting to come. Now he began to meet Navajos of all ages, riding to the dance. The young men bunched together﻿—a line of jingling bridles, dark, excited faces, flashing silver, turquoise, velveteen shirts, dirty, ragged overalls, a pair of plaid calico leggings, a pair of turkey-red ones. Some of them were heavy with jewelry; Horse Giver’s Son wore over four hundred dollars in silver alone; most of them had more than Laughing Boy. They stopped to look at his bow-guard, which he himself had made.

===Chinese===
正午时分，他身无分文，在钦利（Chinlee）向一个开铺子的讨了些咖啡，又上了路，把饥饿当宝贝似的攒着，因为前头有一场盛宴等着。这会儿他开始遇到各色年纪的纳瓦霍人，都骑马往舞会去。小伙子们扎成堆——一长串叮当作响的笼头，一张张黑亮兴奋的脸，闪闪烁烁的银器、绿松石，平绒衬衫，脏污破烂的工装裤，一条格子印花布的绑腿，一条火鸡红的绑腿。有些人浑身披挂着重饰；赠马人之子（Horse Giver’s Son）单是身上戴的银器就值四百多块；大多数人戴的都比笑娃的多。他们停下来看他腕上的弓护腕（bow-guard），那是他自己亲手做的。

===Original===
“I am a good jeweller,” he said, elated; “I make silver run like a song.”

===Chinese===
“我是个好银匠，”他得意地说；“我打的银子，跑起来像一支歌。”

===Original===
“You should make a song about yourself,” they told him, “and teach the burros to sing it.”

===Chinese===
“你该给自己编一支歌，”他们跟他说，“再教毛驴们去唱。”

===Original===
“Have you had any rain up by T’o Tlakai?”

===Chinese===
“特奥·特莱凯那边落过雨吗？”

===Original===
“No, it is just like last year. It is the devil. The grass is all dried up and the sheep are dying.”

===Chinese===
“没有，跟去年一个样。准是魔鬼作祟。草全枯死了，羊也快死了。”

===Original===
“They had a cloudburst over by T’isya Lani. It washed out the dam.”

===Chinese===
“提斯亚·拉尼（T’isya Lani）那边下过一场骤雨，把水坝冲垮了。”

===Original===
“It washed out the missionary’s house, they say. His wife ran out in something thin and got wet, they say.”

===Chinese===
“还把传教士的房子冲垮了，听说。他老婆穿着件薄衫跑出来，淋了个透，听说。”

===Original===
“*Ei-yei!*”

===Chinese===
“咿-耶！”

===Original===
Tall Hunter and his wife drove past in a brand-new buckboard behind two fast-trotting, grey mules. He owned over five hundred head of horses, and his wife had thick strings of turquoise and coral around her neck.

===Chinese===
高猎人（Tall Hunter）和他老婆赶着一辆崭新的四轮轻便马车过去了，拉车的是两匹快步小跑的灰骡子。他有五百多匹马，他老婆脖子上挂着一串串厚实的绿松石和珊瑚。

===Original===
“His brother is in jail for stealing cattle, they say.”

===Chinese===
“听说他兄弟偷牛，蹲了监狱。”

===Original===
“What is jail?” asked Laughing Boy.

===Chinese===
“监狱是什么？”笑娃问。

===Original===
Slender Hair explained: “It is something the American Chief does to you. He puts you in a room of stone, like a Moqui house, only it is dark and you can’t get out. People die there, they say. They haven’t any room; they can’t see anything, they say. I do not like to talk about it.”

===Chinese===
细发（Slender Hair）解释道：“那是美国酋长（American Chief）用来治你的一样东西。他把人关进一间石头屋子，像莫基人（Moqui）的房子，只是里头黑黢黢的，出不去。人会死在里头，听说。人在里头没有一点空儿；什么都看不见，听说。我不爱说这个。”

===Original===
Laughing Boy thought, I should rather die. He wanted to ask more, but was ashamed to show his ignorance before these southern Navajos, many of whom wore hats like Americans, and who knew so much of Americans’ ways.

===Chinese===
笑娃心想，我宁可死。他还想多问，却羞于在这些南边的纳瓦霍人面前露怯——他们当中好多人都戴着美国人那样的帽子，懂得那么多美国人的门道。

===Original===
They raced. His horse was tired, but it won by a nose, which was just as well, since he had bet his bow-guard. Now he had six dollars. He hoped there would be gambling.

===Chinese===
他们赛了一场。他的马累了，还是以一鼻之差赢了——这也算万幸，因为他把弓护腕押上去打了赌。这下他手里有六块钱了。他盼着有赌局。

===Original===
Tsé Lani showed a distant bonfire in the dusk, with mounted Indians moving in on it like spokes of a wheel. About two hundred young men came together half a mile away, making their ponies prance, exchanging greetings. Crooked Ear carried the ceremonial wand. Now they all lined up, with the dull, red sunset behind their black figures. They started going like getting off to a race, right into a gallop, yelling. Over by the fire was shouting, and another line tearing towards them. The world was full of a roar of hooves and two walls of noise rushing together, the men leaning forward over their horses’ necks, mouths wide. “*E-e-e-e-e!*” They met in a great swirl of plunging, dodging horses, and swept on all together, whooping for dear life, with the staff in front of them, almost onto the fire, then dissolved with jingling of bits, laughter, and casual jokes as they unsaddled by the pool.

===Chinese===
暮色里，特塞拉尼远远现出一堆篝火，骑马的印第安人从四面八方涌向火堆，像车轮的辐条。约莫两百个小伙子在半英里外聚拢，催得小马踏着碎步，彼此招呼。歪耳朵（Crooked Ear）捧着仪杖。这会儿他们全体排成列，黑黢黢的身影背后衬着暗红的落日。他们起跑就像出赛一般，一上来便纵蹄飞奔，齐声呐喊。火堆那边喊声大作，另一列人马朝他们猛冲过来。满世界都是马蹄的轰响，两堵声浪的墙迎头相撞，人人伏在马颈上，嘴张得老大。“咿-咿-咿-咿-咿！”两队人马撞进一处，卷起好大一团人仰马翻、左躲右闪的漩涡，又合成一股冲将过去，豁出命似的呼哨，仪杖在前头开路，险些冲上火堆，随后哗啦散开，笼头嚼子叮当乱响，一片笑声和随口的打趣，在池边卸下了马鞍。

===Original===
The steady motion of excitement was slowed then, in the last of the day, by the rocks and the piñons, by the reflection of the sky in the pool where flat, vague silhouettes of horses stooped to drink. The voices of many people, the twinkling of fires continued the motif, joining the time of quiet with elation past and to come; a little feeling of expectation in Laughing Boy’s chest, a joyful emptiness, part hunger and part excitement.

===Chinese===
随后，在这天将尽的时分，兴奋的奔流被乱石和皮尼翁松放缓了，被池水里映着的天空放缓了——马匹低头饮水，在水面投下扁扁的、影影绰绰的轮廓。众人的语声、火光的明灭延续着这个调子，把已过的欢腾和将来的欢腾缝合进此刻的安静里；笑娃胸口有一点小小的期待，一种快活的空落，一半是饿，一半是兴奋。

===Original===
He tended his pony minutely. The little mare had had two days of loping; shortly he wanted to race her; three days of rest would not be too much. She was his only horse; he had traded two others for her. She was tough, as a horse had to be to live at all in the North country. He ran his hands down her withers, feeling the lean, decisive muscles. In all that section, from Dennihuitso to Biltabito, from T’o Tlikahn to To Baka, where he knew every horse by sight, she was the best, but she would meet some competition here. He felt as if she were his own creation, like the bow-guard; at least he had selected her, as he had chosen the soft blue turquoise in the ornament. Little, compact, all black save for the tiny white spot on her forehead, she had the ugly Roman nose of character. She was like an arrow notched to a taut bowstring﻿—a movement of the hand would release level flight swiftly to a mark.

===Chinese===
他把小马照料得无微不至。这匹小母马已经连着两天大步跑；过不多久他还要让她出赛；歇上三天也不嫌多。她是他仅有的一匹马；是拿另外两匹换来的。她皮实——在北边那地方，一匹马要活命就得这么皮实。他的手顺着她的耆甲往下捋，摸着那精瘦而果断的肌肉。那一带地方，从德内霍佐（Dennihuitso）到比尔塔比托（Biltabito），从特奥·特莱凯（T’o Tlikahn）到托巴卡（To Baka），哪匹马他见了都认得，她是顶好的一匹，只是到了这儿怕是要遇上些对手。他觉得她就像自己造出来的东西，像那只弓护腕；至少她是自己挑中的，就像他从饰物上挑中那颗柔和的蓝绿松石。小小个头，结实的身量，浑身乌黑，只有额心一点白星，长着一只鹰钩鼻，丑，却丑出个性。她像一支扣在绷紧弓弦上的箭——手一动，平直的箭去便迅疾地直奔标的。

===Original===
He was thinking some of these things, half hearing the noises of the people. Just like the prayer, “travelling in beauty.” It would be good to be a singer as well, to express all these things through the prayers. He would like to know many of them, to learn to conduct the Mountain Chant, and know all the beautiful stories behind the songs and ceremonies inside the Dark Circle of Branches. That would be really on the trail of beauty; to work in silver and turquoise, own soft-moving ponies, and lead the Mountain Chant. Just thinking about it was good. It made him feel cool inside.

===Chinese===
他心里转着这些念头，人声嘈嘈，一半进耳一半不闻。就像那祷词，“在美之中行进”。要是还能做个颂唱师（Singer），也很好，借祈祷把这些东西一一唱出来。他想多懂得些，学到能主持山地颂仪（Mountain Chant），把暗枝圈（Dark Circle of Branches）里那些歌与仪式背后的美故事统统知道。那才算真正踏上了美的小径；以银和绿松石做活计，养几匹步子轻柔的小马，再执掌山地颂仪。光是想想就是好的。想得他心里一片清凉。

===Original===
“*Hozho hogan ladin nasha woyen﻿ ﻿…*
						

						In the house of happiness there I wander﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
“Hozho hogan ladin nasha woyen……

						在幸福之屋里，我在那里游荡……”

===Original===
All the time he was passing his hand along the pony’s neck, along her back, feeling the lines of tough muscles.

===Chinese===
他的手一路顺着小马的脖颈、顺着她的背抚过去，摸着一道道坚韧的肌肉。

===Original===
“*E-ya,* Grandfather, are you going to dance with the horse?” Jesting Squaw’s Son called over to him, “food is ready.”

===Chinese===
“咿呀，老爷爷，你要搂着马跳舞吗？”打趣婆娘之子（Jesting Squaw’s Son）朝他喊道，“饭好了。”

===Original===
“*Hakone!*” He returned abruptly to the quick-moving life of the dance. “I can eat it. I did not know you were coming.”

===Chinese===
“哈科内！”他猛地回到舞会上那活泛欢腾的日子里。“我吃得下。先前不知道你们也来了。”

===Original===
“I came when I heard you were to race your mare. I think there is money to be made, then, and I want to see her race.”

===Chinese===
“听说你要让母马出赛，我就赶来了。我想这回有钱可赚，也想看看她跑。”

===Original===
They went up arm in arm into the crowd, pushing their way into the circle around one of the fires. Busy housewives gave them coffee, the big pot of meat was passed over, and a flat, round loaf of rubbery, filling bread. The meat was the backbone of a yearling calf, boiled with corn. It was good. He munched joyfully, feeling his empty stomach fill, wadding himself with bread, washing it down with bitter coffee. A couple of Americans carrying their own plates dipped in gingerly. A Hopi, having collected everything he could possibly eat, sat down officiously beside them to air his school English and his bourgeois superiority.

===Chinese===
两人挽着胳膊走进人群，挤进一堆篝火四周的圈子。忙碌的主妇们递给他们咖啡，一大锅肉从头顶传过来，还有一块又圆又扁、筋道管饱、像胶皮似的面包。肉是一岁牛犊的脊骨，跟玉米一块儿煮的。好吃。他嚼得欢畅，觉出空肚子一点点填满，拿面包把自己塞得严严实实，再用苦咖啡往下冲。两个端着自家盘子的美国人小心翼翼地凑进来蘸了蘸。一个霍皮人（Hopi）把能吃得下的东西搜罗了个遍，煞有介事地在他俩旁边坐下，卖弄他的学堂英语和他那小市民的优越感。

### II / II

===Original===
A small drum beating rapidly concentrated the mixed noises into a staccato unison. Young men gathered about the drummer. Laughing Boy might have eaten more, but he left the fire immediately with Jesting Squaw’s Son. Someone led off high-pitched at full voice,

===Chinese===
一面小鼓急促地敲起来，把嘈嘈的声响收拢成一板一眼的齐一。小伙子们围到鼓手身边。笑娃本来还能再吃些，却立刻同打趣婆娘之子离了火堆。有人起了头，尖着嗓门放声唱：

===Original===
“Yo-o galeana, yo-o galeana, yo-o galeana﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
“哟-哦，加莱亚纳，哟-哦，加莱亚纳，哟-哦，加莱亚纳……”

===Original===
By the end of the second word the crowd was with him; more young men hurried up to join the diapason,

===Chinese===
唱到第二个词的末尾，人群已经跟上了他；更多小伙子赶上来，汇入那一片洪音，

===Original===
“Galeana ena, galeana eno, yo-o ay-e hena ena﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
“加莱亚纳-埃纳，加莱亚纳-埃诺，哟-哦 啊伊-埃 赫纳-埃纳……”

===Original===
They put their arms over each other’s shoulders, swaying in time to the one drum that ran like a dull, glowing thread through the singing, four hundred young men turning loose everything they had.

===Chinese===
他们胳膊搭着彼此的肩，随着那唯一的一面鼓摇荡；鼓声像一根幽幽发亮的线，从歌声里穿行而过，四百个小伙子把浑身的一切全都放开来。

===Original===
A bonfire twenty feet long flared to the left of them. Opposite, and to the right, the older people sat wrapped in their blankets. Behind them, men crouched in their saddles, heads and shoulders against the night sky, nodding time to the rhythm, silent, with here and there a reflection of firelight on a bit of silver, a dark face, or a horse’s eye.

===Chinese===
一堆二十英尺长的篝火在他们左边熊熊烧着。对面和右边，上了年纪的人裹着毯子坐着。他们身后，男人们蜷在马鞍上，头和肩膀衬着夜空，随节奏一点一点头，一声不响，四下里偶有一星火光，映在一小块银器上、一张黑脸膛上，或是一只马眼睛上。

===Original===
Twelve girls in single file stole into the open space, moving quietly and aloof as though the uproar of singing were petrified into a protective wall before it reached them. Only the pulse of the drum showed in their steps. They prowled back and forth before the line of young men, considering them with predatory judgment.

===Chinese===
十二个姑娘排成一列，悄悄溜进空场，步子又轻又冷，仿佛那鼎沸的歌声还没挨到她们身边，就先凝成了一堵护身的墙。只有鼓的脉动显在她们的脚步里。她们在小伙子们那一列跟前来回逡巡，用掂量猎物的眼光打量他们。

===Original===
Laughing Boy at the back of the crowd looked at them with mild interest; he liked to watch their suave movements and the rich display of blankets and jewelry. One caught his attention; he thought she had on more silver, coral, turquoise, and white shell than he had ever seen on any one person. He speculated on its value﻿—horses﻿—she must have a very rich mother, or uncles. She was too slender, seeming frail to dance in all that rich, heavy ornamentation. He wished she would move more into the firelight. She was well dressed to show off what she wore; silver and stones with soft highlights and deep shadows glowed against the night-blue velveteen of her blouse; oval plaques of silver were at her waist, and ceremonial jewels in the fringe of her sash. Her blue skirt swung with her short, calculated steps, ankle-length, above the dull red leggings and moccasins with silver buttons. The dark clothing, matching the night, was in contrast to the other dancers, even her blanket was mainly blue. He felt animosity towards her, dark and slight, like a wisp of grass﻿—only part of a woman. Her gaze, examining the singers, was too coolly appraising. Now she was looking at him. He threw his head back, losing himself in the singing. He wished he, too, had an American hat.

===Chinese===
笑娃在人群后头看着她们，带着淡淡的兴味；他爱看她们柔缓的步态，爱看毯子和首饰铺排出的阔绰。有一个攫住了他的注意；他觉得她身上的银子、珊瑚、绿松石和白贝，比他生平在任何人身上见过的都多。他掂估这份家当值多少——马——她娘准是个很有钱的，再不就是舅舅们有钱。她太瘦了，裹着那么一身又富丽又沉重的行头跳舞，简直显得单薄。他盼着她往火光里多挪几步。她这身穿戴，正是为了把身上的东西炫出来；银器和宝石柔光浅浅、阴影深深，在她夜蓝色平绒的衬衫上幽幽发亮；腰间缀着椭圆形的银牌，腰带的流苏上垂着仪礼的珠饰。蓝裙子随着她那短促而有算计的步子摆动，长可及踝，下面是暗红的绑腿和钉着银扣的鹿皮鞋。这身同夜色相合的深色衣裳，在众舞者中间显得独异，连她的毯子也大半是蓝的。他对她生出一股敌意——黑黑的，细细的，像一茎草——只是半个女人。她打量歌手们的目光，太冷静，太会估价。这会儿她正看他。他把头往后一仰，把自己丢进歌声里。他想，自己要是也有一顶美国人的帽子就好了。

===Original===
Her mincing steps took her out of sight. Jesting Squaw’s Son’s arm was over his shoulder, and on the other side another Indian, unknown, but young. Their life flowed together with all those others, complete to themselves, merged in one body of song, with the drumbeats for a heart,

===Chinese===
她踩着细碎的步子，走出了视线。打趣婆娘之子的一条胳膊搭在他肩上，另一边是另一个印第安人，不相识，也年轻。他们的生命同其余所有人汇作一流，各自完足，融进一个歌的身子，拿鼓点当心，

===Original===
“Yo-o galeana, yo-o galeana﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
“哟-哦，加莱亚纳，哟-哦，加莱亚纳……”

===Original===
Song followed song with a rush; when one ended, the next took up, as though the whole night would never suffice to pour out all that was in them.

===Chinese===
一支歌赶着一支歌地涌上来；一支收了尾，下一支立时接上，仿佛整个长夜也倒不尽他们心里的东西。

===Original===
Someone plucked at his blanket; then with another, stronger pull it was snatched from his shoulders. He whirled about. The men near him snickered. The frail girl held his blanket up toward him, mockingly.

===Chinese===
有人扯了扯他的毯子；接着更重地一拽，毯子整个儿从他肩上夺了去。他霍地转过身。近旁的男人们哧哧地笑。那瘦怯的姑娘把他的毯子朝他举起来，一脸的戏弄。

===Original===
“*Ahalani!*” she greeted him.

===Chinese===
“阿哈拉尼！”她跟他打了个招呼。

===Original===
He stood for a moment in feigned stupidity. He did not want to dance. The devil! Then with a sudden lunge he snatched the blanket. It was no use. She hung on with unexpected strength, digging her heels into the sand, laughing. The men on either side were watching over their shoulders with open joy.

===Chinese===
他装傻，愣头愣脑地站了片刻。他不想跳舞。见鬼！接着他猛地一扑，去夺毯子。没有用。她抓得死紧，力气大得出奇，脚跟往沙里蹬住，直笑。两边的男人们回过头来看，一个个乐得敞亮。

===Original===
“What’s the matter? I think your feet hurt, perhaps. I think you are bandy-legged, perhaps.”

===Chinese===
“怎么啦？我看你是脚疼了吧，兴许。我看你是罗圈腿了吧，兴许。”

===Original===
Girls didn’t usually say these things. He was shocked. Her clear, low voice turned the insults into music, bringing out to the full the rise and fall of a Navajo woman’s intonation. All the time they tugged against each other, her long eyes were talking. He had seen girls’ eyes talk before as they pulled at the blanket, but these were clear as words. He wanted desperately to be back among the men. He nearly pulled her over, but she hung on, and her eyes seemed to be making a fool of him.

===Chinese===
姑娘家平常不说这些话。他吃了一惊。她那清亮低沉的嗓音，把这些羞辱化成了音乐，把纳瓦霍女人声腔里的起起落落唱了个十足。两人拉拉扯扯的当口，她那双长长的眼睛一直在说话。姑娘们夺毯子时眼睛会说话，他从前也见过，可这一双，明白得就像说出口的话。他一心想回到男人们中间去。他差点儿把她拽了个跟头，她偏死抓住不放，一双眼睛像是在把他当傻子耍。

===Original===
Suddenly he gave up. She led him around behind the men, not speaking to him, uninterested. He pulled his end of the blanket over his shoulders, assuming the conventional pose of resistance, setting each foot before the other reluctantly, in response to her dragging. He watched her closely, but her grip did not slacken. Out in the clear space she transferred her hands to his belt. He pulled his blanket to his chin, masking enjoyment in a pose of contemptuous tolerance, like the other men dancing there.

===Chinese===
他忽然撒了手。她领着他绕到男人们身后，不同他说话，一副没兴致的样子。他把自己这一头的毯子拽上肩，摆出那副照例的抗拒架势，被她拖着，一步挨一步，老大不情愿。他细细地看她，她的手却毫不松劲。到了空场上，她把两只手挪到他的腰带上。他把毯子一直拉到下巴，把受用藏在满不在乎的隐忍姿态里，像在那儿跳舞的其他男人一样。

===Original===
The solemn turning of the couples contrasted with the free release of the singers: this was a religious ceremony and a rustic, simple pleasure, the happiness of a natural people to whom but few things happen. They were traditional and grave in their revelry.

===Chinese===
一双双人庄重地旋转，同歌手们无拘无束的放散恰成对照：这是一场宗教仪式，也是一种乡野的、简单的乐子，一个难得有事儿临头的自然民族的快乐。他们在狂欢里守着传统，一本正经。

===Original===
According to the etiquette, whenever there is a rest, the man asks what forfeit he must pay; by the length of time taken by the girl to get down to a reasonable figure, he gages her liking for his company. The music paused an instant for singers to catch their breath. He made a feeble attempt to get away, then asked,

===Chinese===
按规矩，每逢乐声歇息，男人便问他该付多少罚金；姑娘用多长时间才把价钱落到一个像样的数目，他便以此来掂量她中不中意他的陪伴。乐声停了一瞬，好让歌手们换口气。他软绵绵地挣了一下，随后问道：

===Original===
“How much?”

===Chinese===
“多少？”

===Original===
“Ten cents.”

===Chinese===
“一毛钱。”

===Original===
The prompt answer astonished him. He paid the forfeit, still staring at her, chagrined, and furious at the blank, correct impassiveness of her face, at the same time noting delicately chiselled features, set of firm lips, long eyes that in their lack of expression were making fun of him. Ten cents! Already! With a splendid gesture he swept his blanket round him, stalking back to the singers.

===Chinese===
这脱口而出的回答叫他吃了一惊。他付了罚金，眼睛还盯着她，又懊又恼——恼她那张脸规矩得没一丝表情；同时又注意到那眉眼雕得精细，嘴唇抿得坚定，长长的眼睛一片空无表情，偏就在这空无表情里耍笑他。一毛钱！张口就是！他一记漂亮的手势，把毯子往身上一裹，昂首阔步回到歌手们中间。

===Original===
He was set to lose himself in the songs, but he watched the girl drag out a man nearly as tall as himself. Instead of dancing in the usual way, they held each other face to face and close to, each with one hand on the other’s shoulder. It was shocking; and why had she not done it with him? But she had let him go the first time he had asked. She had insulted him, she was too thin, and probably ill-behaved.

===Chinese===
他正要把自己重新丢进歌里，却看见那姑娘拽出一个几乎同他一样高的男人。两人不按平常的跳法，倒面对面、贴得很近，各把一只手搭在对方肩上。真不成体统；她怎么不跟他这样跳？可他头一回开口，她就放他走了呀。她羞辱了他；她太瘦了，多半还没规矩。

### III / III

===Original===
Jesting Squaw’s Son’s arm was over his shoulder, his ears were full of the beat and uproar of music. He was a man among men, swinging with them, marking the rhythm, releasing his joy of living in ordered song.

===Chinese===
打趣婆娘之子的胳膊搭在他肩上，他满耳是鼓点的敲击和音乐的喧腾。他是男人们中间的一个男人，同他们一起摇荡，应着节拍，把生之欢畅放进整齐的歌里，释放出来。

===Original===
“Nashdui bik’é dinni, eya-a, eyo-o﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
“Nashdui bik’é dinni，哎呀-啊，哎哟-哦……”

===Original===
A late moon rose, cool and remote, dissociated. They brought another tree up to the bonfire, standing it on end a moment so that the hot light played on its dead branches; then they let it topple over and fall, sending up in its place a tree of moving sparks into the blackness.

===Chinese===
一轮迟升的月亮起来了，清冷，邈远，同这一切不相干。他们又拖来一棵树，添上篝火，先把它竖直了竖了片刻，让灼热的光在枯枝上跳动；然后由它翻倒、跌落，一树移动的火星在原来的地方腾起来，升进黑暗里。

===Original===
Night passed its middle and stood towards day. The girls moved off together in single file, blankets drawn over heads, worn out by the night of unremitting dancing. The older people fell rapidly away. Inert forms like mummies stretched out in their blankets by the embers of the feast fires. Most of the young men gave in, leaving about a hundred knotted in a mass, still hard at it. They surrounded the drummer, an older man, intently serious over drawing forth from a bit of hide stretched across the mouth of a jar rapidly succeeding beats that entered the veins and moved in the blood. He played with rhythm as some men play with design; now a quick succession of what seemed meaningless strokes hurried forward, now the beat stumbled, paused, caught up again and whirled away. Devotedly intent over his work, his long experience, his strength and skill expended themselves in quick, wise movements of the wrist, calling forth a summation of life from a piece of goatskin and a handful of baked clay, while younger men about him swayed and rocked in recurrent crescendos.

===Chinese===
夜过了中天，朝白天那边站。姑娘们排成一列走开了，毯子蒙着头，一整夜不歇的舞蹈把她们熬乏了。上了年纪的人很快散尽。木乃伊似的一具具身子，裹着毯子直挺挺躺在节庆火堆的余烬旁。小伙子们大半也服了输，剩下百来个抱作一团，还在苦干。他们围着那鼓手——一个上了年纪的人，神情专注而严肃，一门心思要从那一小块绷在瓦罐口上的皮膜里，抽出一记赶着一记的鼓点来；鼓点钻进血管，在血里运行。他摆弄节奏，就像有些人摆弄花样：忽而一串看似无义的敲击急急赶路，忽而鼓点绊了一下，停住，又追上来，打着旋儿跑远。他一片虔敬地埋头于自己的活计，多年的经验、他的力气和手艺，全都使在腕子敏捷而聪明的动作里，从一块山羊皮和一捧烧成的陶土上，唤出生命的一个总结；他四周的年轻人则在一浪高过一浪的渐强里摇晃、颠动。

===Original===
Night stood towards morning, now night grew old. Now the first white line was traced across the east far away, outlining distant cliffs. Now it was first light, and Dawn Boy was upon them. The drumming stopped; suddenly the desert was empty and vast. Young men, whose bodies felt like empty shells and whose heads still buzzed with songs, moved down to drink at the pool.

===Chinese===
夜朝早晨那边站着，这会儿夜老了。此刻，遥远东方描出了第一道白线，勾出远处峭壁的轮廓。此刻天光初动，黎明少年（Dawn Boy）降临到他们头上。鼓声停了；沙漠忽然空旷而浩大。小伙子们身子像空了的壳，脑袋里还嗡嗡响着歌，挪到池边去喝水。

===Original===
“Hayotlcatl Ashki, Natahni﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
“Hayotlcatl Ashki，Natahni……”

===Original===
Laughing Boy breathed his prayer to himself, feeling a moment of loneliness,

===Chinese===
笑娃默诵着自己的祷词，感到一阵孤独，

===Original===
“Dawn Boy, Chief﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
“黎明少年，首领……”

===Original===
He rolled up in his blanket. When he rode his horse in the races, people would see; he would ride past the people, back to T’o Tlakai, with all his winnings. That girl was strong for one who looked so slight. He would make a bracelet about her, thin silver, with stars surrounded by stone-knife-edge. His horse came to stand by him. He roused himself to look at it, struggled awake, and dragged out the corn from under his saddle.

===Chinese===
他把自己卷进毯子里。等他骑马出赛的时候，人们会看见的；他会带着赢来的一切，从人们跟前骑马走过，回特奥·特莱凯去。那个姑娘，瞧着那么单薄，劲儿倒不小。他要为她打一只手镯：细银的，缀着星星，星星四围绕着石刀錾出的刃边。他的马走过来，站在他身旁。他撑起身子看了看她，挣扎着醒过来，从马鞍底下拖出玉米。

===Original===
He pulled his blanket over his head. All different things melted together into one conception of a night not like any other.

===Chinese===
他把毯子拉过头顶。种种各不相同的东西，融成了一个整体——一个不似寻常的夜。

## Chapter II / 第二章

### I / I

===Original===
Someone was calling him,

===Chinese===
有人在喊他，

===Original===
“*Ei shichai, ei-yei!*”

===Chinese===
“Ei shichai, ei-yei!（喂，老爷爷，哎咿！）”

===Original===
He opened his eyes, staring upward at the face of Jesting Squaw’s Son that laughed at him as he sat high above him in the saddle. The face was in shadow under the circle of his stiff-brimmed hat, cut out against the gleaming, hard sky. The sun was halfway up.

===Chinese===
他睁开眼睛，怔怔地朝上望去，打趣婆娘之子（Jesting Squaw’s Son）高高地坐在马鞍上，正笑着低头看他。那张脸笼在硬檐帽投下的一圈暗影里，衬着亮晃晃、硬邦邦的天空，像剪出来的一般。太阳已经升到一半高了。

===Original===
“Wake up, Grandfather! Big Tall Man is going to play tree-pushing against everybody.”

===Chinese===
“醒醒，老爷爷！大高个（Big Tall Man）要跟大伙儿比推树了。”

===Original===
“*Hakone!*” He was up at the word. “Give me a smoke, Grandfather.” He climbed up behind his friend’s saddle. “Come on.”

===Chinese===
“哈科内（Hakone，快走！）”他闻声一跃而起。“老爷爷，给我支烟抽。”他爬上马去，坐在朋友鞍后。“走吧。”

===Original===
They stopped for coffee at a hogan near the pool, where the woman of the house mocked him for sleeping late.

===Chinese===
他们在水潭边一座霍根屋（hogan，纳瓦霍人泥木筑的小屋）前停下喝咖啡，屋里的女主人笑话他睡到这般晚。

===Original===
The people were gathered in a little box canyon, where fire had destroyed a number of scrub oaks and piñons under one wall near a seep of water. There they were dividing into two groups, according to whether they backed Big Tall Man or Man Hammer, the policeman from over by T’o Nanasdési. Hill Singer rode back and forth between, collecting and announcing the bets. Most of the money was on Big Tall Man, and there were few takers. Laughing Boy could not place any. He saw that girl sitting among the neutral spectators.

===Chinese===
人们聚在一处小小的箱形峡谷里，一面岩壁底下、一眼渗泉近旁，野火烧掉过好些矮栎和皮尼翁松。众人正在那里分成两拨，全看各人押的是大高个，还是锤子汉（Man Hammer）——从托纳纳斯代西（T’o Nanasdési）那边过来的那个警察。山颂唱师（Hill Singer）骑马在两拨人之间来来回回，收注、报注。钱大多押在大高个身上，敢接的没几个。笑娃（Laughing Boy）却一个注也押不上。他瞧见那个姑娘坐在不下注的看客中间。

===Original===
“Who is that girl,” he asked Slender Hair﻿—“the one who had so much hard goods on last night?”

===Chinese===
“那个姑娘是谁？”他问细发（Slender Hair）——“就是昨儿晚上戴了那么多硬货的那个？”

===Original===
“She is called Slim Girl, I think. She comes from down by the railroad track, from near Chiziai, I think.”

===Chinese===
“她叫瘦姑娘（Slim Girl），我想。她是从铁道边上下来的，家就在奇扎伊（Chiziai）附近，我想。”

===Original===
Big Tall Man and Man Hammer moved up to two dead trees of roughly the same size. Hill Singer and Hurries to War were judging. Now they pushed and strained at the trees, digging their feet in the sand, heaving shoulders. Big Tall Man’s tree began to crack; then suddenly it went over. People exclaimed and laughed. After that nobody more wanted to play against him.

===Chinese===
大高个和锤子汉走到两棵粗细相仿的枯树跟前。山颂唱师和急赴战场（Hurries to War）做裁判。两人各自发力推树，脚往沙里蹬，肩膀一耸一耸地顶。大高个那棵树开始咔咔作响，跟着猛地翻倒下去。众人又是惊呼又是大笑。打那以后，再没有人想跟他比了。

===Original===
Then they had wrestling for the young men. Laughing Boy bet a little and lost a couple of dollars. There was a tall man wearing an American shirt and trousers and a hat, who made a great deal of noise about himself. He beat one challenger easily. Laughing Boy recognized the man who danced so outrageously last night.

===Chinese===
接着是小伙子们的摔跤。笑娃押了点小注，输了两块钱。有个高个子，穿着美国式的衬衫和长裤，戴着帽子，把自己咋呼得震天响。他轻轻松松撂倒了一个来挑战的。笑娘认出来了，正是昨儿夜里舞跳得那么放浪形骸的家伙。

===Original===
“Who is that?” he asked.

===Chinese===
“那是谁？”他问。

===Original===
“That is Red Man. He comes from down by the railroad.”

===Chinese===
“那是红汉子（Red Man）。也是从铁道边上下来的。”

===Original===
“He is too skinny. I am going to beat him.”

===Chinese===
“他太瘦了。我要撂倒他。”

===Original===
He challenged Red Man.

===Chinese===
他便去向红汉子讨战。

===Original===
“How much will you bet on yourself?”

===Chinese===
“你押自己多少？”

===Original===
“I have three-fifty and this bow-guard.”

===Chinese===
“我有三块五，还有这只弓护腕。”

===Original===
“That makes eight-fifty.”

===Chinese===
“那就凑成八块五。”

===Original===
“The bow-guard is worth more; it is worth ten dollars.”

===Chinese===
“这弓护腕值得多些；值十块钱。”

===Original===
The man looked at it judgingly. “Well, it is worth eight. That makes eleven-fifty. Why don’t you bet your belt?”

===Chinese===
那人端详着它，作出评判的样子。“嗯，值八块。这就十一块五了。你怎么不把腰带也押上？”

===Original===
“It is not mine.”

===Chinese===
“那不是我的。”

===Original===
“So you are sure you are going to lose, I think?”

===Chinese===
“这么说，你是笃定自己要输了，我想？”

===Original===
Laughing Boy did not like this Indian. “No; I’m going to throw you right away.”

===Chinese===
笑娃不喜欢这个印第安人。“不；我这就把你撂倒。”

===Original===
“*Ei-yei!* Then bet the belt. See, mine is better than yours. It has turquoise in it.”

===Chinese===
“咿-耶！那就把腰带押上。瞧，我这条比你的强，上面镶着绿松石。”

===Original===
“All right.”

===Chinese===
“行。”

===Original===
They piled up the stakes: three-fifty and a bow-guard against eleven-fifty, belt against belt. The belt was worth money, but it was ugly, Laughing Boy thought. He did not like this man. He knew how to dance improperly.

===Chinese===
双方把注码堆在了一处：三块五加一只弓护腕，对十一块五，腰带对腰带。那腰带值几个钱，可是丑，笑娃心想。他不喜欢这个人。这人是懂得怎样把舞跳得不成体统的。

===Original===
They stood face to face. They laid hands on each other. As he felt the man in his grasp, Laughing Boy saw all red. He and his enemy were alone in space with anger. He heaved with all his skill and strength, like one possessed. The other grunted and strained, then suddenly gave way﻿—a fall.

===Chinese===
两人面对面站定，互相搭住了手。一攥住对方，笑娃眼前便只剩一片红。他和他的仇敌连同满腔怒气，孤零零地悬在天地之间。他拿出全身的技巧和力气发力，像着了魔一般。对方吭吭哧哧地死命顶住，忽然便垮了——摔了一跤。

===Original===
Red Man arose puzzled and angry. He went at the next bout seriously. He would have liked to foul, but he was afraid of Hurries to War. Laughing Boy, staring over his opponent’s shoulder, saw Slim Girl’s face as she watched, half smiling. Again he ceased seeing, his jaws clamped fiercely together, he gripped close and lifted, then over﻿—now! A fall, and a hard one.

===Chinese===
红汉子爬起来，又懵又恼。下一局他上了心。他本想使个阴招，可又怕急赴战场。笑娃的目光越过对手的肩头，看见瘦姑娘正望着这边，嘴角含着半丝笑意。他眼前又是一空，牙关狠狠咬紧，贴身抱住往上一提，随即翻了过去——着！又是一跤，摔得结结实实。

===Original===
Red Man was shaken, and came into the next bout without confidence. The fall he got was worse than the others.

===Chinese===
红汉子被摔懵了，再上场时已经没了底气。这一跤摔得比先前哪一跤都重。

===Original===
“Take the goods,” Hill Singer told the winner.

===Chinese===
“把彩头拿去吧。”山颂唱师对赢的人说。

===Original===
“Put up your horse, and try again. You might get your belt back,” Laughing Boy mocked.

===Chinese===
“把你的马押上，再来一回。兴许还能把腰带赢回去呢。”笑娃打趣道。

===Original===
“We are going to play *Tset Dilth* on the fourth night, then bring your belts.” Red Man was feeling the back of his head.

===Chinese===
“第四天夜里我们要玩 Tset Dilth（一种赌戏），到时候把你们的腰带都带来。”红汉子正揉着自己的后脑勺。

===Original===
“I shall be there.”

===Chinese===
“我准到。”

===Original===
Laughing Boy gathered up his winnings. He looked around. Slim Girl had disappeared. He was hungry. He hunted up Jesting Squaw’s Son.

===Chinese===
笑娃把赢头收拢到一处。他四下里张望，瘦姑娘已经不见了。他饿了，去寻打趣婆娘之子。

===Original===
“It is noon. Let us go eat.”

===Chinese===
“晌午了。走，吃饭去。”

### II / II

===Original===
Many visitors were at the hogans scattered about Tsé Lani. There was much food and much talk. Where they went, they reclined on sheepskins, while two small naked boys brought ears of corn as they were roasted, and calm women set broiled goats’ ribs and corn bread before them. They ate at leisure, having a pleasant feeling of being at a party, yet at ease, and enjoying their appetites. Gossip was exchanged; they discussed crops, sheep, rain, and horses.

===Chinese===
特塞拉尼（Tsé Lani）四下散布的霍根屋里来了许多客人。吃食多，话也多。他们走到哪家，就在哪家倚着羊皮半躺下来，两个光屁股的小不点儿把烤好的玉米一穗穗捧来，安详的妇人们把烤羊排、玉米饼摆到他们面前。他们从容不迫地吃着，心里有一股做客的愉快，却又自在安适，胃口大开。众人交换着闲话，谈庄稼，谈羊，谈雨水，谈马。

===Original===
“I hear you have a horse to race,” a man said to Laughing Boy.

===Chinese===
“听说你有匹马要赛。”有人对笑娃说。

===Original===
“Yes, I have a good one.”

===Chinese===
“是，我有匹好马。”

===Original===
“A man brought a tall bay over from Tsézhin; it is very fast, they say.”

===Chinese===
“有人从特塞津（Tsézhin）那边牵了匹高大的枣红马来，据说快得很。”

===Original===
“We shall see. I shall bet on my horse.”

===Chinese===
“到时候见分晓。我要押我自己的马。”

===Original===
“Where did you get your bow-guard?”

===Chinese===
“你这弓护腕是哪儿来的？”

===Original===
“I made it.”

===Chinese===
“我自己做的。”

===Original===
“I’ll give you six dollars for it.”

===Chinese===
“我出六块钱买它。”

===Original===
“I don’t want to sell it.”

===Chinese===
“我不卖。”

===Original===
The man changed the subject. “Did you hear about Red Goat? His wives put his saddle outside the door, they say.”

===Chinese===
那人岔开了话头。“红山羊（Red Goat）的事儿听说了吗？他的婆娘们把他的马鞍撂到门外去了，据说是这样。”

===Original===
“What had he done?” somebody asked.

===Chinese===
“他干了什么啦？”有人问。

===Original===
“He drank whiskey; he spent their money on it, so they say.”

===Chinese===
“他喝了威士忌；钱都花在这上头了，她们说的。”

===Original===
“They were right, I think.”

===Chinese===
“她们做得对，我想。”

===Original===
“I have never tasted whiskey,” Laughing Boy said; “what is it like?”

===Chinese===
“威士忌我还从来没尝过，”笑娃说；“那是什么滋味？”

===Original===
“It tastes bad, but one feels good. Then later one has a headache.”

===Chinese===
“味道不好，可人觉得舒坦。过后就头疼。”

===Original===
“It sounds like *t’oghlepai*.”

===Chinese===
“听着倒像 t’oghlepai。”

===Original===
“It is stronger. I’ll give you eight dollars for that bow-guard.”

===Chinese===
“它劲儿更冲。这弓护腕我出八块。”

===Original===
“I don’t want to sell it; it is lucky.”

===Chinese===
“我不卖；它是件吉利物。”

===Original===
“That turquoise is no good, and the work is not very good.”

===Chinese===
“那绿松石成色不行，做工也平平。”

===Original===
Laughing Boy looked bored. “Give me a smoke, Grandfather.”

===Chinese===
笑娃一脸的索然。“给我支烟抽，老爷爷。”

===Original===
“The turquoise is too green. Eight dollars is a lot.”

===Chinese===
“这绿松石绿得太狠。八块钱可不少了。”

===Original===
“Eight dollars is nothing,” he answered loftily, with a pleasant remembrance of his winnings.

===Chinese===
“八块钱算得了什么。”他答得高傲，想起赢来的钱，心里正受用。

===Original===
“Here, I have nine-fifty. That is all I have.” The man held out the money.

===Chinese===
“这样吧，我这儿有九块五。我有的全在这上了。”那人把钱递了过来。

===Original===
“No, I really do not want to sell. I would not sell it for a horse.”

===Chinese===
“不，我真不想卖。拿匹马来换我都不换。”

===Original===
“It is a fine bow-guard. If you make many things like that, you will get rich.”

===Chinese===
“这是只好弓护腕。你要是多做些这样的物件，会发家哩。”

===Original===
Everything was well, Laughing Boy thought. He had money now, and a belt that was ugly, but could be sold to a trader for fifty dollars. People praised his work. That girl was only an incident; one should not let oneself be ruffled so easily.

===Chinese===
一切都顺遂，笑娃想。他如今有了钱，还有一条丑腰带，不过那能卖给商人，换五十块钱。人们夸他的手艺。那个姑娘不过是个小小的插曲；人不该这么轻易就乱了方寸。

===Original===
It was good to lie in the sand talking a little, borrowing smokes now and then. Now that he had money, he would buy tobacco when he came to a trading post. Meantime he thought he would hunt up those two Americans to see if they would give him one of their big, white cigarettes. Perhaps they would buy his belt; they were travelling just for fun, people said; they must be rich. Perhaps, too, they would have sweet food, canned goods, and coffee with much sugar in it. He called his friend.

===Chinese===
躺在沙地里，有一句没一句地说话，时不时跟人讨支烟抽，这样真好。如今有了钱，等走到交易站，他就买烟丝。眼下呢，他想去找那两个美国人，看他们肯不肯给他一支那种又大又白的纸烟。没准儿他们还会买他的腰带；人们说，他们纯粹是出来游逛的，必定有钱。说不定他们那儿还有甜口的食物，有罐头，有搁足了糖的咖啡。他把朋友唤了来。

===Original===
“Let us see if those Americans will buy my belt. Let us see what they will give us.”

===Chinese===
“走，瞧瞧那两个美国人肯不肯买我的腰带。瞧瞧他们会给咱们些什么。”

===Original===
“Good.”

===Chinese===
“好。”

===Original===
They rode off sitting sideways on Jesting Squaw’s Son’s unsaddled horse, heels drumming softly on opposite sides, humming a song together.

===Chinese===
两人侧身同骑在打趣婆娘之子那匹没备鞍的马上，脚跟在马身两侧轻轻地叩，嘴里合哼着一支歌，走远了。

### III / III

===Original===
The Americans, a rich Eastern tourist and his guide, were tired of feeding stray Indians, of whom there had been a plague all day. They set out to ignore these two who descended gravely upon them, but the double line of silver plaques about Laughing Boy’s waist caught the tourist’s eye.

===Chinese===
那两个美国人——一个是阔气的东部游客，一个是他的向导——早就喂烦了那些野狗般游荡讨食的印第安人，一整天，这号人闹瘟疫似的没断过。他们本打定主意不去理睬这两个郑重其事朝他们围上来的人，可笑娃腰间那两排银饰牌晃进了游客的眼里。

===Original===
“Ask him to let me see those belts,” he told the guide, and then, in babytalk American’s Navajo, “Your belt﻿—two﻿—good.”

===Chinese===
“叫他让我瞧瞧那些腰带。”他吩咐向导，接着又用美国佬娃娃腔的纳瓦霍话补了一句：“你的腰带——二——好。”

===Original===
Laughing Boy sat down beside him. “*Nashto, shadani*﻿—give me a smoke, brother-in-law.”

===Chinese===
笑娃在他身旁坐下。“Nashto, shadani——给我支烟抽，姐夫。”

===Original===
It is rude to call a man brother-in-law, and like most Navajos, he enjoyed using the term, and teaching it, to innocent foreigners. Americans were good fun. This one gave him a black cigar, cutting the end for him and holding out a match. It nearly killed him at the first whiff, only medicine-hogan experience in swallowing smoke enabled him to keep a calm face.

===Chinese===
管一个男人叫姐夫是失礼的，而同大多数纳瓦霍人一样，他偏偏爱拿这个称呼去逗无辜的外乡人，还爱把这词教给他们。美国人可是顶好的乐子。这一位给了他一支黑雪茄，替他剪好头，又递上一根火柴。头一口差点儿要了他的命，全亏在医药霍根屋里练下的吞烟功夫，才教他保住一张不动声色的脸。

===Original===
“This is good!” He passed it over to his friend, who habitually inhaled deeply. “It is like the magic tobacco Natinesthani gave the magician. We have nothing like this. Try it, elder brother.”

===Chinese===
“这东西来劲！”他把雪茄递给朋友——此人向来惯于把烟一口吞到肺底。“这就像纳蒂内斯塔尼（Natinesthani）赐给那巫师的神烟。我们这儿可没这样的东西。尝尝，大哥。”

===Original===
He tried it, cautiously at first, the tiniest puff, then a good lung-full that clutched his agonized insides like talons. Desperately he fought back tears and a choking cough, while Laughing Boy struggled with almost equal difficulty to keep a straight face. By a heroic effort he let the smoke out slowly. Then, with a sigh that disguised relief as critical enjoyment,

===Chinese===
他试了一口，起初小心在意，只抿了顶小的一丝，随后是结结实实的一肺，那口烟像利爪般攥紧他叫苦不迭的五脏。他拼命把眼泪和呛咳压回去，笑娃那边也费着几乎同样的劲，才把脸绷住不笑。他凭一股英雄气概，把烟慢慢吐了出来。随后长叹一声，把如释重负装成行家品鉴的受用——

===Original===
“Yes, little brother, that is very good tobacco.”

===Chinese===
“是啊，兄弟，这烟可真是好烟。”

===Original===
The tourist was fingering Laughing Boy’s belts, pulling them around. The Indian thought of pulling in turn at his necktie, but decided it would be poor business.

===Chinese===
那游客用手指拨弄着笑娃的腰带，把它拽来拽去。这印第安人盘算着也去拽拽他的领带，转念一想，这么办不划算。

===Original===
“Ask him how much he wants for the one with the turquoise in it.”

===Chinese===
“问他那条镶绿松石的要多少钱。”

===Original===
“How much do you want for the one with the blues, Grandfather?” the guide asked.

===Chinese===
“镶蓝石头的那条你要多少，老爷爷？”向导问。

===Original===
“A horse, perhaps.” He puffed gingerly at the cigar which Jesting Squaw’s Son passed back to him.

===Chinese===
“一匹马吧，兴许。”他小心翼翼地嘬着打趣婆娘之子递还回来的雪茄。

===Original===
“I’ll offer you a nickel, perhaps.”

===Chinese===
“我兴许出你一个五分镍子。”

===Original===
Both laughed.

===Chinese===
两人都笑了。

===Original===
“You say, how much.”

===Chinese===
“你说，多少。”

===Original===
The formal gambits were over. The guide cocked his head, pursed his lips, and looked critical and rather disgusted. “I’ll give you twenty-five dollars.”

===Chinese===
开场的客套走完了。向导歪着头，抿着嘴，一脸挑剔，还带着几分嫌弃。“我给你二十五块。”

===Original===
“No, no.”

===Chinese===
“不，不。”

===Original===
“How much, then?”

===Chinese===
“那你要多少？”

===Original===
He took it off. “This is a good belt. These stones are good. The silver is heavy; Mexican silver. That is good work. Seventy-five dollars.”

===Chinese===
他把腰带解了下来。“这是条好腰带。石头是好石头。银子压手；墨西哥银。做工也地道。七十五块。”

===Original===
The guide grunted, and threw a pinch of sand on it in token of its worthlessness.

===Chinese===
向导哼了一声，捻起一撮沙子撒在腰带上，以示它一钱不值。

===Original===
“What does he say he wants?”

===Chinese===
“他说他要多少？”

===Original===
“He says seventy-five.”

===Chinese===
“他说七十五。”

===Original===
“What’s it worth?”

===Chinese===
“它值多少？”

===Original===
“Up to about sixty, I guess. Them’s good stones.”

===Chinese===
“顶多六十上下，我估摸。不过石头是真好。”

===Original===
“Get it for less if you can.”

===Chinese===
“能压就压，尽量拿下。”

===Original===
Laughing Boy passed the cigar back. His friend, who knew a little English, whispered, “He says sixty, I think, that he will pay.” He blew out on the cigar to use up as much as was possible.

===Chinese===
笑娃把雪茄递了回去。他那位懂点儿英语的朋友低声说：“他说六十，我想，说他出六十。”他朝雪茄吹着气，好教它尽量多烧掉些。

===Original===
Laughing Boy asked the guide, “Where do you come from?”

===Chinese===
笑娃问向导：“你们打哪儿来？”

===Original===
“From Besh Senil. We are going to the Moqui.”

===Chinese===
“打贝什塞尼尔（Besh Senil）来。我们要上莫基人（Moqui）那儿去。”

===Original===
“*Ei-yei!* That’s far! Why do you want to see the Moqui?”

===Chinese===
“咿-耶！那可远！你们去看莫基人做什么？”

===Original===
“We want to see them dance with snakes.”

===Chinese===
“我们要看他们跟蛇跳舞。”

===Original===
“They are crazy to do that. Our dances are better.”

===Chinese===
“他们那么干是疯了。我们的舞才好。”

===Original===
“Perhaps. Well, this man says your belt is pretty good, and he will give you forty for it. No more.”

===Chinese===
“也许吧。是这样，这位先生说你的腰带还算不赖，他出四十块。不能再多。”

===Original===
“No, seventy, no less.” He buckled it on again.

===Chinese===
“不，七十，一分不让。”他又把腰带扣回腰上。

===Original===
“Perhaps we can give you forty-five, but that is all.”

===Chinese===
“兴许我们能给到四十五，不过也就到头了。”

===Original===
Laughing Boy took the cigar again. It was a long time burning down. He wondered if he would die and be brought to life again, like the magician who smoked with Natinesthani.

===Chinese===
笑娃又接过雪茄。它要烧上好一阵才见底。他不知道自己会不会死过去再还阳，就像那个同纳蒂内斯塔尼一起抽烟的巫师。

===Original===
“What does the Indian want?” the tourist asked.

===Chinese===
“这印第安人要多少？”游客问。

===Original===
“He still says seventy; it’s too much.”

===Chinese===
“他还咬定七十；太贵了。”

===Original===
“Get it if you can.”

===Chinese===
“能拿下就拿下。”

===Original===
Laughing Boy whispered, “What are they saying, Grandfather?”

===Chinese===
笑娃悄声问：“他们在说什么，老爷爷？”

===Original===
“I’m not sure. That one who speaks Navajo says ‘too much,’ I think. The pink one says ‘get it.’ ”

===Chinese===
“拿不准。那个会说纳瓦霍话的说‘太贵了’，我想。那个粉红脸膛的说‘买下来’。”

===Original===
The guide spoke to them. “This man says he will give you fifty because he likes your belt. He cannot give any more.”

===Chinese===
向导对他们说：“这位先生说，他中意你的腰带，给五十。再多就给不出了。”

===Original===
“No, I do not want to sell. He does not want to pay what it is worth, he is just talking about wanting it.” The cigar was done at last. He rose.

===Chinese===
“不，我不卖了。他不想照它的身价掏钱，不过是把想要挂在嘴上罢了。”雪茄总算烧到了头。他站起身。

===Original===
“Oh, give him what he wants!”

===Chinese===
“唉，他要多少就给他多少吧！”

===Original===
“How much, Grandfather? You say.”

===Chinese===
“多少，老爷爷？你说。”

===Original===
“Sixty-five, perhaps.”

===Chinese===
“六十五，兴许。”

===Original===
“He says sixty-five. Looks like he won’t come down no lower.”

===Chinese===
“他说六十五。看样子他是不肯再让了。”

===Original===
“I’ll take it.”

===Chinese===
“我买了。”

===Original===
“He says he’ll take it.”

===Chinese===
“他说他买了。”

===Original===
Laughing Boy handed over the belt. “Grandfather, do you know this paper money?”

===Chinese===
笑娃把腰带递了过去。“老爷爷，这种纸票子你识得不识得？”

===Original===
Jesting Squaw’s Son considered the bills. “Yes, these with tracks here in the corners are fives. These with little sticks and the man with long hair and the ugly mouth on them are ones. This with the yellow back, I do not know it. I think it is no good.” He had been stung once on cigar coupons.

===Chinese===
打趣婆娘之子把钞票端详了一番。“识得，这四角上带印道道的是五块的。这些印着小棍棍、还有个长头发歪嘴巴的人的，是一块的。这张黄背的，我不识得，我看它靠不住。”他在雪茄奖券上头吃过一回亏。

===Original===
At last the sum was made up, with ones, fives, and the silver dollars which they preferred.

===Chinese===
末了钱凑齐了，有一块的、五块的，还有他们更中意的银元。

===Original===
“Ask that man,” Laughing Boy told the guide, “to give us another of those big, black cigarettes. They are good.”

===Chinese===
“劳驾问问那位先生，”笑娃对向导说，“再给我们一支那种又大又黑的烟卷。那玩意儿好。”

===Original===
The guide translated.

===Chinese===
向导翻了话。

===Original===
“My God! I thought it would make them sick. Here’s one for each of them.”

===Chinese===
“老天爷！我还当他们要难受坏呢。喏，一人一支。”

===Original===
“Good. Now, Grandfather, give me some cigarette papers.”

===Chinese===
“好。现在，老爷爷，给我些卷烟纸。”

===Original===
The guide forked up. As they shook hands all around, elaborately, Navajo fashion, the Americans’ faces and voices seemed to grow very distant and uncertain. Riding away, Laughing Boy sighed deeply.

===Chinese===
向导掏了出来。众人照纳瓦霍的礼数，郑重其事地一一握过手，这时那两个美国人的脸和声音，仿佛都变得很远、很飘忽。骑马走开去时，笑娃深深叹了一口气。

===Original===
“Let us go to a quiet place. I want to be sick.”

===Chinese===
“咱们寻个清静地方去。我要吐了。”

===Original===
“I too.”

===Chinese===
“我也是。”

===Original===
Later, at sunset, they went to wash at the pool, dipping up liquid silver and lilac in their hands. They lay back against the rock watching the sun go down, the shadows and lights on the water, the distant fires and people moving. They had slept, they felt very empty, clean, and peaceful.

===Chinese===
后来，日落时分，他们去水潭里洗身，双手掬起一捧捧流银和丁香色。他们背靠着岩石仰卧，看太阳落下去，看水面上明明暗暗的光影，看远处的火堆和走动的人影。他们睡过了一觉，只觉得空落落的，干净，安宁。

===Original===
“Shall we try making a cigarette with that tobacco?”

===Chinese===
“咱们拿那烟丝卷支烟试试？”

===Original===
“Not yet, I think. Go tend your horse. It is time to eat again.”

===Chinese===
“先不忙，我想。去照看你的马吧。又到吃饭的时辰了。”

===Original===
“I go. I hope there will be much gambling after this.”

===Chinese===
“我去了。但愿这顿饭之后能有大把的赌局。”

## Chapter III / 第三章

### I / I

===Original===
The dance of the second night was much like that of the first, although perhaps a little less exuberant. He entered once more into the river of song, and was happy, yelling his head off, save that he kept on being conscious of that girl. While she was dancing, he would forget about her, but when he saw her looking for another partner, he would be uneasy until she had made her choice. He noticed that she did not dance with Red Man. Halfway between midnight and dawn, the women having departed, he fell out, to sleep by a fire.

===Chinese===
第二夜的舞同第一夜大同小异，只是兴头兴许淡了一点。他再度投身进歌的河流里，快活得很，扯着嗓子吼，只有一桩：他总惦记着那个姑娘。她跳着舞的时候，他会把她忘了；可一瞧见她在物色下一个舞伴，他便心神不定，直到她拣定了人才安生。他留意到她没有同红汉子（Red Man）跳。午夜过后、天亮之前，妇女们散了场，他也退了出来，在一堆火旁边睡了。

===Original===
They rode down to Ane’é Tseyi that day, where the dance of the final night would be held. He rode behind Jesting Squaw’s Son’s saddle, leading the mare. He hoped they would find a place with some grass for the animal, and reflected that in any case, now, he could afford to buy corn. The long, hot ride, hot sun, hot wind, unrelieved, weighed on them somewhat, combining with lack of sleep to make limbs sluggish and eyes heavy. It was a relief to ride into the narrow canyon of their destination, to rest in a strip of afternoon shade. Laughing Boy took the horses down to the windmill for water, and staked them out in a corner where uncropped spears of grass stood singly, each inches from the next, in brown sand. A beaten track toward an oak tree and a break in the rock caught his eye. A spring, perhaps.

===Chinese===
那天他们骑马下行，去阿内埃特塞伊（Ane’é Tseyi），末一夜的舞要在那里举行。他坐在打趣婆娘之子（Jesting Squaw’s Son）的鞍后，牵着那匹母马。他指望能给牲口寻着一块有草的地方，转念又想，如今无论如何，买玉米的钱总有了。长长的热路，毒太阳，热风，一刻不得缓解，把他们压得有些发沉，加上缺觉，胳膊腿都滞重了，眼皮也沉了。骑进目的地那条窄峡谷，在午后一线阴凉里歇下来，才算松快了。笑娃（Laughing Boy）把马带到风车跟前饮水，又把它们拴在场角一处棕沙地里；那儿立着几簇没被啃过的草叶，一簇与一簇隔着几寸远。一条踩出来的小道通向一棵橡树和岩壁上的一道裂口，惹眼。兴许是一眼泉。

===Original===
He followed it. Behind the oak, currant bushes grew in a niche of red rock like the fold of a giant curtain. At the back was a full-grown, lofty fir. A spring, surely. Behind the fir a cleft opened at shoulder height into transparent shadow. The footholds were worn to velvety roundness in the sandstone; at one side a pecked design showed that long before the Navajos had swooped upon the land, a people of an elder earth had known this entrance. Laughing Boy climbed lightly in.

===Chinese===
他循着小道走了过去。橡树后面，红岩的壁龛里长着一丛丛茶藨子，那壁龛像巨幅帷幕的一道褶。龛底立着一棵成材的冷杉，高挑挺拔。准是泉了。冷杉背后，齐肩高的地方裂开一道口子，通进一片透明的幽暗。砂岩上的踏脚窝磨得天鹅绒般溜圆；一旁有个凿刻出来的图样，表明早在纳瓦霍人扑到这片土地上来之前，一个更古老大地上的民族就已经认得这个入口。笑娃轻捷地钻了进去。

===Original===
It was a stone-lined pocket, scarce twenty feet across, narrower at the top. One went forward along a ledge at one side, shouldering against young aspens, then slid down a rock face into a curving bowl, with a seep at one lip from which silent water oozed over moss and cress into the bottom. Spears of grass grew in cracks. By the tiny pool of water in the bowl was a square of soft turf with imprints of moccasins. He squatted there, leaning back against the rock. Here was all shade and peace, soft, grey stone, dark, shadowed green, coolness, and the sweet smell of dampness. He dabbled his hands, wet his face, drank a little. He rolled a cigarette with crumpled cigar tobacco. This was good, this was beautiful.

===Chinese===
那是一处石壁围成的洞窝，宽不过二十英尺，顶上收得更窄。人沿着一侧的石台往前走，肩膀擦过幼小的白杨，再滑下一面石壁，进到一个弯曲的碗状石盆；盆沿一边有个渗水口，无声的水顺着苔藓和水田芥渗到盆底。石缝里钻出一支支草叶。盆里那一小汪水旁边，有一方软草皮，上面印着鹿皮鞋的脚印。他蹲在那儿，背往后靠着岩石。这里全是阴凉和安宁，软和的灰石头，幽暗的、荫翳的绿，凉意，还有潮气的甜香。他把手在水里搅了搅，湿了湿脸，喝了一小口。他用揉皱的雪茄烟丝卷了支烟。这儿真好，这儿真美。

===Original===
Away above, the intolerant sky gleamed, and a corner of cloud was white fire. His eyes shifting lightly, the edge of the rocks above took on a glowing halo. He amused himself trying to fit it back again, to get the spot the cloud made back against the cloud, playing tricks with his half-closed eyelashes that made things seem vague.

===Chinese===
高高的上方，那不容冒犯的天空灼灼闪光，一角云是白色的火。他的目光轻轻一斜，头顶岩石的边缘便镶上一圈光晕。他自得其乐地想把它安回去，想让云彩打出的那块亮斑重新对回云彩上，半阖着睫毛变把戏，教万物都显得朦朦胧胧。

===Original===
“*Ahalani!*” The two-toned greeting came from a voice like water.

===Chinese===
“阿哈拉尼！”一声两个调门的问候，出自一条流水似的嗓子。

===Original===
He returned to himself with a start. Slim Girl stood poised on the edge of the bowl, above his shoulder, water-basket in hand.

===Chinese===
他猛然回过神来。瘦姑娘（Slim Girl）亭亭立在石盆边沿上，就在他肩膀上方，手里拎着水篮。

===Original===
“*Ahalani, shicho.*” Dignified, casual.

===Chinese===
“阿哈拉尼，希乔。”端庄，随意。

===Original===
“Move over, wrestler, I want to come down.”

===Chinese===
“挪一挪，摔跤的，我要下来。”

===Original===
He observed her small feet in their red, silver-buttoned buckskin, sure and light on the rocks as a goat’s. She seemed to be hours descending. She was businesslike about filling the basket, but she turned utilitarian motions into part of a dance. Now she knelt, not two feet from him, taking him in with the long, mischievous eyes that talked and laughed.

===Chinese===
他端详她那双小脚：红鹿皮的面子，缀着银扣，踏在岩石上像山羊蹄子一般又稳又轻。她这一路下来，仿佛下了几个钟头。灌水篮时她一副办公事的做派，可她把实用的动作也化成了舞的一部分。这会儿她跪了下来，离他不到两英尺，用那双又长又淘气、会说话也会笑的眼睛，把他端详。

===Original===
She is a butterfly, he thought, or a hummingbird. Why does she not go away? I will not go﻿—run away from her. He thought, as he tried to read her face, that her slimness was deceptive; strength came forth from her.

===Chinese===
她是一只蝴蝶，他想，再不然就是一只蜂鸟。她怎么还不走？我不会走——不会从她身边逃开。他一面想读她的脸，一面觉出，她这瘦削是骗人的；力气正从她身上透出来。

===Original===
“Now, for ten cents, I go.”

===Chinese===
“喂，一毛钱，我就走。”

===Original===
He blinked. “I save that to get rid of you tonight, perhaps.”

===Chinese===
他眨了眨眼。“那一毛钱我留着，兴许今晚好打发你走。”

===Original===
“I do not dance tonight. There is trouble, a bad thing. I come from far away.”

===Chinese===
“我今晚不跳了。出了事，一桩坏事。我从很远的地方来。”

===Original===
He thought he had better not ask questions. “Tomorrow there will be horse-racing, a chicken-pull, perhaps.”

===Chinese===
他想还是不问的好。“明天有赛马，兴许还有一场抢鸡赛。”

===Original===
“And you have a fine horse to race, black, with a white star and a white sock.” He grunted astonishment. She smiled. “You are a good jeweler, they say. You made that bow-guard. You sold Red Man’s belt to the American, they say, for sixty-five dollars.”

===Chinese===
“再说你还有匹参赛的好马，黑的，额上一颗白星，一条腿上一只白袜。”他惊得哼了一声。她笑了。“你是个好银匠，她们说。那只弓护腕是你打的。红汉子的腰带，你卖给了那个美国人，她们说，卖了六十五块。”

===Original===
“You are like an old wife, trying to find out about everything a man is doing.”

===Chinese===
“你真像个老婆子，男人干什么都要打听个遍。”

===Original===
“No, I am not like an old wife.”

===Chinese===
“不，我可不像个老婆子。”

===Original===
They looked at each other for a long time. No, she was not like an old wife. Blood pounded in his ears and his mouth was dry. He pulled at the end of his dead cigarette. At length,

===Chinese===
两人对望了许久。不，她是不像个老婆子。血在他耳朵里轰轰地撞，嘴里发干。他捻着那截熄了的烟头。过了半晌，

===Original===
“You should stay for the racing. There will be fine horses, a beautiful sight.”

===Chinese===
“你该留下看赛马。会有好马，好看的景致。”

===Original===
“I shall stay, perhaps.”

===Chinese===
“我兴许留下。”

===Original===
Her rising, her ascent of the rock, were all one quick motion. She never looked back. He stayed, not exactly in thought, but experiencing a condition of mind and feeling. Loud laughter of women roused him, to pass them with averted eyes and go forth dazed into the sunlight.

===Chinese===
她起身、攀上岩壁，一气呵成，全是轻快的动作。她始终没有回头。他留在原地，说不上是在想什么，只是在经历一种心境、一种感受。妇人们的哄笑把他惊醒，他垂着眼从她们身边走过，恍恍惚惚走进日光里去。

### II / II

===Original===
The last night of the dance was a failure for Laughing Boy, for all its ritual. He tried to join the singing, but they were not the kind of songs he wanted; he tried to concentrate on the prayer that was being brought to a climax, but he wanted to pray by himself. He quit the dance, suddenly very much alone as he left the noise and the light behind him, strongly conscious of himself, complete to himself. He followed a sheep trail up a break in one canyon wall, to the rim, then crossed the narrow mesa to where he could look down over the broad Ties Hatsosi Valley, a great pool of night, and far-distant, terraced horizon of mesas against the bright stars, cool, alone, with the sound of the drumming and music behind him, faint as memory. This also was a form of living.

===Chinese===
对笑娃来说，舞的末一夜是败兴的，尽管仪轨一应俱全。他试着加入唱歌，可那些歌不是他要唱的那种；他试着凝神去随那正被推向高潮的祷文，可他想一个人独自祷告。他退出了舞场，把喧闹和灯火抛在身后，霎时形单影只，强烈地意识到自己，自成一个完足的世界。他顺着一条羊道，从峡谷一面岩壁的裂口登上崖顶，再穿过窄窄的平顶山，走到能俯瞰开阔的蒂斯哈托西（Ties Hatsosi）谷地的地方——那是一大泓夜色，极远处，方山台地层层叠叠的地平线衬着亮星。清凉，孤独，鼓点和乐声留在身后，隐约如记忆。这也是一种活法。

===Original===
He began to make up a new song, but lost interest in it, feeling too centred upon himself. He sat noticing little things, whisper of grass, turn of a leaf﻿—little enough there is in the desert at night.

===Chinese===
他动手编一支新歌，却又失了兴致，只觉心思太系在自己身上。他坐着留意细小的东西，草的低语，一片叶子的翻转——沙漠的夜里，这样的细小本就不多。

===Original===
“Yota zhil-de tlin-sha-igahl﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
“Yota zhil-de tlin-sha-igahl……”

===Original===
His song came upon him.

===Chinese===
他的歌找上了他。

===Original===
“*A-a-a-ainé, ainé*,
						

						I ride my horse down from the high hills
						

						To the valley, *a-a-a*.
						

						Now the hills are flat. Now my horse will not go
						

						From your valley, *a-a-a*.
						

						*Hainéya, ainé, o-o-o-o*.”

===Chinese===
“啊-啊-啊-伊内，伊内，

						我骑着我的马，从高高的山岗上下来，

						下到山谷里，啊-啊-啊。

						如今山岗都平了。如今我的马不肯走，

						不肯离开你的山谷，啊-啊-啊。

						哈伊内亚，伊内，哦-哦-哦-哦。”

===Original===
Slim Girl sat down beside him. His song trailed off, embarrassed. They rested thus, without words, looking away into the night while contemplation flowed between them like a current. At length she raised one hand, so that the bracelets clinked.

===Chinese===
瘦姑娘在他身旁坐了下来。他的歌讪讪地低了下去。两人就这样待着，不说一句话，目光望进夜色深处，心思却像一股流水，在两人之间来回地淌。末了她抬起一只手，好让手镯叮地一响。

===Original===
“Sing that song.”

===Chinese===
“把那支歌唱唱。”

===Original===
He sang without effort. This was no common woman, who ignored all convention. The long-drawn “*Hainéya, ainé, o-o-o-o*,” fell away into the lake of darkness; silence shut in on them again.

===Chinese===
他不费力气地唱了。这可不是寻常女人，全然不理会什么规矩。那声拖长的“哈伊内亚，伊内，哦-哦-哦-哦”坠进黑暗的湖里去；寂静重新向他们合拢过来。

===Original===
On the heels of his song he said, “My eldest uncle is here. I am going to speak to him tomorrow.”

===Chinese===
歌声才落，他便说：“我的大舅舅在这儿。明天我要去跟他谈。”

===Original===
“I should not do that if I were you.”

===Chinese===
“我要是你，就不去谈。”

===Original===
He rolled a cigarette with careful movements, but forbore to light it. Again they sat watching the motionless stars above the shrouded earth. No least breeze stirred; there were no details to be seen in the cliffs or the valley, only the distant silhouettes against the sky. A second time her hand rose and her bracelets clinked, as though speech unannounced would startle the universe.

===Chinese===
他动作仔细地卷了一支烟，却忍着没有点。两人又坐着，望那裹在夜幕里的大地上空纹丝不动的星星。一丝最小的风也没有；峭壁上、谷地里看不出一点细部，只有衬着天光的远影。她的手第二次抬起来，手镯又叮地一响，仿佛不打声招呼就开口，会惊动整个宇宙似的。

===Original===
“You are sure you are going to speak to your uncle, then?”

===Chinese===
“这么说，你是拿定主意要去跟你舅舅说了？”

===Original===
“Yes.” The second self that is a detached mentor in one’s mind recognized that he would never have talked this way with any other woman. Etiquette had been left behind down in the narrowness of Ane’é Tseyi.

===Chinese===
“是。”心里那个置身事外的良师般的第二个自我明白，换了任何别的女人，他绝不会这样讲话。礼数都被留在下头阿内埃特塞伊那条窄谷里了。

===Original===
“Perhaps you will listen to what he says, I think; perhaps you will not. Perhaps your mind is made up now.”

===Chinese===
“兴许你会听他怎么说，我想；兴许你不听。兴许你这会儿主意已经定了。”

===Original===
“I am thinking about what I intend to do. I shall not change.”

===Chinese===
“我在想我打定主意要做的事。我不会变。”

===Original===
“We shall see then. Goodbye.”

===Chinese===
“那就走着瞧吧。再见。”

===Original===
She rose like smoke. He called a startled “Goodbye,” then began to follow at a distance. He stopped at the rim of the canyon, where the noise of singing that welled up from below passed him by as he stood watching her dark form, down to the bottom, along by the grove where his camp was, and beyond into the shadows.

===Chinese===
她像一缕烟似的起了身。他慌忙应了声“再见”，随后远远地缀着她走。他在峡谷边上停住，脚下涌上来的歌声从他身边流过去；他站在那儿，望着她那一小团黑影下到谷底，沿着他那营地的树丛边走过去，再走进更远处的暗影里。

===Original===
He went back to the far edge of the mesa. He did not want to sleep, not ever again.

===Chinese===
他回到平顶山远端的边上。他不想睡，永远都不想再睡了。

===Original===
“Now with a god I walk,
						

						Now I step across the summits of the mountains,
						

						Now with a god I walk,
						

						Striding across the foothills.
						

						Now on the old age trail, now on the path of beauty wandering.
						

						In beauty﻿—*Hozoji, hozoji, hozoji, hozoji-i*.”

===Chinese===
“如今我与一位神明同行，

						如今我跨过群山的峰顶，

						如今我与一位神明同行，

						大步跨过山麓的丘岗。

						如今在暮年的古道上，如今在美之径上徘徊。

						在美之中——霍卓吉，霍卓吉，霍卓吉，霍卓吉伊。”

===Original===
The deep resonance of the prayer carried his exaltation through the land. Then he began to analyse her words, finding in them nothing save unconventionality, no promise, and his own he found laggard and dull. Was she playing with him, or did she mean all he read into her brevity? Was she thus with other men?

===Chinese===
祷文深沉的共鸣载着他的激昂，传遍大地。随后他开始剖解她的话，从里头只找得出不循常规，找不出半点许诺；再掂掂自己的话，又迟钝又呆板。她是在耍弄他呢，还是他往她那三言两语里读出的一切，她都当真？她对别的男人也这样吗？

===Original===
“I ride my horse down from the high hills
						

						To the valley, *a-a-a﻿ ﻿…*”

===Chinese===
“我骑着我的马，从高高的山岗上下来

						下到山谷里，啊-啊-啊……”

===Original===
He was up and down, restless, no longer on the path of beauty, yet tormented by a new beauty. Far away, high-pitched, he heard the faint “*Yo-o galeana, yo-o galeana*,” and the thudding drum. He walked to and fro. My mind is made up, I shall make things as they should be. Now with a god I walk﻿—or is it a game, looseness?

===Chinese===
他起来又坐下，坐立不安，人已不在美之径上，却被一种新的美折磨着。远远地，尖细高亢，他听见那隐约的“哟-哦 加莱阿纳，哟-哦 加莱阿纳”，还有闷闷的鼓声。他走来走去。主意我拿定了，我要把事情弄成它们该有的样子。如今我与一位神明同行——还是说，这是一场游戏，一场放荡？

===Original===
Suddenly he fled to sleep for refuge, rolling in his blanket by a high place under thickly clustered, brilliant, unhelpful stars, falling asleep with the feeling of vastness about him and clean, gracious silence.

===Chinese===
忽然，他一头逃进睡眠里避难，在一处高地上裹上毯子滚了滚躺下，头顶密密簇簇、亮灿灿却帮不上一点忙的星星，带着周身四野的浩茫之感，带着干净而慈厚的寂静，睡着了。

### III / III

===Original===
He woke to a feeling of expectation, and made his Dawn Prayer with all the gladness that his religion prescribed. He could not wait to see his uncle and have the matter settled before they went to the trading post for the races. At the same time, his own certainty told him that his eldest uncle, his mother, and all her kin were only wanted to ratify a decision already made. What was, was; he would announce what he wanted to do, not ask for permission.

===Chinese===
他醒来时怀着一种期盼，照他的宗教所定的那满腔欢喜，做了黎明祈祷。他等不及要见舅舅，要赶在众人去交易站看赛马之前把事情了结。与此同时，他自己的笃定告诉他：大舅舅也好，他母亲也好，母亲全族亲人也好，要来不过是为一件已成之局画个押。事已如此，便是如此；他要宣布自己想做什么，不是去讨许可。

===Original===
Now he stood on the rim above the canyon, bathed in sunlight, while below him in thick, visible shadow unimportant people moved, horses stamped, smoke rose from tiny fires.

===Chinese===
这会儿他站在峡谷上方的崖沿上，浴在日光里；脚下厚厚的、看得真切的阴影里，无关紧要的人们在走动，马在刨蹄，小小的火堆升起烟来。

===Original===
His uncle was staying down by the trading post with Killed a Navajo. He started off without breakfast, leading the pony, and sorely tempted to mount and gallop those few miles, but the thought of the race and the pleasure of winning restrained him. I’ll win for Slim Girl, he thought with a smile, and burst into song, lustily pouring forth keen delight from tough lungs over the empty flat. The dusty walk and hot sun, the heat that lay over the baked adobe and dull sagebrush, troubled him not at all. The bleak, grey parts of the desert have a quintessential quality of privacy, and yet one has space there to air one’s mood. So Laughing Boy sang loudly, his horse nosed his back, a distant turtledove mocked him, and a high-sailing, pendent buzzard gave him up as far too much alive.

===Chinese===
他舅舅在交易站下边、杀纳瓦霍人（Killed a Navajo）家里落脚。他没吃早饭就动身了，牵着那匹小马，心里痒痒，恨不得骑上去把那几英里一气跑完，可想到赛马，想到赢马的快活，还是忍住了。我要替瘦姑娘赢马，他想着，笑了，跟着放声唱起来，用一副结实的肺，朝空荡荡的平地尽情倾洒锐利的欢畅。尘土扑面的步行，毒辣的日头，闷在烤干的泥坯和灰扑扑山艾上的暑气，一点儿也搅扰他不着。沙漠里那些荒凉的灰色角落，自有一种至纯的清静，而人在那儿又有的是天地，好晾晒自己的心绪。就这样，笑娃高声唱着，他的马用鼻子拱他的背，远处一只斑鸠嘲弄他，高天里盘旋悬停的一只秃鹫，看他活气太旺，断了指望，弃他而去。

===Original===
Killed a Navajo’s hogan was well built, of thick-laid evergreens over stout piñón poles. Looking in through the wide door one was conscious of cool darkness flecked with tiny spots of light, a central brilliance under the smoke-hole, vague outlines of reclining figures, their feet, stretched towards the centre, grotesquely clear. He stood in the doorway. Someone spoke to him, “Come in.” He shook hands all round. They offered him a little coffee, left over from breakfast, and tobacco. He made himself comfortable on the sheepskins beside his uncle in the place of honour.

===Chinese===
杀纳瓦霍人的霍根屋（hogan，纳瓦霍人泥木筑的小屋）造得结实，粗壮的皮尼翁松杆上密密地苫着常青树枝。从宽宽的门洞望进去，只觉一片清凉的暗，点缀着细小的光点，烟洞底下正中一团亮；倚躺着的人影轮廓模糊，只有一只只朝中央伸直的脚，清楚得近乎怪诞。他站在门口。有人招呼他：“进来。”他同满屋的人一一握了手。他们请他喝了点早饭剩下的咖啡，又请他抽烟。他在舅舅身边的贵客席上，就着羊皮舒舒服服地安顿下来。

===Original===
One by one the family went about their work; the children to tend the sheep, Killed a Navajo down to the store where he did odd jobs, and was needed today for distributing free food, his younger wife to preparing a meal for the many guests expected that day, his first wife to weaving, outside. Laughing Boy’s cigarette smoke went up in shadow, was caught in a pencil of sunlight, disappeared, and gleamed once again before it seeped through the roof. A suggestion of a breeze rustled the green walls. He studied his uncle’s face﻿—big and massive, with heavy, high-bridged nose and deep furrows enclosing the wide, sure mouth. Under the blue turban wisps of hair showed a little grey. Across his cheekbone ran the old scar from which he took his name, Wounded Face. It was an old eagle’s head. Laughing Boy was a little afraid of it.

===Chinese===
家里的人一个个自去忙活：孩子们放羊去了；杀纳瓦霍人下到铺子里去了，他在那儿做零工，今天正用得着他分发白给的食物；他的小婆娘在张罗吃食，那天预计要来许多客人；他的大婆娘在外头织布。笑娃的香烟在暗影里升起来，被一柱日光接住，没了踪影，又在渗出屋顶之前亮了最后一下。一丝若有若无的微风把绿墙吹得簌簌作响。他端详舅舅的脸——宽大厚重，鼻梁又高又沉，深深的沟纹围着一张宽阔笃定的嘴。蓝头巾底下，几绺头发见了点灰白。横过他颧骨的，是那道给他名字的旧疤——疤脸（Wounded Face）。那是一颗老雕的头。笑娃对它有几分怕。

===Original===
“My uncle.”

===Chinese===
“舅舅。”

===Original===
“Yes, my child.” The old-fashioned, round silver earrings shimmered faintly.

===Chinese===
“嗯，我的孩子。”老式的圆银耳环微微闪着光。

===Original===
“I have been thinking about something.”

===Chinese===
“我一直在想一件事。”

===Original===
They smoked on. A black-and-white kid slipped in the door, leaped up and poised itself on the cantle of a saddle. Outside was the rhythmic *thump-thump-thump* of a weaver pounding down the threads in her loom. A distant child laughed, someone was chopping wood﻿—sounds of domesticity.

===Chinese===
两人接着抽烟。一只黑白花的小山羊羔溜进门来，蹦起来，稳稳站上一只马鞍的后鞍桥。外头，织布的女人捶实织机上的纬线，一声一声，咚、咚、咚，有节奏。远处有孩子在笑，有人在劈柴——都是居家过日子的声响。

===Original===
“I have been thinking about a wife.”

===Chinese===
“我一直在想娶亲的事。”

===Original===
“You are old enough. It is a good thing.”

===Chinese===
“你年纪到了。这是好事。”

===Original===
He finished his cigarette.

===Chinese===
他抽完了那支烟。

===Original===
“You know that Slim Girl? The one who wears so much hard goods? She danced the first two nights.”

===Chinese===
“你认识那个瘦姑娘吧？就是戴了那么多硬货的那个？头两夜她都跳了舞。”

===Original===
“She is a schoolgirl.” The tone was final. “She was taken away to that place, for six years.”

===Chinese===
“她是个上学堂的。”语气是封了口的。“她当年被带走了，去了那地方，一去六年。”

===Original===
“That is all right. I like her.”

===Chinese===
“那没什么。我喜欢她。”

===Original===
“That is not all right. I do not know how she came to be allowed to dance. They made her stop. Water Singer let her dance, but we stopped him. She is bad. She lives down by the railroad. She is not of the People any more, she is American. She does bad things for the Americans.”

===Chinese===
“那怎么没什么。我不明白怎么会容她来跳舞。他们叫她停了。水颂唱师（Water Singer）放她跳，我们拦了他。她坏。她住在铁道边上。她已经不是族里的人了，她是美国人了。她替美国人干坏事。”

===Original===
“I do not know what you mean, but I know her, that girl. She is not bad. She is good. She is strong. She is for me.”

===Chinese===
“我不知道你说的是什么意思，可我认识她，那个姑娘。她不坏。她好。她刚强。她是配我的。”

===Original===
“You come from away up there; you do not know about these things. Nor do you know her. What is her clan?”

===Chinese===
“你从老远上边儿那地方来；这些事你不懂。你也同样不认识她。她是什么氏族的？”

===Original===
“I do not know.”

===Chinese===
“我不知道。”

===Original===
“Well? And what makes you think you can go out and pick a wife for yourself like this? The next thing I know, you will jump into the fire. I tell you, she is all bad; for two bits she will do the worst thing.”

===Chinese===
“嗯？还有，你怎么就当自己能这样出门自拣一房媳妇？照这么下去，我知道的下一件事，就是你往火里跳了。我告诉你，她坏透了；两毛五，她就肯干顶顶下作的事。”

===Original===
Laughing Boy sat up suddenly. “You should not have said that, you should not have thought it. Now you have said too much. I hope that bad thing follows you around always. Now you have said too much. Ugh! This place is too small for me!”

===Chinese===
笑娃猛地坐起身来。“这话你不该说，这念头你也不该起。这会儿你说得太多了。但愿那件坏事从此永远跟着你。这会儿你说得太多了。呸！这地方搁不下我了！”

===Original===
He ran outside. He needed space. People were beginning to arrive; there was laughing and shouting around the trading post. He went off rapidly to get by himself, too proud to run before people. His mind was boiling; he wanted to hit something, he was all confused. This way he went on until at last he reached a small butte that offered protection. He tore around the corner.

===Chinese===
他跑出门去。他需要空旷。人们开始到了；交易站周围一片笑语喧哗。他快步走开去寻个没人处，又太要脸面，不肯当着人跑。他的脑子在滚开；他想揍点什么，心里一团乱。就这么一路走下去，末了走到一座小方山跟前，那地方能遮身。他一阵风转过山嘴去。

===Original===
Slim Girl was walking towards him, cool and collected. Her brows rose in surprise as she stopped. He came up to her uncertainly.

===Chinese===
瘦姑娘正迎着他走来，冷静，镇定。她停住脚，眉毛惊讶地扬起来。他迟疑不定地走到她跟前。

===Original===
“Sit down; there is shade here.” They faced each other. “You have seen your uncle.”

===Chinese===
“坐下吧；这儿有阴凉。”两人相对而坐。“你见过你舅舅了。”

===Original===
His hand fell forward in the gesture of assent.

===Chinese===
他的手掌朝前一落，做了个应允的手势。

===Original===
“And he spoke to you.”

===Chinese===
“他也同你说了。”

===Original===
“He said bad things. I am angry with him.”

===Chinese===
“他说了坏话。我在生他的气。”

===Original===
“And towards me?”

===Chinese===
“对我呢？”

===Original===
“You came here on purpose to meet me.”

===Chinese===
“你是存心上这儿来会我的。”

===Original===
“Yes; I knew that when you had seen your uncle, you should see me soon.”

===Chinese===
“是；我知道你见过舅舅之后，很快就得见我。”

===Original===
“What my uncle said will stay with him. He has made a bad thing, it will follow him. The track of an evil thought is crooked and has no end; I do not want it around me; I do not keep it going. I have only good thoughts about you.”

===Chinese===
“我舅舅说的话，留在他自己身上。他造下了坏事，它自会跟着他。恶念的踪迹是弯的，没有尽头；我不要它在我身边打转；我也不去续它。对你，我只有好的念头。”

===Original===
“Your mother will never send someone to ask for me. You must just come with me.”

===Chinese===
“你母亲永远不会打发人来求我。你只有跟我走这一条路。”

===Original===
“Wait; what is your clan?”

===Chinese===
“慢着；你是什么氏族的？”

===Original===
“I am a Bitahni; and you?”

===Chinese===
“我是比塔尼（Bitahni）氏；你呢？”

===Original===
“Tahtchini; so that is all right. But I have nothing to give your mother, only one horse.”

===Chinese===
“塔奇尼（Tahtchini）氏；那这就成了。只是我拿不出东西给你母亲，只有一匹马。”

===Original===
“I have no parents; they died when I was at school. I belong to myself. All this”﻿—she raised the necklaces, turquoise, coral, white shell and silver, one by one, then let them fall back together﻿—“is mine. All this”﻿—she touched her rings, and shook her braceletted wrists﻿—“and much more is mine. They left it for me. Now I do a little work for the missionary’s wife there at Chiziai; she pays me money, so I grow richer. I shall give you silver to make jewelry, and I shall weave, and you shall have fine horses. You can make money with them, and we shall be rich together.”

===Chinese===
“我没有父母；我上学那阵他们死了。我归我自己。这些”——她把项链一件件提起来，绿松石的，珊瑚的，白贝的，银的，又让它们一齐落回去——“全是我的。这些”——她碰了碰戒指，抖了抖戴手镯的手腕，“还有更多，也是我的。他们留给我的。如今我在奇扎伊（Chiziai）那儿替传教士太太做点活计；她给我工钱，我就越来越富。我会给你银子打首饰，我会织布，你也会有骏马。你拿马能挣到钱，咱们就一起富起来。”

===Original===
The long, talking eyes looked into his now, with nothing hidden. He felt her strength, this woman who could talk so straight, who made the direct road seem the only sensible one. It ceased to be strange that they sat and talked about love, while elopement became obvious and commonplace in a scheme of things the whole of which was suddenly miraculous.

===Chinese===
那双会说话的长眼睛此刻直望进他的眼睛，什么也不遮藏。他感到了她的力量，这个女人能把话说得这样直，能把笔直的路说成唯一在理的路。两人这样坐着谈情说爱，忽然不再显得奇怪；私奔也在这幅图景里成了明摆着的寻常事，而这图景的全部，霎时间处处透着神迹。

===Original===
After a while she said, “We shall go tonight, after the races.”

===Chinese===
过了一会儿她说：“咱们今晚走，赛完马。”

===Original===
He reflected. “No, I came here to gamble. I told Red Man I would play against him. If I do not do it, he will say I am afraid.”

===Chinese===
他想了想。“不成，我是来耍钱的。我跟红汉子说过要同他赌。我要是不去，他准会说我怕了他。”

===Original===
“He is crooked; he will take your money.”

===Chinese===
“他手底下不干净；他会把你的钱掏光。”

===Original===
“That makes no difference; I cannot back down now. If I let this go because I was afraid to lose, what would I be? If I refused because of you, what kind of a man should I be for you?”

===Chinese===
“那也无所谓；事到如今我退不得。要是我怕输就撒了这局，我成什么了？要是因为你推了它，我在你跟前又算个什么男人？”

===Original===
He saw that he had spoken well.

===Chinese===
他自觉这番话说得漂亮。

===Original===
“It will be time for the races soon; you must go. I go the other way round.”

===Chinese===
“快到赛马的时辰了；你得走了。我打另一边绕。”

===Original===
He was in a new and more profound daze returning, but yards that had seemed miles were passed as inches. He floated over the ground, he was a walking song.

===Chinese===
回去的路上，他陷入一种新的、更深的恍惚；来时显得有几英里远的路，这会儿像只几寸，一迈就过去了。他飘在地面上走，他自己就是一支走着的歌。

## Chapter IV / 第四章

### I / I

===Original===
The horse-races were to be held in the latter part of the afternoon; during the hottest time almost everybody took a siesta, while those who were entering horses tended to them. Jesting Squaw’s Son joined Laughing Boy in going over the black pony. They discussed the other entries, agreeing that competition would be severe. A man from Navajo Mountain, in old-fashioned fringed buckskin shirt and high leggings, had brought a dun mare, said to be swift as thought. Jesting Squaw’s Son had seen her; she moved beautifully, he said. From Tsézhin came the undefeated bay, and the local contender, a big iron-grey, had a good reputation. Its sire was an American stallion, it was long in the quarters, and relatively heavy-boned; Laughing Boy thought that in a short racecourse﻿—the usual Navajo track is under a quarter of a mile﻿—it could not do justice to itself.

===Chinese===
赛马定在后半晌举行；最热的时辰里，几乎人人都歇了晌，只有报了名的人各自照看自己的马。打趣婆娘之子（Jesting Squaw’s Son）陪着笑娃（Laughing Boy）把这匹黑小马周身检视了一遍。两人议论着别家的参赛马，一致认定这回的对手不弱。有个纳瓦霍山（Navajo Mountain）来的汉子，穿一件老式镶流苏的鹿皮衬衫，套着高筒绑腿，牵来一匹暗褐色的母马，据说快得跟念头一般。打趣婆娘之子见过它；它跑起来的样子那个美，他说。特塞津（Tsézhin）那边来了那匹从没输过的枣红马，本地争魁的是匹大个头铁灰马，名声也响。它的父亲是匹美国种马，后躯偏长，骨架也偏粗；笑娃心想，赛道一短——纳瓦霍人惯常的赛道不足四分之一英里——它就使不出自己的真本事了。

===Original===
Laughing Boy planned to bet a little on the saddle-changing race, and put the rest of his money on himself. His friend would bet here and there, though mostly on him.

===Chinese===
笑娃打算在换鞍接力赛上押一点小注，其余的钱全押在自己身上。他朋友要各处押些，不过大半押他。

===Original===
“Are you going into the chicken-pull?” Jesting Squaw’s Son asked.

===Chinese===
“你去参加抢鸡赛吗？”打趣婆娘之子问。

===Original===
“Why not? That one race won’t tire my pet.”

===Chinese===
“怎么不去？单那一趟赛，累不着我的宝贝。”

===Original===
“But the chicken-pull will come first, they say.”

===Chinese===
“可抢鸡赛排在头里，他们说的。”

===Original===
“That’s bad. Why is that?”

===Chinese===
“那可糟了。这是为什么？”

===Original===
“That man from Tsézhin, his horse got loose, they say. He is out tracking it. So your race will be held last, to let him be in it.”

===Chinese===
“特塞津来的那个人，他的马跑脱了，他们说的。他正出去寻马踪呢。所以你那场赛挪到最后，好教他赶得上。”

===Original===
“The devil! Then I can’t go in the chicken-pull. I won’t risk having something happen to spoil this one. And you?”

===Chinese===
“见鬼！那我就进不成抢鸡赛了。我不能冒险出什么岔子，坏了这一场。你呢？”

===Original===
“I shall go in.”

===Chinese===
“我要进。”

===Original===
All the time they talked so, Laughing Boy was thinking, how do I do this? I am talking about the same things, thinking about them. And I am the man who is going away with that girl tomorrow. I am going away with Slim Girl. I feel like shouting. I am not as all these people.

===Chinese===
他们这么有一句没一句地说着话，笑娃心里却一直在想：我这是怎么撑下来的？我嘴上谈的是这些事，心里想的也是这些事。而我，就是明天要同那姑娘一起走掉的人。我要同瘦姑娘（Slim Girl）一起走掉了。我真想放声大喊。我跟这里所有的人，已经不是一路了。

===Original===
Jesting Squaw’s Son noticed something in his manner. “You seem very eager, my friend.”

===Chinese===
打趣婆娘之子瞧出他神情里有种东西。“你好像心气儿很足啊，我的朋友。”

===Original===
“Why not? Is not all well? I trade everything I have, two ponies, a blanket, five dollars, for this one because I love a fine pony, because I think this one is better than all that. Then I come down here, and right away I make nearly ninety dollars, when I began with nothing. Now we have a race. Nothing is more beautiful than galloping as hard as you can. I do this thing, that I love, on this pony that I bought for pleasure, where many people”﻿—and one person, oh, beautiful!﻿—“may see and speak well of me. If I win, I double my money, for doing what I enjoy. If I lose, it is only what I never had until yesterday.”

===Chinese===
“怎么不足？难道一切不都顺顺当当？我拿我全部的家当——两匹小马、一条毯子、五块钱——换来这一匹，因为我爱一匹好马，因为我认定这一匹比那些统统加起来都强。然后我下到这儿来，一转眼就挣了将近九十块，而我来的时候两手空空。如今又有赛马。天下再没有比撒开了劲狂奔更美的了。我干这件我爱干的事，骑这匹我为着欢喜买下的马，当着许多人的面”——还有一个人哪，啊，多美！——“叫人看见我、夸我。我赢了，钱翻一番，干的还是我乐意的事。输了，输的也不过是我直到昨天才头一回到手的东西。”

===Original===
And whatever happens, I have won more than all the money and hard goods in the world.

===Chinese===
而不论往后怎样，我赢得的，已经比世上所有的钱和硬货加起来还多。

===Original===
He meant what he said. Jesting Squaw’s Son nodded.

===Chinese===
他说的是真心话。打趣婆娘之子点了点头。

===Original===
There was a shot. The pony jumped. Then two shots together, from somewhere over to the right. Hastily tethering the animal, they raced to their camp to get their bows. People were running all about; women gathering around the campfires, packing up bundles, men snatching their weapons and making towards the noise. Three more shots had been fired, about ten seconds apart. The men did not rush towards the firing as Americans would; they went rapidly, but keeping a sharp lookout, and ready to take cover. Someone shouted that a Hopi had killed a Navajo; someone else called that it was Americans. Now they heard a burst of quick shooting, both rifles and revolvers, at a greater distance. Topping a slight ridge, the two friends saw the Navajos just ahead, nearly a hundred already, in an irregular, slightly crescent-shaped line. They came up and pushed to the front. No one was talking.

===Chinese===
一声枪响。小马蹦了起来。跟着又是两声齐发，打从右边什么地方传来。两人急忙把马拴牢，飞奔回营地取弓。人们四处奔跑；女人们围拢在火堆旁，把包袱一捆捆收拾起来，男人们抓起武器朝响动处赶去。又响了三枪，前后约莫隔十秒。男人们不像美国人那样直朝枪响处冲；他们走得快，却眼观六路，随时预备寻掩护。有人喊说是霍皮人（Hopi）杀了个纳瓦霍人；又有人叫说是美国人干的。这时他们听见更远处一阵急促的连射，步枪手枪都有。翻上一道小小的山梁，两个朋友看见纳瓦霍人就在前头，已经差不多上百了，排成一道不甚整齐、微微弯作月牙形的阵线。他们赶上去，挤到最前排。没有一个人说话。

===Original===
About twenty paces in front, facing the crescent, stood Tall Old One, the district headman, and an American from the agency in army hat, riding-breeches, and leather leggings. The American had a rifle and a revolver. Behind these two, in open order, stood Man Hammer and Left Hand, policemen, and a Hopi and a Tewa policeman, all with rifles. The latter two wore parts of khaki uniforms. Over to one side a Navajo leant against a tree, looking sick. Blood ran down his sleeve and dripped from his fingers; at his feet lay a revolver. Farther back another policeman, Mud’s Son, stood guard over a handcuffed Navajo, and, partly hidden by a clump of bushes, somebody was stretched out on the ground.

===Chinese===
月牙阵前约莫二十步，站着老高个（Tall Old One）——本区的头人——和一个代理处来的美国人，头戴军帽，下身马裤、皮绑腿。那美国人带一支步枪、一把手枪。这两人身后散开着站的是锤子汉（Man Hammer）和左手（Left Hand），都是警察，还有一名霍皮人警察和一名特瓦人（Tewa）警察，人人端着步枪。后两个身上穿着几件卡其制服。一边有个纳瓦霍人倚着树站立，像是要呕的样子。血顺着他袖子往下淌，从指头上滴下来；他脚边躺着一支左轮手枪。再靠后些，另一名警察泥之子（Mud’s Son）看守着一个戴手铐的纳瓦霍人，还有一个人直挺挺躺在地上，半掩在一丛灌木后头。

===Original===
The American official and the Hopi were acutely conscious of the fact that several hundred Navajos were thinking that these aliens had started something, and if only the native officials would step aside it might as well be finished now. They also knew that those same officials were aware of this feeling, and sympathized with it. There were a couple of dozen rifles and revolvers in the crowd, and at that range a bow is just as effective. The Indians were all looking at the wounded man; he made an ugly exhibit.

===Chinese===
那美国官员和那霍皮人心下雪亮：几百个纳瓦霍人此刻正在想，是这些外来人挑起的事端，只消本族的头人们往边上一闪，眼下就可以把这笔账了结。他们也知道，这些头人对这股情绪并非不知情，而且心里还向着它。人群里有二三十条步枪和手枪，而在那个距离上，一张弓同样使得。印第安人的眼睛全都望着那个伤者；他这一身摆出来，样子难看得很。

===Original===
The Tewa policeman shifted from foot to foot and grinned. The situation might become serious, but he thought it would work out all right, and he devoutly hoped for an arrest involving a fistfight with a Navajo. Tewas punch; Navajos kick, scratch, and pull hair. For several centuries the Tewas’ official profession was fighting Navajos.

===Chinese===
那特瓦人警察两脚倒换着挪，咧着嘴笑。局面兴许会闹大，可他想总会化解过去，他还一门心思盼着来一场逮捕，好捎带跟哪个纳瓦霍人打一场拳头架。特瓦人抡拳头；纳瓦霍人踢、挠、薅头发。好几百年了，特瓦人的正式营生就是打纳瓦霍人。

===Original===
Nobody knew quite what had happened. A Navajo was arrested, and one was wounded. There was a dead man, but they couldn’t see of what tribe. The older men hoped there would be no trouble; nothing to bring soldiers into the reservation; the younger braves all wanted to start something. Men began to sidle off to the left and right, slowly carrying the horns of the crescent farther around the police. In time, they would have them surrounded.

===Chinese===
谁也不大清楚出了什么事。一个纳瓦霍人被抓了，一个受了伤。还躺着一个死人，却看不出是哪个部族的。年长的盼着别出乱子；别闹出把军队招进保留地的事；年轻的好汉们个个想撩起点什么。人们开始朝左右两边悄悄挪动，慢慢把月牙的两只角往警察身后兜。再有些工夫，就能把他们围死。

===Original===
Tall Old One called: “Wait! Make no mistake! Everything is well and you have no cause to be angry.”

===Chinese===
老高个扬声喊道：“且慢！不要弄错！一切都好，你们没有动气的由头。”

===Original===
They obeyed him, and the tension relaxed slightly.

===Chinese===
众人听了他这话，绷紧的劲儿松开了几分。

===Original===
A man said, “There come some more people.”

===Chinese===
有人说：“又来了些人。”

===Original===
Another cried, “An American is hurt!”

===Chinese===
另一个喊道：“伤的是个美国人！”

===Original===
People felt better immediately.

===Chinese===
众人顿时心里好受起来。

===Original===
“Two Americans﻿—look!”

===Chinese===
“两个美国人——瞧！”

===Original===
They began to talk excitedly, and some of them smiled. The government man let out a sigh and threw his gun across his left arm. Man Hammer said something cheerful to Left Hand.

===Chinese===
人们兴奋地议论开了，有几个笑了。那政府里的人舒出一口气，把枪横搁到左臂上。锤子汉对左手说了句什么提气的话。

===Original===
The newcomers arrived from the direction of the burst of shots that had been fired last. Thin American, the trader from Tséchil, and an unknown Navajo supported between them a badly wounded man who swore slowly and steadily. Behind them a Tewa supported another American official, who limped.

===Chinese===
新来的人从最后那阵枪响的方向走来。瘦子美国佬（Thin American），特塞奇尔（Tséchil）来的商人，和一个不认识的纳瓦霍人，中间架着一个伤得很重的人，那人有条不紊、不紧不慢地咒骂着。他们身后，一个特瓦人搀着另一个美国官员，那人一瘸一拐。

===Original===
