They set the wounded men down by the handcuffed Navajo, the Tewa lined up beside his fellow tribesman, and Thin American came to talk with the official. After a minute of discussion, he interpreted to Tall Old One.

===Chinese===
他们把两个伤者放在戴手铐的纳瓦霍人旁边，那特瓦人挨着他的同族人站定，瘦子美国佬过来同那官员说话。商量了一阵，他把话译给老高个听。

===Original===
The headman stepped forward.

===Chinese===
头人跨步上前。

===Original===
“Hear me, my friends. You know how bad it is to drink whiskey, how it makes you crazy. You know how Washindon has forbidden it. Now the American here, this man whom you have seen brought in, came here to sell it. That Navajo”﻿—he pointed to the handcuffed one﻿—“came with him. That was bad. So American Chief sent these Americans and policemen to stop it.”

===Chinese===
“听我说，朋友们。你们都晓得喝威士忌有多坏，晓得它把人变成疯子。你们晓得瓦辛顿（Washindon，指美国政府）早把这东西禁了。如今这个美国人，就是你们眼看着押进来的这个，是来卖它的。那个纳瓦霍人”——他指了指戴手铐的那个——“是跟他一道来的。那就坏了。所以美国酋长（American Chief）派这些美国人和警察来制止。”

===Original===
As he talked, Thin American translated to the agency man.

===Chinese===
他讲着，瘦子美国佬便一句句译给代理处那人听。

===Original===
“Already one man was drunk; that wounded one over there by the tree. See what it did for him. When we started to arrest them, he began to shoot. He killed that Hopi you see back there.”

===Chinese===
“先前就有一个人喝醉了，就是那边树底下的那个伤号。瞧瞧这东西把他弄成了什么样。我们动手抓他们的时候，他就开了枪。他打死了你们看见躺在后头的那位霍皮人。”

===Original===
So it was a Hopi killed. There were more smiles.

===Chinese===
原来死的是个霍皮人。笑的人更多了。

===Original===
“Now he and this man, the one who helped to sell it, and that man must all go to jail. You know it is right. There is nothing to upset you; there is nothing to spoil your races. After all, there has been a Hopi killed, and two Americans wounded; now an American and two Navajos go to jail for a little while. That is not so bad.”

===Chinese===
“如今他，还有这个人，帮着卖东西的那个，还有那个人，都得去蹲监牢。你们知道这是应当的。没有什么叫你们烦心的；没有什么要坏你们的赛会。毕竟，死了一个霍皮人，伤了两个美国人；如今一个美国人和两个纳瓦霍人去蹲一阵子监牢。这不算太坏。”

===Original===
The trader suppressed a smile as he skipped translating this last remark, saying only, “He’s letting ’em think that Indian will only get a light sentence for shooting the policeman. Best leave it at that.”

===Chinese===
商人忍住笑，最后一段话他跳过去没译，只说：“他这是让他们当那印第安人打了警察也只判个轻罪。最好就照这样。”

===Original===
Older men remarked, “That is right, that is well said. Let us have no trouble.” Some of the young men grumbled, but others asked them, “What would you do? You can’t fight Washindon. Do you want them to send soldiers in here again? Shall we go into exile again?” It was news, an incident, something to talk about. The crowd became just a lot of people, watching the first aid, and talkative.

===Chinese===
年长的人议论：“这话在理，说得好。咱们别生事端。”几个年轻人嘟囔，别的就问他们：“不然你们要怎样？你们打不过瓦辛顿。要他们再把军队派进来吗？要我们再被赶去流放吗？”这算是桩新闻，一桩事件，够人谈论的。人群散了杀气，成了寻常的一群看客，看着包扎救治，你一言我一语。

===Original===
“Have the Hopi and Tewas take off that dead man,” the trader advised; “they’ll never forgive you if you leave a corpse for them to take care of; spoil their party and make ’em leave. They’re plumb scared to death of a corpse. Look how those Navajo policemen are edging away from it.”

===Chinese===
“叫那霍皮人和特瓦人把死人抬走吧，”商人出主意道；“留一具尸首给他们照管，他们会记你一辈子的仇；搅了这场会，还把人吓走。他们对死人怕得要命。瞧那几个纳瓦霍警察，正躲着它往边上蹭呢。”

===Original===
Horses were brought up, the wounded and prisoners mounted. The Pueblo policemen slung their comrade’s body across a saddle. The party rode off, leaving Tall Old One and the local police to return to their games.

===Chinese===
马牵了过来，伤的和被抓的都上了马。普韦布洛（Pueblo）警察们把同伴的尸首横搭上一副马鞍。这一队人马走远了，老高个和本地警察留下，回去接着他们的游艺。

===Original===
The first Tewa remarked to the other, “No fight.”

===Chinese===
头一个特瓦人对另一个说：“没打成。”

===Original===
“No. But it would have been more shooting, anyhow.”

===Chinese===
“没有。不过反正少不了多放一阵枪。”

===Original===
“Some day, perhaps, we arrest an American, unwounded.”

===Chinese===
“兴许哪天，咱们能逮个囫囵没伤的美国人。”

===Original===
“Some day, perhaps.”

===Chinese===
“兴许有这么一天。”

===Original===
They looked at their knuckles.

===Chinese===
两人看了看自己的指节。

### II / II

===Original===
The two friends returned to the pony.

===Chinese===
两个朋友回到小马跟前。

===Original===
“What is this whiskey?” Jesting Squaw’s Son asked. “I am always hearing talk about it. They say it is so bad, yet they try so hard to get it.”

===Chinese===
“这威士忌到底是个什么东西？”打趣婆娘之子问。“我老是听人讲起它。他们说它坏透了，可又那么下力气想弄到手。”

===Original===
“I do not know. They all say it is very bad. It makes you crazy, they say. It must be like eating jimpson-weed I think.”

===Chinese===
“我不知道。人人都说它坏极了，说它把人喝成疯子。我想那准跟吃了曼陀罗草差不多。”

===Original===
“It made that man crazy. He tried to fight alone.”

===Chinese===
“它就叫那个人疯了。他单枪匹马就想打。”

===Original===
“M‑m. It made him brave, I think. But it stopped his sense. When a thing like that happens, a number of men coming against you, you run away first. Then you can get behind something and start shooting.”

===Chinese===
“嗯。我想它叫他胆子大了。可它把他的脑子堵住了。碰上那样的事，一伙人朝你围上来，先得跑。然后寻个遮拦躲起来，再开枪不迟。”

===Original===
“Anyhow, he killed a Hopi.”

===Chinese===
“不管怎么说，他打死了一个霍皮人。”

===Original===
“*Ei-yei!* He shot straight! But jail is very bad, they say.”

===Chinese===
“咿-耶！他打得真准！可蹲监牢苦得很，他们说。”

===Original===
“Well, that’s just for a few months, and he will have that to think about. When he comes back, people will think well of him.”

===Chinese===
“嗐，不过几个月的事，往后他也有的回想了。等他回来，人们会高看他。”

===Original===
The call sounded for the first race, which was the saddle-changing relay. They separated in the crowd, which split into two parties according to whom it backed. Laughing Boy put two dollars on a group of active young men with a short-coupled pony that looked as if it could turn smartly and not get flustered.

===Chinese===
头一场赛的号令响了，正是换鞍接力赛。两人在人群里分开，人群也照各人押的是谁分成两派。笑娃把两块钱押给一伙精悍的年轻人，他们那匹小马身架紧凑，瞧着转弯伶俐，沉得住气。

===Original===
The ponies were saddled and mounted. The cinch-strap was carried through the ring of the girth, then up to the horn, where the rider held it fast with one hand, a finger of which also hooked in the reins. The other hand held a quirt ready to strike. The men were stripped to breechclout and moccasins, slender, golden-brown bodies, the bodies of perfect boys, under the dark colour a glow of red showing through.

===Chinese===
小马们备好了鞍，骑手上了马。肚带尾穿过肚带环，再往上拉到鞍角，骑手一只手紧紧按住它，一根指头还勾着缰绳。另一只手握一条短马鞭，随时预备抽下。汉子们脱得只剩一条遮裆布、一双鹿皮靴，细挑挑金褐色的身子，十足少年的身子，深色底下透出隐隐一层红光。

===Original===
Now! The ponies scampered, people shouted. The horsemen flashed to earth, bringing their saddles with them, the ponies were wheeled around. Bare arms and backs rippled as the new saddles were swung on, the cinch-strap caught through, held to the horn by the same hand on which the new rider swung as he leaped to the saddle, the horse already in motion under him.

===Chinese===
起跑了！小马们撒开蹄子，人群喊成一片。骑手们闪电般翻身落地，鞍子随身带了下来，小马们被拽转过头。赤裸的臂膀和脊背滚动着筋肉，新鞍抡上去，肚带尾一把穿好，还是那只手按住鞍角，新骑手荡身跃上鞍去，胯下的马已经跑动起来。

===Original===
A man’s foot slipped. Everyone laughed and cheered. It was a close race. Now the last men were mounting. The one on the team Laughing Boy was backing lost his grip on the strap, and the saddle turned under him. He wrenched it back, throwing his weight in the stirrups, then clinched his legs under the horse’s belly. But he had thrown his mount out of its stride, and he lost by a good length. They laughed more, and called jokes to him,

===Chinese===
有个人的脚打了滑。众人大笑喝彩。这场胜负咬得紧。这时最后一批骑手正在上鞍。笑娃押的那队，骑手没按住带子，鞍子在他身子底下拧了个转。他拼命把鞍扳回来，全身分量压进马镫，跟着两腿夹死马肚。可他这一折腾乱了马的步子，输了足足一个多马身。众人笑得更欢，冲他喊笑话——

===Original===
“Grease on your fingers, Grandfather! You should have held the strap in your teeth!”

===Chinese===
“指头上抹了油吧，老爷爷！你该拿牙咬住那带子！”

===Original===
Laughing Boy went to pay his bet. They were organizing the chicken-pull. The chicken was a salt-bag half full of dirt. A piece of blue cloth tied around its neck was the head; two bits of red at the bottom corners were the legs. Whoever threw the head over the line, a hundred yards away, won five dollars; each of the legs brought two.

===Chinese===
笑娃去付了赌注。那边正在张罗抢鸡赛。所谓鸡，是半袋子土的盐袋。脖颈上扎一块蓝布，算鸡头；底下两角缀两片红布，算两条腿。谁把鸡头甩过一百码开外的那道线，赢五块钱；一条腿兑两块。

===Original===
Laughing Boy drifted around the edge of the crowd, gay and excited. Never had there been such a four days! He had an eye out for Slim Girl, and saw her at last, sitting slightly apart from a group of women. Their eyes met, then he moved away.

===Chinese===
笑娃满心快活亢奋，在人群边上悠悠地转。从来没有过这样的四天！他留神找着瘦姑娘，终于瞧见了，她坐在一群妇人近旁，略略离开些。两人的目光碰在一处，他便走开了。

===Original===
Red Man hailed him. “You are racing a horse, Grandfather?”

===Chinese===
红汉子（Red Man）招呼他。“你要赛马是吧，老爷爷？”

===Original===
“Yes.”

===Chinese===
“是。”

===Original===
“I hope you win. I shall take it all away from you tonight.”

===Chinese===
“盼你赢。今儿夜里我把它统统从你手里赢走。”

===Original===
“All right.”

===Chinese===
“好啊。”

===Original===
He turned out of the crowd to avoid him; the man made him feel disagreeable. Towards him walked a pinto pony with too-long ears, carrying Half Man, his father’s brother. Laughing Boy watched him sorrowfully as he approached, considering the withered arm and leg, the wasted appearance of this man, and remembering Wolf Killer, the tall, cheerful brave he had known as a boy, before the Ute arrow grazed the right side of his head and, by some strange Ute magic, shrivelled the left side of his body.

===Chinese===
他拐出人群躲开这个人；这人叫他浑身不对味。迎着他走来的是一匹耳朵过长的花斑小马，驮着半人（Half Man），他的伯父。笑娃难过地望着他走近，看着那条枯瘦的胳膊和腿、这个人干瘪下去的模样，想起杀狼汉（Wolf Killer）——他小时候认识的那个高大爽朗的好汉——那是在那支尤特人（Ute）的箭擦过他头右侧、又不知凭着什么古怪的尤特妖法把他左半边身子枯干之前。

===Original===
“*Ahalani*, nephew. Are you here alone?”

===Chinese===
“阿哈拉尼（Ahalani），侄儿。你一个人在这儿？”

===Original===
“Yes. It is good to see you.”

===Chinese===
“是。见到你真好。”

===Original===
“Are all well?”

===Chinese===
“家里都好？”

===Original===
“All are well, but there has been very little rain this spring.”

===Chinese===
“都好，只是今年春天雨水少得很。”

===Original===
“Too bad. The chicken-pull is starting. Aren’t you in it?”

===Chinese===
“那真糟。抢鸡赛开了。你不上一场？”

===Original===
“I have only one horse; that I am riding in the last race.”

===Chinese===
“我就这一匹马；还得留着跑最后一场。”

===Original===
“You should be in it. I should have been in it at your age. This horse is all right; take it.”

===Chinese===
“你该上的。在你这岁数，我准上。这马还行；牵去。”

===Original===
He dismounted clumsily, taking a walking-stick from behind the saddle. Laughing Boy felt his eyes sting.

===Chinese===
他笨手笨脚下了马，从鞍后抽出一根手杖。笑娃只觉眼里一酸。

===Original===
“*Ukehé*, Thank you.”

===Chinese===
“Ukehé——谢谢你。”

===Original===
Navajos almost never say thank you, save in return for very great favours; ordinary gifts and kindnesses are offered and accepted in silence. They regard our custom as obsequious. The word was startled out of Laughing Boy by the occasion. Half Man understood, and avoided his nephew’s gaze as he limped away, the fingers of his useless arm hooked into the front of his silver belt.

===Chinese===
纳瓦霍人几乎从不说谢谢，除非受了天大的恩情；寻常的馈赠和好意，都是默默地给、默默地受。他们把我们这种道谢的规矩看作低声下气。这一回，是这个场合把这句话从笑娃口中惊了出来。半人懂得，一瘸一拐走开去，避开侄儿的目光，那条不中用的胳膊的手指，勾在银腰带前头。

===Original===
The chicken was buried in loose earth, so that just enough of the neck of the sack stuck up to let one get a good grip. A referee stood near, armed with a long horsehair quirt; as each horseman rode past, he swung full force across the animal’s rump, thus ensuring an honest gallop. Laughing Boy cantered up in his turn, tried to hold his pony in, felt it leap to the smack of the whip, and reached too late for the prize. He watched the next few tries, rode back, argued with Slender Hair about his place, and went at it again. He was leaning well down from the saddle before the quirt fell, he could have touched the ground with his fingers. *Smack!* and the pony jumped slightly sideways. The chicken was out of reach. He swung back to his seat and rested. Horse after horse came by, well in hand, then leaped to the stroke of the whip, or shied away from it. The horsemen swooped, swinging incredibly low, reaching amazingly far out, in a haze of dust.

===Chinese===
那鸡埋在浮土里，袋口只露短短一截，刚够攥个满把。一名裁判站在近旁，手里一条长长的马鬃短鞭；每个骑手跑过，他便抡圆了朝马屁股上狠抽一记，务教马匹诚诚实实地放开四蹄。轮到笑娃，他催马小跑上前，想先把小马勒一勒，只觉它应着鞭子的啪声一跃而起，伸手去抓，迟了一步，错过了彩头。他看后面几个人试过，拨转马头回去，跟细发（Slender Hair）争了自己该排的位置，又来了一回。这一回鞭子还没落，他已从鞍上把身子探得极低，手指头都能触到地了。啪！小马微微往旁一闪。鸡够不着了。他荡回鞍上坐着歇气。一匹马接着一匹马过来，本来收得服服帖帖，鞭子一响便蹿出去，要么就闪身躲鞭。骑手们凌空扑下去，身形压得低得叫人不敢信，胳膊探得出奇地远，裹在一团尘雾里。

===Original===
*Ya-hai! E-ya-hai! Ei-yei!* Straight Fingers had it. Straight Fingers galloped for the line. All the young men rose in their saddles, their elbows were spread forward, their knees clutched, their quirts fell on willing ponies. Those who had been waiting just for this headed him off, the others caught up with him. It became a big, spinning wheel of mounted braves, horses’ tossing heads, and dust. Laughing Boy saw Straight Fingers just ahead of him, clinging to the chicken’s head, while someone else held both its legs. He took a lick at the next horse in front of him, saw it carom, and reached for the prize, yelling. Somebody cracked him over the head with the butt of a quirt; somebody else tried to pull him off. He defended himself, wrestling with the man who had grabbed him, while the two ponies plunged, then both let go as the mob swirled away from them.

===Chinese===
呀-嗨！咿-呀-嗨！咿-耶！直手指（Straight Fingers）抢着了。直手指纵马朝那道线奔去。年轻人都从鞍上立起身来，胳膊肘朝前张着，膝盖夹紧，短鞭雨点般落在情愿卖力的小马身上。专等这一刻的人迎头截他，其余的从后面追上。这成了一只飞转的大轮盘：马上的是好汉，抛起的是马头，卷起的是尘土。笑娃看见直手指就在前头，死死攥着鸡头，另一个人还攥着它两条腿。他朝他前面那匹马抽了一记，见它磕偏了出去，便喊着伸手去夺彩头。有人抡鞭柄敲了他的头；又有人要拽他下马。他一边招架，一边同揪住他的那人扭打，两匹小马直立暴跳，随后两人双双撒手，人堆打着旋从他们身旁卷开了。

===Original===
Straight Fingers broke away, and threw the head over the line. Somebody else threw over both legs. Laughing Boy and the stranger, a young fellow with a mustache, laughed at each other.

===Chinese===
直手指冲脱出来，把鸡头甩过了线。另一个人把两条腿也甩了过去。笑娃和那个陌生的小伙子——嘴上蓄着两撇小胡子的——相视大笑。

===Original===
“This has been a lovely day!” said the young man.

===Chinese===
“今天这一天，美得很呐！”年轻人说。

===Original===
“Yes, a perfect day!”

===Chinese===
“是啊，十全十美的一天！”

===Original===
“Aren’t you the man from T’o Tlakai who has a horse to race? Is *that* the horse?”

===Chinese===
“你不就是特奥·特莱凯（T’o Tlakai）来的、有马要赛的那位吗？胯下这匹就是那马？”

===Original===
“I’m the man. This is not the horse.”

===Chinese===
“是我。这不是那匹马。”

===Original===
And I am the man whom that girl loves, I am the man who is going away with the magic girl.

===Chinese===
而我，就是那姑娘爱着的人，就是要同那有魔法的姑娘一起远走的人。

### III / III

===Original===
The man from Tsézhin had found his horse; now the last race was called. Laughing Boy placed everything except his bow-guard in bets. If he won, he would have nearly two hundred dollars. Now he stripped off his clothes to his breechclout, settled his headband, and adjusted the light hackamore around his horse’s nose.

===Chinese===
特塞津那个人找回了他的马；如今最后一场的号令也叫响了。笑娃把除了弓护腕以外的一切都押了上去。赢了，他就差不多有两百块钱了。他脱掉衣裳，只留一条遮裆布，扎正束发带，又把那副轻巧的笼头在马鼻梁上拢正。

===Original===
They gathered at the starting place, good horses and eager, with shiny coats, erect ears, and quick, small hooves. Hurries to War, the starting judge, cautioned them. Laughing Boy saw Slim Girl, halfway down the track, watching him.

===Chinese===
好马们聚到起跑的地方，匹匹急着要跑，毛色油亮，耳朵直竖，小小的蹄子踏得轻快。起跑裁判急赴战场（Hurries to War）告诫了他们一番。笑娃望见瘦姑娘在赛道中段，正望着他。

===Original===
Here I am doing just this, and these others do not know who I am. They do not know they are racing against the man who goes with her tomorrow. Oh, I must win, I want to win, I must win!

===Chinese===
我眼下干的正是这件事，可这些人都不知道我是谁。他们不知道，他们赛的是明天要同她一起走的那个人。啊，我必须赢，我要赢，我必须赢！

===Original===
He made a very brief prayer, and patted his pony’s neck. “Little sister, we must win. Do not fail me.”

===Chinese===
他匆匆祷告了一句，拍了拍小马的脖子。“小妹妹，咱们必须赢。别辜负我。”

===Original===
They mounted. He felt the warm, silky hide between his knees, pushed her up to the reins and felt the play of muscles. Counting for the start; he leant forward, held his breath, raised his quirt. Go!

===Chinese===
众人上马。他觉出两膝之间那温暖缎子似的皮毛，催她拢上缰，觉出肌肉的一收一放。起点在报数；他伏身向前，屏住呼吸，扬起短鞭。跑！

===Original===
Arrows from the bow﻿—no other simile. At the tearing gallop, flat-stretched, backs are level, the animals race in a straight line; all life is motion; there is no body, only an ecstasy; one current between man and horse, and still embodied, a whip hand to pour in leather and a mouth to shout. Speed, speed, but the near goal is miles away, and other speed spirits on either side will not fall back.

===Chinese===
离弦之箭——再没有第二个比方。撕裂般的疾驰里，人马平贴着抻开，脊背齐平，牲口们循一条直线竞逐；整个生命全是奔驰；身子没有了，只剩一阵狂喜；人与马之间只一道流，却又还寄在形骸里——一只鞭手往皮革上灌劲，一张嘴放声呐喊。快，快，可近在眼前的目标还像隔着多少里，两翼另一些速度的精灵又不肯退后半步。

===Original===
*E-é-é-é-é!* His left hand, held forward, would push the horse through slack reins, his heels under her belly would lift her clear of the ground. *E-é-é-é-é!*

===Chinese===
咿-欸-欸-欸-欸！他左手朝前递着，隔着松松的缰绳把马往前送，脚跟在她肚腹下磕动，恨不得把她整个儿从地上托起来。咿-欸-欸-欸-欸！

===Original===
A quiet, elderly Indian let his hand fall. The ponies cantered, trotted, were turned and walked back to the finish.

===Chinese===
一位安安静静的年长印第安人垂下了手。小马们收成小跑、快步，被拨转过头，朝终点线走了回去。

===Original===
“The black mare was first.”

===Chinese===
“黑母马跑了第一。”

===Original===
He rode off to his camp, to dress. We won. I am rich. Was there ever such a day? And tomorrow I go with that girl. Oh, beautiful! I wish it were tomorrow now. About a hundred and eighty dollars. My pet, my little black pet, well done. I wish it were tomorrow.

===Chinese===
他骑马回自己的营地穿衣裳去了。我们赢了。我有钱了。哪里有过这样的日子？明天我就同那姑娘走了。啊，真好！但愿现在就是明天。约莫一百八十块钱。我的宝贝，我的小黑宝贝，干得好。但愿现在就是明天。

## Chapter V / 第五章

### I / I

===Original===
Laughing Boy went off alone to wrestle with gods: Slim Girl turned to loneliness as a tried friend and counsellor. To be with herself, complete to herself, that was familiar reality; distraction and strangeness was to be among many, to consult. On a high place she sat down to think, not facing the greatness of the desert, but where she could look on her gathered people, made small and impersonal below her. Long habit and self-training had made her cool and contained; she did not like to admit that she had need of mere emotion, and when she did allow herself a luxury of feeling, it had to be where none could spy on her. It was not that she would make any demonstration; she just did not want to be looked at when she was not quite mistress of herself. Now her isolated, high position put a physical difference between her impulses and those of the people in the valley, making them visibly superior. She lit a cigarette and relaxed.

===Chinese===
笑娃（Laughing Boy）独自走开了，去同诸神角力；瘦姑娘（Slim Girl）则转向孤独，把它当作一位考验过的老友和谋士。独自同自己在一起，独自完足，这是她熟悉的实在；分神与生疏，倒是置身众人之中、事事与人商量的时光。她在一处高地上坐下思量，并不去面朝沙漠的浩大，而是坐在能把聚齐的族人尽收眼底的地方——他们在她脚下显得渺小，看不出一个个是谁。多年的习惯和自我磨砺使她冷静克制；她不愿承认自己还需要纯粹的情感这东西，而当她果真放任自己奢侈地感受一回时，也必须寻个没人窥看的地方。倒不是说她会有什么举动做给人看；她只是不愿在自己还拿不太住自己的时候被人端详。此刻，她孤悬高踞的位置，在她和谷中众人的种种冲动之间隔出一道实实在在的差距，叫她的冲动明显高出一头。她点起一支烟，松弛了下来。

===Original===
If she did not watch out, she would love this man. She did not intend to love anyone; had she not learned enough of that? He was necessary to her; he was the perfect implement delivered to her hands; he was an axe with which to hew down the past; he was a light with which to see her way back to her people, to the good things of her people. She held him up against the past, matrons and teachers at school, platitudes and well-meaning lies. And now, for all their care and training and preaching, she was “going back to the blanket,” because under the blanket were the things worth while, and all the rest was hideous. With her knowledge and experience, with what the Americans had taught her, she would lead this man, and make for them both the most perfect life that could be made﻿—with an Indian, a long-haired, heathen Indian, a blanket Indian, a Navajo, the names thrown out like an insult in the faces of those who bore them, of her own people, Denné, The People, proud as she was proud, and clear of heart as she could never be.

===Chinese===
她若不留神，就要爱上这个男人了。她不打算爱上任何人；这上头的苦头她还没吃够么？他对她是个必需；他是交到她手里的一件顶顺手的器具；他是一把斧子，好拿来劈倒过去；他是一盏灯，好照见她走回自己族人那里去的路，走回族人种种美好事物那里去的路。她拿他去对照过去——学校里的女监学和教师们，陈词滥调，一片好心的谎话。而如今，任凭他们怎么照料、训导、说教，她到底是要“回到毯子里去”了，因为毯子底下才是值得的东西，其余的一切都丑陋。凭着她的知识和阅历，凭着美国人教给她的本事，她要引领这个男人，为他们俩造出所能造出的最完美的生活——就同一个印第安人来过：一个长头发的、异教徒的印第安人，一个毯子印第安人，一个纳瓦霍人；这些名号像辱骂一样甩出去，甩在背负它们的人的脸上，甩在她自己族人的脸上——迪内（Denné），“人”，这一族人像她一样高傲，心地澄澈，那是她永远也及不上的。

===Original===
There were to be no mistakes, and no chances. She could tie this man to her as surely as any prisoner; she would follow her clear plan to its victorious end. She had conquered herself, she had conquered circumstance; emerging from the struggle not American, not Indian, mistress of herself. Now from the Americans she took means, and in the Indians would achieve her end. Not such an amazing end, perhaps, but strange enough for her: a home in the Northern desert, and children, in a place where the agent’s men never came to snatch little children from their parents and send them off to school. They would be Navajo, all Navajo, those children, when the time came. This was her revenge, that all the efforts of all those very different Americans, to drag her up or to drag her down into the American way, in the end would be only tools to serve a Navajo end.

===Chinese===
不许有差错，也不碰运气。她能把这个男人拴在自己身边，拴得像拴住任何一个囚徒那样十拿九稳；她要顺着那条清晰的计划走到底，走到胜利的终点。她已经征服了自己，征服了境遇；从这场搏斗里脱身出来，既不是美国人，也不是印第安人，是自己一身的主宰。如今她从美国人那里取来手段，要在印第安人身上成全自己的目的。这目的算不上多么惊人，或许，可对她也够奇特了：在北方的沙漠里安一个家，生下孩子，在一个印第安事务官的手下从不来把小孩子从爹娘身边抓走、送去上学的地方。到时候，那些孩子会是纳瓦霍人，彻头彻尾的纳瓦霍人。这就是她的报复：那许多各不相同的美国人，千方百计要把她拖进美国人的活法里去——往上拖也罢，往下拽也罢——到头来，统统只落得给一个纳瓦霍人的目的当差遣的工具。

===Original===
It made her happy to think of that man, Laughing Boy. He was more than just what that name implied; one felt the warrior under the gaiety, and by his songs and his silver, he was an artist. All Navajo, even to his faults, he would teach her the meaning of those oft-repeated phrases, “*bik’é hojoni*, the trail of beauty”; through him she would learn the content, and she would provide the means.

===Chinese===
想起笑娃那个人，她心里就欢喜。他不只是他那个名字点出的那点意思；欢快底下能觉出一名武人，而凭他的歌、他的银饰，他是个艺人。浑身上下都是纳瓦霍，连毛病也是纳瓦霍的，他会教她懂得那些念了又念的话——“bik’é hojoni，美之踪迹”；通过他，她将习得内容，而手段由她来供给。

===Original===
Yes, this was her man, as though he had been made expressly for her, strong, straight, gay, a little stubborn. He had character, she would develop it. And she would bind him. There would be no second wife in her hogan.

===Chinese===
不错，这就是她的男人，仿佛是专为她造出来的：健壮，挺直，快活，还有几分倔。他有性子，她要调教这性子。她还要拴牢他。她的霍根屋（hogan，纳瓦霍人泥木筑的小屋）里，可不会有什么第二个老婆。

===Original===
“Patience,” she told herself; “you are not in the Northern desert yet. You have a long road to travel yet, full of ambushes.”

===Chinese===
“沉住气，”她对自己说；“你还没到北方的沙漠呢。你还有长长的路要走，一路都是埋伏。”

===Original===
She had no intention of herding sheep and slaving away her youth in a few years of hard labour, herding sheep, hoeing corn, packing firewood, growing square across the hips and flat in the face and heavy in the legs. No; she had seen the American women. First there was money; the Americans must serve her a little while yet; then, after that, the unmapped canyons, and the Indians who spoke no English.

===Chinese===
她可没打算放羊，没打算拿几年苦活儿把青春作践成奴隶——放羊，锄玉米，背柴火，落到胯骨宽方、脸颊平板、两腿粗重的地步。不；她见识过美国女人。先得有钱；美国人还得为她再效一阵子力；然后，在那之后，是那些地图上没有标出来的峡谷，和那些不会说英语的印第安人。

===Original===
She sat perfectly still, looking at nothing and hating Americans. She had not turned herself loose like this in a long time. Some young man, far below, was singing a gay song about the owl that turned her thoughts to Laughing Boy; she relaxed and smiled. This was something happy to think of. He came like the War God in the song, she thought, and began to sing it haltingly, not sure of the exact words﻿—

===Chinese===
她一动不动地坐着，望着虚空，心里恨着美国人。她好久没有这样撒开自己了。底下远处，不知哪个小伙子正唱着一支唱猫头鹰的快活歌子，把她的念头引到了笑娃身上；她松快下来，笑了。这是件想着就叫人高兴的事。他就像那支歌里的战神，她想，便开始磕磕绊绊地唱起来，字句拿不太准——

===Original===
“Now Slayer of Enemy Gods, alone I see him coming;
						

						Down from the skies, alone I see him coming.
						

						His voice sounds all about. *Lé-é!*
						

						His voice sounds, divine. *Lé-é!*”

===Chinese===
“如今，诛敌神者（Slayer of Enemy Gods），我看见他独自来临；

						自天而降，我看见他独自来临。

						他的声音响彻四方。莱-埃！

						他的声音，声声如神。莱-埃！”

===Original===
That is he, she thought, Slayer of Enemy Gods. He would be shocked to hear me say it, to hear a woman sing that song.

===Chinese===
就是他，她想，诛敌神者。他要是听见我这样说——听见一个女人唱这种歌——非吓一跳不可。

===Original===
She went on to the formal ending, “In beauty it is finished, in beauty it is finished,” then changed it, “In beauty it is begun. In beauty it is begun. Thanks.”

===Chinese===
她接着唱到那套正式的收尾，“在美之中告成，在美之中告成”，随即改了词，“在美之中开启。在美之中开启。感谢。”

===Original===
That is a good religion, as good as Christianity. I wonder if I can learn to believe in it? One needs some religion. At least, I can get good out of its ideas. If he is that god, what am I? White Shell Woman? Changing Woman, perhaps. I must mould and guide this War God I have made. I must not let him get away from me. None of the bad things must happen; I must make no mistakes. I am not a Navajo, nor am I an American, but the Navajos are my people.

===Chinese===
这是个好宗教，跟基督教一样好。不知我能不能学着信了它？人总得有个宗教。至少，我能从它的道理里得着好处。他是那位神，那我算什么？白贝壳女人（White Shell Woman）？兴许是变化女人（Changing Woman）吧。这尊战神是我造下的，就得由我来雕琢，由我来引路。我决计不能让他从我手里走脱。坏事情一件也不许发生；我决计不能出半点差错。我不是纳瓦霍人，也不是美国人，可纳瓦霍人是我的族人。

### II / II

===Original===
The sun was low; the shadow sides of cliffs became deep pools of violet seeping out across the sand. She rose and drew her blanket about her, composing herself for contacts with intrusive humanity. Down the steep trail her little, moccasined feet sought the sure footholds lightly. Above her feet, the clumsy deerskin leggings were thick; the heavy blanket gave a quaint stiffness to her body. Wrapped so, her feet and head and slender hands alone showing, she became pathetically small, a wisp; but her thoughtful eyes were not pathetic.

===Chinese===
太阳低了；岩壁背阴的那一面成了一汪汪紫色的深潭，慢慢渗到沙地上去。她站起身，把毯子往身上一裹，定住神，预备去应付闯上来的世人。陡峭的小径上，她那双小小的、裹着鹿皮鞋的脚，轻轻地找着一个个稳当的落脚处。脚踝往上，是厚墩墩、笨乎乎的鹿皮绑腿；沉重的毯子给她的身子添了一种古怪的僵直。这样裹着，只露出一双脚、一颗头和一双细长的手，她显得小得可怜，像一茎细草；可她那双若有所思的眼睛，一点不可怜。

===Original===
The American guide hailed her as she passed his camp, using her school name, “Hi, there, Lily!” She dismissed him with a measuring glance that made his backbone feel cool.

===Chinese===
她打那美国向导的营帐边经过，向导用她在学校里的名字招呼她：“嗨，莉莉（Lily）！”她掂量似的扫了他一眼，便把他打发了，那一眼看得他脊背发凉。

===Original===
“God-damnedest la-dee-dah squaw I ever run acrost!”

===Chinese===
“真他娘的见鬼，这么个拿腔拿调的婆娘，老子跑了这些年还是头一回撞上！”

===Original===
She had little appetite, but camped with a group of distant relatives all too ready to look askance at her, she took pains to do the normal, which was to sup well. She helped with the cooking, dipped into the pot of mutton, drank coffee, then rolled a cigarette. The Indians joked and laughed without reducing the speed of their eating. Chunks of meat and bits of squash were scooped, dripping, from the pot, to be compounded with bread into appalling, mouth-filling tidbits. Three coffee cups and a Hopi bowl served for all to drink in turn; a large spoon was purely a cooking implement. They sprawled on a half-circle of sheepskins within the open brush shelter, facing the fire, chattering and joking. Still in holiday mood, they heaped the blaze high, lighting up the circle and throwing lights that were ruddy, soft shadows on the bushes roundabout.

===Chinese===
她没什么胃口，可如今同一群远亲扎营在一处，那些人正巴不得拿白眼看她，她偏要费心做出正常的样子来，也就是好好吃这顿晚饭。她帮着烧饭，从羊肉锅里捞着吃，喝了咖啡，然后卷了一支烟。印第安人们说说笑笑，嘴里吃的速度却丝毫不减。一块块肉、一牙牙南瓜从锅里捞出来，汤汁淋漓，同面包裹成吓人的、一口塞满腮帮的吃食。三只茶缸和一只霍皮（Hopi）碗，供全伙人轮着喝；一把大勺子，纯粹是烧饭的家什。他们在敞着口的柴枝棚里，摊开身子躺在围成半圈的羊皮上，脸朝着火，闲磕牙打趣。众人还在节庆的兴头上，把火堆挑得高高的，照亮了这一圈人，把红亮的光、柔柔的影子投在四周的灌木丛上。

===Original===
Some of them prepared to sleep. Visitors dropped in; more coffee was made. Slim Girl drew apart, into the darkness, and rolled up. Over there, a chink of light showed in the blanketed door of a big, earth-covered winter hogan. Singing came out of it, rollicking, running songs. They were gambling there; Laughing Boy would soon be penniless. She smiled at the thought of him and his stubbornness. The bushes rustled faintly. From where she lay, she could see a clump of yucca in a fixed pattern against the sky. The voices by the fire became distant. The stars stooped near.

===Chinese===
有些人张罗着睡下。客人陆陆续续地来；又煮上了咖啡。瘦姑娘独自退开去，退进黑暗里，蜷起身子躺下。那边，一座覆土过冬的大霍根屋，毯子掩着的门口漏出一线灯光。里头传出歌声，是欢闹的、连跑带颠的歌子。他们在那儿赌钱呢；笑娃眼看就要输得一个子儿不剩了。想到他，想到他那股倔劲儿，她笑了。灌木丛沙沙地轻响。从她躺的地方望出去，能看见一小丛丝兰衬着夜空，纹丝不动地定在那里。火边的人声远下去了。星星低低地俯下来。

===Original===
“In beauty it is begun. In beauty it is begun. Thanks.”

===Chinese===
“在美之中开启。在美之中开启。感谢。”

## Chapter VI / 第六章

### I / I

===Original===
At first light, before dawn, the desert is intimate, and each man feels the presence of others as an intrusion. Blinding colour has not supplanted soft greys, uncertain forms; cliffs harsh by daylight, and thunderous-walled canyons loom soft with wells of coolness. The east is white﻿—mother-of-pearl﻿—the world is secret to each one’s self.

===Chinese===
天光初透、黎明未到的时候，沙漠是亲切的，人人都觉得旁人的在场是一种搅扰。炫目的色彩还没有排挤掉柔和的灰色和朦胧的形影；白日里嶙峋刺目的悬崖、四壁回声如雷的峡谷，此刻都隐现得温柔，蓄着一口口清凉。东方一片白——珍珠母的白——这世界，人人都只把它守成自己的秘密。

===Original===
Slim Girl, sitting apart, watching the slow increase of visible forms, looked towards the gambling hogan. She heard them announce sunrise with the Magpie Song, and, after the last ringing “It dawns, it dawns,” saw the straight dark forms coming out, moving away; some alone, some together talking, their voices intruding upon the hushed world.

===Chinese===
瘦姑娘（Slim Girl）独自坐在一旁，看着眼前能辨出的形影一点点多起来，朝那座通宵赌钱的霍根屋望去。她听见他们唱起喜鹊之歌（Magpie Song）报晓，待到最后一声清亮的“天亮了，天亮了”唱过，只见一条条挺直的黑影从屋里走出来，四散而去；有的独自一个，有的结伴说话，人声搅扰了这静谧的世界。

===Original===
She rose to intercept the path of one. He stood before her, answering her smile with a smile, tall and straight and shameless as he let his blanket fall to show﻿—no silver belt, no jewelry, only the lucky bow-guard on his left wrist.

===Chinese===
她起身拦住其中一个人的去路。他站到她跟前，用微笑回答她的微笑，又高又直，一脸没羞没臊的坦然，任毯子从身上滑落，露出——没有银腰带，没有饰物，只有左腕上那只吉利的弓护腕。

===Original===
“Take this bow-guard, now, to keep. By first cold moon you will hear from me again. My uncles will look for you, or I shall.”

===Chinese===
“这只弓护腕，你先拿去替我收着。到头一个寒月，你自会再听到我的音信。我的舅舅们会来寻你，要不然我自己来。”

===Original===
“So they won everything?”

===Chinese===
“这么说，他们把什么都赢光了？”

===Original===
“Everything.”

===Chinese===
“全赢了。”

===Original===
“Horse, belt, money?”

===Chinese===
“马、腰带、钱？”

===Original===
“Horse, belt, money. I go to T’o Tlakai to make silver.”

===Chinese===
“马、腰带、钱。我要去特奥·特莱凯（T’o Tlakai）打银子。”

===Original===
“You were foolish.”

===Chinese===
“你犯傻了。”

===Original===
“What else could I have done? And it was fine play! I was happy. We sang, all night we sang. We made new songs about ourselves. Now I must work.”

===Chinese===
“不这样还能怎样？再说，赌得多痛快！我快活得很。我们唱了，唱了整整一夜。我们编了唱自己的新歌。如今我得干活了。”

===Original===
She was prepared for this. “You are not a man yet, I think.”

===Chinese===
这话她早有备。“我看，你还算不得一个男子汉。”

===Original===
That gave him a start. “Why do you say that? That is not a good thing to say.” Losing the goods meant little, but if losing them meant losing her, the world was a loom of lies.

===Chinese===
这话叫他一个激灵。“你为什么这样说？这可不是一句好话。”输掉那些东西算不得什么，可要是输掉它们就等于输掉她，那这世界便成了一架满织谎话的机子。

===Original===
“You are like a child. You are happy now, so you forget what you wanted before.”

===Chinese===
“你像个孩子。眼下高兴了，就把先头心里想要的忘了。”

===Original===
“What thing?”

===Chinese===
“什么东西？”

===Original===
“Where is the love-song now? ‘Now my horse will not go / From your valley, *a-a-a*,’ ” she sang.

===Chinese===
“那支情歌呢？这会儿上哪儿去了？‘如今我的马不肯走 / 不肯离开你的山谷，啊-啊-啊，’”她唱道。

===Original===
“I tell you, I have nothing now. I have not even a horse. Nothing.” He struck his right hand across his left in emphatic gesture.

===Chinese===
“我告诉你，我如今一无所有了。连一匹马都没有。什么都没有。”他右手啪地拍过左手，加重着这句话。

===Original===
“I tell you, you do not have to pay for me. I have no mother. If you come, you must come now.”

===Chinese===
“我也告诉你，你不用为我破费什么。我没有母亲。你要来，就得现在来。”

===Original===
“I am a man. I cannot come to you with nothing. I cannot let you buy me.”

===Chinese===
“我是个男子汉。我不能空着两手到你跟前来。我不能让你把我买下。”

===Original===
“Look at me.” She shook herself so that her jewelry clanked. He heard the sound, but his eyes were upon hers. The east was banded with orange, red, and purple. “Look at me.” Her eyes were long and narrow, and deep enough to absorb a man. “I am rich. I shall give you silver and turquoise to work, horses to breed, till you too are rich. Must I tell you twice?” Her eyes were more beautiful than springs among the rocks. “You have spoken to your uncle; you know what he said. Your mother will give you no sheep, no horses for me. If you want to come with me, come now. I cannot wait until first cold moon. You cannot cache me in a tree until you are ready for me. You have your manhood and your weapons; if you are not good enough with them, nothing can make you good enough. Come now.”

===Chinese===
“看着我。”她晃了晃身子，好让身上的饰物叮当作响。他听见了那声响，眼睛却盯在她眼睛上。东方已经横上了一道道橙、红、紫的彩带。“看着我。”她的眼睛细长，深得足以把一个男人整个吸进去。“我有钱。我要给你银子和绿松石供你打制，给你马群供你繁育，直到你也富起来。还要我说第二遍吗？”她的眼睛比岩石间的泉水还美。“你跟你舅舅说过了；他说了什么，你知道。你母亲不会为了我分给你羊、分给你马。你要想跟我走，现在就走。我等不到头一个寒月。你不能把我像存下的物件似的搁在树杈上，等你什么时候预备好了再来取。你有你的男子气概，有你手里的家伙；要是你使它们使得还不够好，旁的什么都帮不了你。现在就来。”

===Original===
He was a long time answering, searching and searching her eyes. At last, “It is good. Get your horse.”

===Chinese===
他隔了好半天才作答，一遍又一遍地在她眼睛里搜寻。末了说：“好吧。去牵你的马。”

===Original===
She thought she had stood for twenty years with a rifle pointed at her breast. Her face did not change; she walked away slowly. He saw that full day lay golden along the tops of the cliffs, and the sky was brilliant; from the camps he heard the noise of departure, bustle and low voices and laughter that to an American would have seemed furtive.

===Chinese===
她只觉得自己胸口抵着一杆步枪，足足站了二十年。她脸上纹丝没变，慢慢走开了。他看见大亮的天光金灿灿地铺在崖顶上，天空明净耀眼；营地里传来启程的响动，忙乱声、低语声、笑声——这些声响要叫美国人听去，准显得偷偷摸摸。

===Original===
I am like Natinesthani and the magician’s daughter, he thought, but I have no sacred tobacco. I have just myself and my bow. I wonder what medicine will she give me? I shall make a bracelet that is like her walking; she is silver strong as iron. When I have horses again, we must both come back to T’o Tlakai. There is good water in Tseya Kien canyon, that is the place for our hogan.

===Chinese===
我就像纳蒂内斯塔尼（Natinesthani）和那巫师的女儿，他想，只是我没有神烟。我有的只是我自己和我的弓。不知道她会给我什么药？我要打一只手镯，打得像她走路的样子；她是银子，却硬得像铁。等我再有了马，我们俩一定要回特奥·特莱凯去。特塞亚基恩峡谷（Tseya Kien）里有好水，我们的霍根屋就该安在那儿。

===Original===
He rolled a cigarette. The freshness would leave the air soon. Already he felt tired.

===Chinese===
他卷了一支烟。空气里的清爽快要散了。他已经觉得乏了。

===Original===
She rode as well as she danced or walked. Her pinto pony tossed its head, working against her light touch on the reins, ringing the tinklers on its bridle. That girl on that horse﻿—*ei-yei!* Reaching him, she smiled, and he forgot his fatigue. He walked tall and proud beside her, one hand on her stirrup, not caring who might see.

===Chinese===
她骑马，跟她跳舞、走路一样好。她那匹花斑小马甩着头，跟她手上轻轻一带的缰绳较劲，笼头上的小铃铛摇得叮零作响。那姑娘骑在那马上——咿-耶！到了他跟前，她一笑，他便忘了疲倦。他高高的、傲然地走在她旁边，一只手搭在她的马镫上，谁看见不看见，全不在乎。

### II / II

===Original===
Red Man sought out Wounded Face where he stood at his pony’s head, talking to Killed a Navajo. Despite a certain jauntiness, he did not look like a gambler who had just won a small fortune. He addressed the older man rather abruptly,

===Chinese===
红汉子（Red Man）寻到疤脸（Wounded Face）跟前——他正站在自己小马的头旁，同杀纳瓦霍人（Killed a Navajo）说话。红汉子尽管还带着几分快活劲儿，看上去却不像个刚赢下一小注钱财的赌客。他冲这位年长者开口，说得相当突兀——

===Original===
“Grandfather!”

===Chinese===
“老爷爷！”

===Original===
“Yes?”

===Chinese===
“什么事？”

===Original===
“Are you not the uncle of that man who won the horse-race, the one from T’o Tlakai?”

===Chinese===
“您不是赢了赛马的那个人的舅舅吗，就是特奥·特莱凯来的那一位？”

===Original===
“I am. What is it?”

===Chinese===
“我是。怎么了？”

===Original===
Red Man had meant to go slow, but his words were jumping out on him. “Did he speak to you? Has he told you what he planned to do?”

===Chinese===
红汉子本打算话说慢些，话却一句句自个儿往外蹦。“他跟您说了吗？他把打算做的事告诉您了没有？”

===Original===
Wounded Face and his friend suddenly lost all expression; they became wooden.

===Chinese===
疤脸和他的朋友脸上的神情霎时褪了个干净，成了两截木头。

===Original===
“I do not know what you mean. We talked together yesterday. What is in your mind?”

===Chinese===
“我不明白你的意思。昨天我们在一起聊过天。你心里在想什么？”

===Original===
“He has gone to Chiziai. He has gone﻿—he has gone﻿—he has not gone alone.”

===Chinese===
“他到奇扎伊（Chiziai）去了。他去了——他去了——他不是一个人去的。”

===Original===
“He went with the woman who was stopped from dancing?”

===Chinese===
“是同那个被拦下来不许跳舞的女人一起走的？”

===Original===
“Yes.”

===Chinese===
“是。”

===Original===
“Well?”

===Chinese===
“然后呢？”

===Original===
“Do you not know about her?”

===Chinese===
“她的底细，您不知道？”

===Original===
“I have heard a little talk; I do not know anything. She is rich; perhaps it is a good marriage, I think.”

===Chinese===
“闲话听过一点儿；我什么也不知道。她有钱；兴许是门好亲事，我想。”

===Original===
Red Man saw that Wounded Face very much wanted firsthand information. “I live not far from Chiziai. I know, not just talk. She lives alone, she does no work, she is rich. The Americans make her rich, for badness. She is two faces and two tongues. You see her clothes and her skin, and hear her voice, but all the rest inside is American badness. I know. Hear me, I know.”

===Chinese===
红汉子看出疤脸极想要个一手的底细。“我住在离奇扎伊不远的地方。我知道的是实情，不光是闲话。她一个人过活，什么活儿也不干，却有钱。是美国人让她发了财，靠的是做下作的营生。她有两张脸、两条舌头。你看得到她的衣裳、她的皮肤，听得到她的声音，可她里头其余的一切，全是美国式的坏。我知道。你听着，我知道。”

===Original===
He had managed to be gay all night; he had been the cheerfullest of all the gamblers, the readiest singer, the pleasantest loser. Now suddenly it all went back on him. He moved his lips, and found he did not dare speak. He raised his hand to his mouth with two fingers outstretched, and thrust it forward﻿—two-tongued. He struck his heart, then raised his fist before his face and brought it down rapidly﻿—heart that kills with a knife. He struck his heart again, then brought his right fist down on his left hand﻿—like a stone﻿—making the gesture with all his force. He repeated how she made her living; in sign talk it was frightfully graphic and coarse.

===Chinese===
他撑着快活了一整夜；赌客里头数他兴致最高，数他张口就能唱，输了钱也数他输得最漂亮。此刻这一切忽然全都翻回来落在他身上。他动了动嘴唇，发觉自己不敢说出口。他抬起一只手凑到嘴边，伸出两根指头，猛地朝前一戳——两条舌头。他捶了捶自己的心口，随后举起拳头在脸前一晃，飞快劈下——用刀子捅人心口。他又捶了捶心口，右拳随即重重砸落在左手掌上——像一块石头——使出全身的力气打出这个手势。他把她营生的来路一一比划了出来；落在手语里，那真是又露骨又粗鄙，骇人得很。

===Original===
“That is enough, Grandfather,” Wounded Face said. “You did well to tell me.”

===Chinese===
“够了，老爷爷，”疤脸说。“你来告诉我，做得对。”

===Original===
Red Man departed.

===Chinese===
红汉子走开了。

===Original===
“Shall we ride after him?” Killed a Navajo asked.

===Chinese===
“咱们要不要骑马追他去？”杀纳瓦霍人问。

===Original===
“No. That is what that young man wants us to do, I think. You saw him, how moved he was. We have heard something of what he says, but still, he had reason to lie. Besides, it would be no use. He is like me, he is like his mother, and his father. You know them. When it is something serious he makes up his mind; you cannot move him unless you can convince him. I have six nephews, he is the best of them.” Wounded Face stood with his hand on his saddle, staring at the stirrup. “Well, we can only wait. Do not speak of it, my friend.”

===Chinese===
“不追。咱们追上去，倒正中了那年轻人下怀，我想。你看见他的样子了，动了多大的气。他说的话，咱们听着了一些，可话说回来，他有撒谎的由头。再说，追也没用。他像我，像他母亲，也像他父亲。他们是什么样的人，你清楚。碰上正经大事，他一旦拿定主意，你就挪他不动，除非你能把他说服。我六个外甥，数他最出色。”疤脸一只手搭在鞍子上站着，眼睛盯着马镫。“唉，咱们只能等。这话别往外说，我的朋友。”

===Original===
“I hear you.”

===Chinese===
“我听到了。”

===Original===
He mounted swiftly, and rode off at a trot.

===Chinese===
他利落地上马，一路小跑着去了。

### III / III

===Original===
It grew hot when the sun was halfway up. Laughing Boy’s last sleep seemed years ago. From time to time he looked at her as one might drink at a spring, and her occasional speech was like rain falling. She rode in triumph.

===Chinese===
日头升到一半高的时候，天热起来了。笑娃（Laughing Boy）上一次睡觉，恍如隔了几年。他不时望她一眼，像人在泉边饮水，她偶尔说出一句话，就像落下的雨。她骑在马上，一派得胜的模样。

===Original===
Abruptly he stopped, gazing first at the trail, then over to the right, while with a hand on the bridle he stopped her horse. He said in a sure voice, “Get off your horse.”

===Chinese===
他猛地停住，先盯着路面看，又往右边望去，一只手按着笼头，把她的马也带停了。他用笃定的声音说：“下马。”

===Original===
She did not quite know why she obeyed so immediately. He took off saddle and bridle, tied a thong about the animal’s lower jaw, then stood for an instant, one hand on the withers, head raised high. She saw his lips moving, and was afraid of his intent face and a hard, excited look about his mouth. With a quick gesture he strung his bow, and before she could speak to him, mounted and was off, galloping. There was nothing for it but to wonder and wait.

===Chinese===
她不大明白，自己为什么立刻就依了。他卸下鞍子和笼头，拿一条皮绳拴住马的下颚，随后伫立了一瞬，一只手搭在马鬐甲上，头高高扬起。她看见他的嘴唇在翕动，叫他那专注的神情和嘴角一股又硬又兴奋的样貌骇住了。他一个利落的手势给弓上了弦，还没容她开口，人已经翻身上马，飞驰而去。她毫无办法，只有纳闷，只有等。

===Original===
She knew by the sun that he had not been gone over half an hour, but it seemed more than she could stand to wait longer. Her feelings alarmed her; was she falling in love? She saw him rounding a butte, trotting, driving two more ponies ahead of him. This, she thought, was madness. Truly, she must take him in hand. She rose as he drew near.

===Chinese===
她凭日头知道他走了还不到半个钟头，可再等下去，她觉得自己就受不住了。这份心事叫她自己吃惊：她莫不是在坠入情网？她望见他绕过一座孤丘，小跑着，前头还赶着两匹马。这简直是疯了，她想。说真的，她得把他拿在手里管起来。他走近时，她站起身。

===Original===
“What have you done? American Chief will put you in jail.”

===Chinese===
“你干了什么呀？美国酋长（American Chief）会把你抓去坐牢的。”

===Original===
“No; it is all right. That man”﻿—he gestured toward the butte﻿—“I did not hurt him much; besides, he is a Pah-Ute. He took this horse from my brother last year. He is bad, that one. He lives up beyond Oljeto. I saw him at the dance. Now I have something, to come with you. He was a bad shot, look.”

===Chinese===
“不怕；没事的。那个人”——他朝那座孤丘一指——“我没怎么伤着他；再说，他是个派尤特人（Pah-Ute）。去年他从我兄弟手里抢走了这匹马。那家伙不是个好东西。他住在奥尔赫托（Oljeto）再往上去的地方。我在舞会上见过他。这下我手里总算有点东西，好来投奔你了。他那枪法不济，你瞧。”

===Original===
He showed her proudly a long, shallow scratch on his forearm.

===Chinese===
他得意地把前臂上一道又长又浅的划伤给她看。

===Original===
“And the belt?” She pointed to the silver at his waist.

===Chinese===
“那腰带呢？”她指了指他腰间的银腰带。

===Original===
“I do not know from whom he stole that. It is a pretty good belt.”

===Chinese===
“那是他从谁手里偷来的，我不知道。倒真是条不错的腰带。”

===Original===
They laughed together.

===Chinese===
两人一齐笑了。

===Original===
Immensely alone in that white stretch of adobe desert, they rode side by side, like two men, like friends. It seemed to Laughing Boy that she promised freedom and astonishing companionship; her small mannerisms, her casual remarks, were unconventional without consciousness; it was good. The ponies stepped out well despite the heat, the bridle jingled, the spare horse, with high head, pranced alongside, obedient to the rope. He sat slackly in the saddle, leaning back, flicking his pony’s quarters in rhythm to his song.

===Chinese===
在那片白茫茫的泥漠上，天大地大只他们两个，他们并辔而行，像两个男人，像两个朋友。在笑娃看来，她许诺给他自由，还有一份叫人惊叹的相与；她那些小小的做派、随口的话，处处不循规矩，自己却浑然不觉；这很好。天气虽热，两匹小马步子迈得欢，笼头叮当作响，那匹备换的马昂着头，在旁边踏着碎步，被绳子牵得服服帖帖。他松松地坐在鞍上，身子后仰，合着他歌子的节拍，轻轻掸着小马的后胯。

===Original===
They stopped seldom, ate little, and rode fast. It was hard on her; she was not accustomed to missing meals and sleeping where night happened to catch her, but she knew better than to complain. His easy toughness, his enjoyment of momentary comfort, were a compensation for her, and at night, camped beside a tiny waterhole, she listened to his singing.

===Chinese===
他们难得停，吃得少，赶路赶得急。这可苦了她；她不惯错过的饭食，也不惯夜里落到哪儿就睡在哪儿，可她知道犯不上抱怨。他那份吃得了苦的皮实劲儿、那份有片刻舒服便受用片刻的脾性，对她算是一种补偿；夜里，在一小洼水泉旁边扎了营，她听他唱歌。

===Original===
She was tired and stiff. Already she had been alarmed, worried, tired, and hungry for this man. With a sudden fear, as she looked at him across the fire, she realized that she loved him. She had started something she could not stop, then. Well, it was all right, it was good. If only he hadn’t gone off after that Pah-Ute, it wouldn’t have happened; it was that waiting without understanding; it was that imperious warrior who gave her orders and was suddenly stronger than she, and apart from her. That had done it. While he sang, she looked at his hands locked across his knees, at the bow-guard on his left wrist. When he loosed his shaft, the bowstring had snapped down across the leather on the inside; towards her he turned the lovingly worked silver on the back of it. The shaft had gone true, into the shoulder, between the neck and the butt of the aimed rifle. She shivered.

===Chinese===
她又累又僵。为了这个男人，她已经受过惊、担过忧、挨过累、忍过饥。隔着火堆望他的时候，一阵突如其来的恐惧攫住了她，她明白自己爱上他了。这下，她是一件由不得自己收手的事起了头。也好，没什么不好，这是好事。倘若他没有去追那个派尤特人，这事就不会发生；是那种不明就里的干等；是那个专断的武士，对她发号施令，忽然间比她强，又忽然间离她远远的。是这些把事情做成的。他唱着歌，她望着他交扣在膝头的双手，望着他左腕上的弓护腕。他放箭的那一下，弓弦啪地砸在内侧的皮面上；朝着她的一面，他转过那精心打制的银面。那一箭去得正，扎进肩窝，正在脖颈和那人端平瞄准的枪托之间。她打了个寒噤。

===Original===
He stopped singing. She rose and sat down again close beside him, and waited. He made no move. She knew now that these next few days when she would be with him alone were desperately important to her, but she was meeting with a restraint blended of tribal custom and ignorance for which her knowledge of the American’s world had not prepared her. It was beyond all other necessity to possess him fully now while the trail was single and straight, but he was a religious man, schooled to obedience of absolute conventions.

===Chinese===
他停了歌。她起身，又紧挨着他身边坐下，等着。他没有一点动静。她如今明白了，往后这几日与他单独相守，于她是性命交关的事；可她碰上的是一堵拘谨的墙，由部族的规矩和蒙昧混和砌成，她在美国人世界里攒下的那点见识，全没教她应付过这个。趁这条路还只是一条直路，此刻就该把他整个占有，这比什么都要紧；可他是个敬神的人，受的调教便是对绝对规矩的俯首听从。

===Original===
She thought. He was unused to her originality; she delighted him, but she came close at times to alarming him. She must go slow in all things. She would wait. The effort her decision cost her was so great that it frightened her. Perhaps, she told herself, it is a good thing to have to wait. I love him, but I must remain mistress of myself and him. This is good for me.

===Chinese===
她思量。他吃不惯她的别出心裁；她叫他欢喜，可有时又险些把他惊着。事事都得放慢来。她决定等。这决断耗得她心力之大，大到叫她自己害怕。兴许，她对自己说，非等不可倒是桩好事。我爱他，可我必须做我自己的主，也做他的主。这于我有好处。

===Original===
She wanted to touch his face with her fingertips, to brush his hair with her lips. When they galloped together and he sang exultantly beside her, she wanted him to swing her to his saddle. There is very little gesture of tenderness in Indian experience, but she thought she saw latent in him the same desires, promising herself days to come when she would teach him many things. She thought to herself, I shall complete him with my knowledge. I shall make a god of him.

===Chinese===
她想用指尖去抚他的脸，用嘴唇去拂他的头发。两人并辔疾驰、他在她身边纵情高歌的时候，她想让他伸手把自己一把揽上他的马鞍。印第安人的日子里，温存的举动本来就少，可她觉得在他身上看见了一样的欲望在潜滋暗长，便预许给自己来日方长，到那时她要教会他许多事情。她心里想：我要用我的见识把他补全。我要把他造就成一个神。

### IV / IV

===Original===
The town of Los Palos shimmered in the heat. A lot of adobe houses and frame shacks pushed carelessly together were beaten down by the sun. Behind them was a strip of irrigated green like a backdrop, alfalfa, corn, beans, cottonwoods, alfalfa, corn, cottonwoods, a mile long and a few hundred yards wide. Rich, deep, cool green was not part of the desert landscape; it was something apart that the sands held prisoner. The mean little town was a parasite on the goodness of the water; here water and earth and man made beauty; there man and mud and boards created squalor.

===Chinese===
洛斯帕洛斯（Los Palos）镇在暑气里蒸腾闪光。一大片胡乱挤作一处的土坯房和木板棚屋，被日头压得矮趴趴的。屋后是一条水浇出来的绿地，像一道舞台布景：苜蓿，玉米，菜豆，三角叶杨；又是苜蓿，玉米，三角叶杨；一英里长，几百码宽。那浓郁、深厚、清凉的绿，本不是沙漠景致里的事物；它是被沙地囚押着的一件异物。这个寒碜的小镇是寄生在水的恩泽上的：这边，水、土和人造出美；那边，人、泥和木板造出腌臜。

===Original===
A few yards of concrete and some blistered paint made a gesture of civic pride at the railroad’s edge. A two-story hotel, compounding Spanish mission with cubism, was a monument of the railroad’s profitable beneficence. From a rise where the trail crossed the railroad track, a little way to the west, it all compounded into a picture; the dejected town with its dominant hotel-station, the green strip behind it, yellow-grey sand, and farther, dancing buttes in the mirage.

===Chinese===
铁路边上，几码长的水泥地和几片起了泡的油漆，算是摆出一点市民的自豪。一座两层旅馆，西班牙传教式掺和着立体派，是铁路一本万利的善举纪念碑。从小道跨过铁道的一处高坡上，略偏西望过去，这一切合成为一幅画：蔫头耷脑的镇子，压镇的旅馆兼车站，镇子背后那条绿地，黄灰色的沙地，再远处，海市蜃楼里翩然舞动的孤丘。

===Original===
Laughing Boy’s attention was divided. “Do these iron paths run all the way to Washindon? That is a beautiful place; there must be much water there. I have never seen so many houses; how many are there? Five hundred? I should like to go there. Are there many trading posts, or just one? Those are rich fields. Can one come here and see the iron-fire-drives?” He silenced himself, ashamed at having shown himself so carried away.

===Chinese===
笑娃的心思分成了两半。“这些铁道一路通到瓦辛顿（Washindon，即华盛顿）吗？那是个顶美的地方；那儿的水必定多得很。我从没见过这么多房子；一共有多少座？五百座？我很想上那儿去一趟。那儿交易站多不多，还是只有一座？那些田真肥。人家能上这儿来看铁火驱车吗？”他自己噤了声，为方才那副忘形的样子害了臊。

===Original===
“Let us not go there now,” she told him quickly; “it is better that we go first to my hogan. The horses are tired.”

===Chinese===
“咱们这会儿先别上那儿去，”她赶忙对他说；“先到我那霍根屋去才好。马都乏了。”

===Original===
“You are right. Are there more than five hundred houses?”

===Chinese===
“你说得对。房子真比五百座还多？”

===Original===
“Yes, a few more. The iron-fire-drives goes by many times a day; it goes that way to Washindon and that way to Wide Water. Anyone may see it. Come now.”

===Chinese===
“是，还多几座。铁火驱车一天要打这儿过好多趟；往那头去是瓦辛顿，往这头去是宽水（Wide Water）。谁想看都能看。来吧。”

===Original===
They gave the town a wide berth, trotting east past the end of the irrigated land along a trail between two buttes. About three miles farther on, where the clay walls widened again to face the southern desert, an adobe shack stood in the shadow of one wall. Behind it a tiny spring leaked out. Here they dismounted.

===Chinese===
他们远远避开镇子，策着小跑往东，走过灌溉地的尽头，沿一条夹在两座孤丘之间的小道前行。再往前约莫三英里，泥壁又豁然张开，面朝南方的大漠，一座土坯棚屋立在其中一面崖壁的荫里。屋后有一眼小泉渗出水来。他们在这里下了马。

===Original===
“But this is not a hogan, it is a house. Did an American make it?”

===Chinese===
“可这不是霍根屋，这是一座房子。是美国人盖的吗？”

===Original===
“No, a Mexican built it. He went away to herd sheep, and I took it.”

===Chinese===
“不是，一个墨西哥人盖的。他赶着羊走了，我把它占了。”

===Original===
He stepped inside. “It does not smell like Mexicans.”

===Chinese===
他走进屋里。“这里头没有墨西哥人的气味。”

===Original===
“I have been here a long time. Yellow Singer made the House Song for me. Is it not good? The door is to the east, like a hogan.”

===Chinese===
“我在这儿住得久了。黄颂唱师（Yellow Singer）给我做过屋歌（House Song）。你说好不好？门朝东开，跟霍根屋一个样。”

===Original===
“Yes, it is good. It is better than a hogan, I think; it is bigger and the rain will not come through. It will be good summer and winter.” He hobbled the horses. “There is not much grass by that spring; we shall have to find pasture.”

===Chinese===
“是，好。我看比霍根屋还强；它宽敞，雨水也漏不进来。夏天冬天都好住。”他给几匹马上了绊。“那泉边草不多；咱们得另找牧场去。”

===Original===
“There is a little pasture just down there you can use. You must not let the horses run all over the place; this is American country. The Navajo country begins across the railroad track. There is good pasture just this side of Natahnetinn Mesa, enough for many horses. You must keep them up there.”

===Chinese===
“就在下头那儿有一小块牧场，你尽可以用。可不能由着马满地乱跑；这儿是美国人的地界。纳瓦霍的地界，要到铁道那一边才起头。纳塔内廷台地（Natahnetinn Mesa）这一边就有好牧场，养多少匹马都够。你得把马撂在上头。”

===Original===
She lit a fire in front of the house.

===Chinese===
她在屋子前头生起了火。

===Original===
“You have no loom. There is no sheep-pen.”

===Chinese===
“你没有织机。也没有羊圈。”

===Original===
“I have been alone. I have had no one to weave for, and no sheep.”

===Chinese===
“我一直是一个人过。没有要我织布的人，也没有羊。”

===Original===
“How do you live?”

===Chinese===
“那你怎么过活？”

===Original===
She was laying the big logs over the first flame.

===Chinese===
她正把大根的柴禾架到初起的火苗上。

===Original===
“I work a little bit, now and then, for the missionary’s wife in the town. She is a good woman. Now I am going to set up a loom, and you shall have a forge.”

===Chinese===
“我隔三差五给镇上传教士的太太打点儿零工。她是个好女人。往后我要支起一架织机，再给你起一座锻炉。”

===Original===
He thought that something was wrong. Her face was too blank. “Not all missionaries are good, they say. There used to be a bad one at T’o Nanasdési, they say.”

===Chinese===
他觉得有点儿不对劲。她的脸太平淡了。“传教士也不是个个都好，他们说。从前托纳纳斯代西（T’o Nanasdési）就有一个坏的，他们说。”

===Original===
“No, not all of them are good; but this one is.” She spoke musingly. “His wife pays me much money. She is not strong; I am.”

===Chinese===
“是，不是个个都好；可这一位是好的。”她说得出了神。“他太太给我好些钱。她身子骨不强；我强。”

===Original===
Her strange, pensive smile troubled him. He thought how beautiful she was. He thought again of the magician’s daughter. He did not care what bad magic she might do to him; just she was worth all other things.

===Chinese===
她那古怪而若有所思的笑叫他心乱。他想她有多美。他又想起那巫师的女儿。哪怕她要对他使什么邪法，他都不在乎；单是她这个人，就抵得过世上其余的一切。

===Original===
Sprawled out on his saddle-blanket, he watched as she brought food from the house and began to prepare it. Her movements were like grass in the wind. He eyed a banquet of luxury﻿—canned goods, tomatoes, fruit.

===Chinese===
他摊手摊脚躺在鞍毯上，看她从屋里搬出吃食来，动手拾掇。她的动作像风里的草。他眼睁睁望着一场豪奢的席面——罐头，西红柿，水果。

===Original===
“Perhaps when we go into the town tomorrow we can buy some candy.”

===Chinese===
“兴许明儿进镇的时候，咱们能买些糖吃。”

===Original===
She thought, he must be kept away from town. I must think of something. “I have a little here.”

===Chinese===
她心里想：得让他离镇子远远的。我得想个法子。“我这儿有一点儿。”

===Original===
“Sticks with stripes on them?”

===Chinese===
“带条纹的糖棍？”

===Original===
“Yes.”

===Chinese===
“是。”

===Original===
He sighed luxuriously. The food on the fire smelt good. It was cool. With a couple of ditches one could make a good cornfield by that spring, and plant peaches, perhaps. If they were to have food like this all the time﻿—It was important to find that pasture for the horses, he must tend to it tomorrow. The town could wait. A swift movement caught his eye, lifting the coffeepot aside. *Ei!* she was beautiful.

===Chinese===
他美滋滋地长叹了一声。火上的饭食香味很好。天凉快下来。在那泉边挖上两道沟，就能开出一片好玉米地，兴许还能栽上桃树。要是往后天天都有这样的吃食——给马找那片牧场是件要紧事，明天他就得去办。镇子不急，可以等等。一个轻快的动作闯进他眼里，她正把咖啡壶从火上挪开。咿！她真美。

### V / V

===Original===
They talked as they ate, lounging, while night filled the valley.

===Chinese===
他们懒洋洋地边吃边说，夜色一寸寸灌满了山谷。

===Original===
“Do you speak American then?” Laughing Boy asked. “Is it hard to learn?”

===Chinese===
“这么说，你会说美国话？”笑娃问。“难学不难？”

===Original===
“It is not hard; we had to learn it. They put me in a room with a Ute girl and a Moqui and a Comanche; all we could do was learn English. Sometimes some Navajo girls sneaked out and talked together, but not often. They did not want us to be Indians.” She rested on her elbow, staring into the fire. “They wanted us to be ashamed of being Indians. They wanted us to forget our mothers and fathers.”

===Chinese===
“不难；我们是非学不可。他们把我同一个尤特族（Ute）姑娘、一个莫基人、一个科曼奇人（Comanche）关在一间屋里；我们所能做的，就只有学英语。有时候，几个纳瓦霍姑娘偷偷溜出去，聚在一处说说话，可不常有。他们不打算让我们做印第安人。”她支着手肘，盯着火堆出神。“他们要我们以做印第安人为耻。要我们忘掉自己的父亲母亲。”

===Original===
“That is a bad thing. Why did they do that?”

===Chinese===
“那是桩坏事。他们为什么要那样做？”

===Original===
“Do not talk about it. I do not want to think about those things.”

===Chinese===
“别说了。我不愿意去想那些事。”

===Original===
When she had put away the dishes, as they lit their cigarettes she said, “If ever they come to take a child of ours to school, kill her.”

===Chinese===
她把碗碟收拾停当，两人点烟的时候，她说：“往后他们要是来把咱们的孩子带去上学，你就把她杀了。”

===Original===
“Is it like that?”

===Chinese===
“当真是那样的？”

===Original===
“Yes.”

===Chinese===
“是。”

===Original===
“I hear you.”

===Chinese===
“我听到了。”

===Original===
They lay side by side against the wall of the house, watching the fire. Her shoulder moved closer to him. He said,

===Chinese===
两人并排倚着屋墙躺下，望着火堆。她的肩膀朝他这边挪近了些。他说，

===Original===
“Tell me your true name.”

===Chinese===
“把你的真名告诉我。”

===Original===
“My name is Came With War. What is yours?”

===Chinese===
“我的名字叫携战而来（Came With War）。你的呢？”

===Original===
“My parents named me Sings Before Spears. It is a good name. Yours is good.”

===Chinese===
“我父母给我起的名字，是矛前放歌（Sings Before Spears）。是个好名字。你的也好。”

===Original===
“Why do they always give women names about war?”

===Chinese===
“为什么给女人起的名字，总绕着打仗转？”

===Original===
“They have always done it. It brings good fortune to the whole People, I think.”

===Chinese===
“历来都是这么办的。我想，这会给整个迪内带来福气。”

===Original===
She moved so that she touched him. Sings Before Spears!

===Chinese===
她挪了挪身子，贴上了他。矛前放歌！

===Original===
He asked her, “Have you any relatives here? Someone must get a singer to make the prayers over us. There are the four days after that to wait; that is a long time. Let us have them end soon.”

===Chinese===
他问她：“你在这儿有亲戚吗？总得有人去请一位颂唱师来，替咱们行祈祷。过后还有那四天要等；日子怪长的。让这四天早些完了吧。”

===Original===
She caught her breath and looked at him despairingly. He felt a wind blow between them while he met her eyes, a hollowness behind his heart. He clenched his left hand against his side, repeating slowly,

===Chinese===
她倒吸一口气，绝望地望着他。他与她的目光相接，只觉一阵风从两人中间吹过，心口后头空落落的。他把左手攥紧，抵在腰侧，缓缓地重复道，

===Original===
“Four days is a long time to wait,” and then, almost inaudibly, “Oh, beautiful!”

===Chinese===
“四天，可真是一段长长的等待，”随后，声音低得几乎听不见，“哦，多美的人！”

===Original===
She looked away, wanting to laugh, to cry, to swear, and to kick him. He could not know; how could he know? She examined the line of his chin, the set of his lips, so very Indian in their fine chiselling and faint outthrust. Devices ran through her mind. This was a Navajo. This was something her missionaries and teachers never dreamed of. This was part of what she loved. She set her nails into the palms of her hands. Patience.

===Chinese===
她把目光挪开，想笑，想哭，想骂人，还想踹他一脚。他不可能知道；他怎么可能知道？她端详他下巴的线条、唇的样子——雕琢得那样精细，微微前凸，道地的印第安相。种种主意在她脑子里跑马。这是一个纳瓦霍人。这是她的那些传教士和老师们做梦也梦不到的东西。这是她所爱的一部分。她把指甲掐进掌心。忍着。

===Original===
“I have a friend near here who will speak to a Singer tomorrow. He will be here tomorrow night.”

===Chinese===
“我这儿有个朋友，明天会去同一位颂唱师说。明天夜里他就能到。”

===Original===
They smoked again. At last he said, “I do not think I shall sleep in your house now. I think it will be well to sleep up there.”

===Chinese===
两人又抽了一会儿烟。末了他说：“我看，眼下我还不能睡在你屋里。我想还是睡到上头去的好。”

===Original===
“Yes; that will be better.”

===Chinese===
“是；那样更好。”

===Original===
He got his blanket. “I shall forget the trail.”

===Chinese===
他取来毯子。“我怕要把路走忘了。”

===Original===
He loomed above her, in the play of darkness and firelight. She saw all the strength of the Navajo people embodied, against the sky, and she felt ashamed before it.

===Chinese===
他高高立在她上方，黑暗与火光在明灭嬉戏。她看见纳瓦霍人全部的力量化在他身上，衬着天空，她在这力量面前觉出了羞愧。

===Original===
“Four days is not long, Laughing Boy.”

===Chinese===
“四天不长，笑娃。”

## Chapter VII / 第七章

### I / I

===Original===
Early in the morning she got Laughing Boy off with the horses to find pasture. When he was well away, she put on American clothes; high-laced shoes, an outmoded, ill-fitting dress, high to the neck, long-sleeved, dowdy, the inevitable uniform of the school-trained Indian. It was a poor exchange for barbaric velveteen and calico, gay blanket and heavy silver. She had deleted from the formula a number of layers of underclothing; the slack, thin stuff indicated her breasts with curves and shadow; a breath of wind or a quick turn outlined firm stomach, round thigh, and supple movement, very little, but enough.

===Chinese===
一大清早，她便打发笑娃（Laughing Boy）赶着马群找牧草去了。等他走得远远的，她换上美国人的衣裳：高帮系带鞋，一条过时又不合身的连衣裙，高领，长袖，邋遢臃肿——学堂里调教出来的印第安人千篇一律的那身穿戴。拿它换下蛮艳的平绒衫和印花布裙、鲜亮的毯子和沉甸甸的银饰，实在是个亏本的交换。这一套章程里该有的几层内衣，都被她删去了；松弛的薄料子用曲线和阴影点出她的乳胸；一丝风、一个急转身，便勾出紧致的小腹、浑圆的大腿和柔韧的动势——只那么一点点，却也够了。

===Original===
It began to be hot when she reached the wretched ’dobes and stick hovels on the outskirts of Los Palos, among the tin cans and the blowing dust. She stopped by a dome of sticks, old boxes, and bits of canvas.

===Chinese===
走到洛斯帕洛斯（Los Palos）镇边上那片寒碜的土坯房和柴棍窝棚跟前时，天开始热起来，四下里尽是空锡罐和乱卷的尘土。她在一座柴棍、旧箱板和几片破帆布搭成的穹顶棚子旁边停了下来。

===Original===
“*Hé, shichai!*”

===Chinese===
“Hé, shichai!（嘿，老爷爷！）”

===Original===
Yellow Singer crawled out into the sun, blinking red eyes.

===Chinese===
黄颂唱师（Yellow Singer）爬出棚子，来到日头底下，眨巴着一双红眼睛。

===Original===
“Hunh! What is it?”

===Chinese===
“哼！什么事？”

===Original===
His dirty turban had slipped over one ear, his hair was half undone. He sat looking at her uncertainly, his open mouth showing the remnants of yellow teeth. She noticed his toes coming out from the ends of cast-off army boots.

===Chinese===
他那脏头巾滑到了一边耳朵上，头发散了半边。他坐在那儿，拿不准似的打量她，张着的嘴里露出几颗残存的老黄牙。她注意到他的脚趾头正从那双弃置的军靴靴头里钻出来。

===Original===
“Wake up. Were you drunk last night?”

===Chinese===
“醒醒。昨儿夜里喝醉了？”

===Original===
He grinned. “Very drunk. You lend me a dollar, perhaps?”

===Chinese===
他咧嘴一笑。“醉得厉害。你借我一块钱，兴许？”

===Original===
“You keep sober this morning, perhaps I give you a bottle.”

===Chinese===
“你今儿早上安分清醒着，兴许我给你一瓶。”

===Original===
“Hunh?” He focused his attention.

===Chinese===
“哼？”他这才定下神来。

===Original===
“I am going to be married this afternoon. I want you to come and sing over us.”

===Chinese===
“今儿下午我要成亲。我要你来给我们颂唱。”

===Original===
“Coyote!” He swore, and then in English, “God damn! What do *you* want to get married for? What kind of a man have you caught?”

===Chinese===
“郊狼！”他咒骂了一声，接着换用英语：“该死的！你干吗偏要结婚？你逮着的是个什么样的男人？”

===Original===
“You talk too much, I think; it may be bad for you some day. You come this afternoon and sing over us; I shall give you a bottle. Then you keep your mouth closed.”

===Chinese===
“你的话太多了，我想；兴许哪一天于你不利。今儿下午你来给我们颂唱；我给你一瓶。到那时你就把嘴闭严实了。”

===Original===
He read her face, remembering that her grandmother had been an Apache who, in her time, had sat contemplating the antics of men tied on ant-heaps. And he knew this woman pretty well.

===Chinese===
他读着她的脸色，记起她的祖母原是阿帕奇人（Apache），当年也曾坐在一旁，静观捆在蚁堆上的男人们如何蹦跳。而这个女人，他也算知道得透透的了。

===Original===
“Good, Grandmother,” he said respectfully, “we shall come.”

===Chinese===
“好，老奶奶，”他恭恭敬敬地说，“我们一定来。”

===Original===
She left without more words. In the town she had shopping to do﻿—food, a jeweller’s simple tools from a trader, a can of Velvet tobacco and big, brown Romanian cigarette papers. Then she rifted idly to the post-office, sauntering past it in an abstracted manner, not seeing the men who lounged there. One of them immediately walked off in the other direction. She continued down the street, till it became merely a strip more worn than the land on either side of it at the edge of the town, where she entered a small, neat ’dobe house. In a few minutes he followed, closing the door behind him.

===Chinese===
她没再多话便走了。镇上有东西要置办——吃食；从一个商人手里买的首饰匠的简单工具；一罐“天鹅绒”（Velvet）牌烟丝，还有大大的棕色罗马尼亚卷烟纸。随后她懒懒地荡往邮局那边，心不在焉地从门前踱过去，只当没瞧见在那儿闲晃的几个男人。其中一个立刻拔脚，朝相反的方向走了。她顺着街往前走，一直走到镇子口，街不过成了一条比两旁地皮多踩出些路印的土道，她便走进一座小小的、齐整的土坯房。没过几分钟，那人跟了进来，随手带上了门。

===Original===
He wore a clean, checked woollen shirt, the usual big hat, and very worn, well-cut whipcord riding-breeches. He was of good height, light-haired but tanned, with rather sad eyes and a sensitive mouth. Even now, when he was plainly happy, one could see a certain unhappiness about him. He threw his hat on the table, put his hands on his hips, and drew a breath as he looked down on her, smiling.

===Chinese===
他穿着干净的格子呢衬衫，戴一顶寻常的大檐帽，一条剪裁合身却磨得很旧的斜纹骑马裤。他个子很高，头发颜色浅，脸晒得黑黑的，一双眼睛略带忧郁，一张嘴却秀气。即便是眼下这样分明高兴的时候，也看得出他身上带着某种不快活。他把帽子往桌上一扔，两手往腰上一叉，吸了一口气，低头望着她，笑着。

===Original===
“Well, you’re back on time.”

===Chinese===
“嗬，你倒按时回来了。”

===Original===
“Yes, why not? Didn’t I tell you?” She held out her hand to him. Speaking English, she retained the Navajo intonation.

===Chinese===
“是啊，怎么不呢？我不是跟你说过了？”她向他伸出手去。说的是英语，却留着纳瓦霍话的腔调。

===Original===
He sat down on the arm of her chair, and ran the tip of his index finger along the curve of her throat. “That’s a terrible dress, about the worst you’ve got. I’d like you to get some good clothes.”

===Chinese===
他在她椅子的扶手上坐下，用食指指尖顺着她咽喉的曲线划下去。“这件衣裳糟透了，差不多是你最糟的一件。我倒想给你置几件好衣裳。”

===Original===
“How will I do dat? Do you tink I can walk into dat store, dat one down dere, and dey sell me a dress? Will one of dose women, dey make dresses, work for me? You talk silly, you say dat. Maybe I give you my measure, maybe you write to dat place in Chicago, hey?”

===Chinese===
“我怎么弄嘛？你当我能走进那家铺子，下头那一家，他们就肯卖我一条裙子？那些做裙子的女人，肯不肯替我做？你说这个，说的是傻话。兴许我把尺寸给你，兴许你写信到芝加哥那个地方去，嘿？”

===Original===
“Sears Roebuck, my God! Well, it’s not such a bad idea. All right, bring me your measurements.” He leant over to kiss her.

===Chinese===
“西尔斯·罗巴克（Sears Roebuck），我的老天爷！嗯，这主意倒也不坏。行，把你的尺寸拿来给我。”他俯身要吻她。

===Original===
“Don’t start dat now. I got to go back soon now.”

===Chinese===
“这会儿别来这个。我不久就得回去了。”

===Original===
“What the hell?”

===Chinese===
“搞什么鬼？”

===Original===
“My husban’, he makes trouble, dat one. I can’ stay away right now. Soon maybe.”

===Chinese===
“我男人，他找麻烦，那一个。眼下我还走不开。快了，兴许。”

===Original===
He heaved a sigh of exasperation. “Listen! you’ve kept me waiting a week while you went off on that trip. Now you put me off again. You’re always putting me off. I don’t think you’ve got a husband.”

===Chinese===
他懊恼地长叹一声。“听着！你跑那趟远门，晾了我整整一个礼拜。这会儿你又推我。你老是推我。我看你压根儿没有什么男人。”

===Original===
“Yes, I have, an’ he’s a long-hair. You know dat. Don’t I point him out to you one time, dat one? You want him to kill me, hey?”

===Chinese===
“有，我有，他还是个长头发。这个你知道的。我不是有一回指给你看过么，那一个？你想叫他杀了我，嘿？”

===Original===
“Well, all right. Tomorrow, then.”

===Chinese===
“好吧，行。那就明天。”

===Original===
“I can’ do it. It ain’t I don’ want to, George. I can’, dat’s all.” She passed her hand along his cheek, slowly. “You know dat.”

===Chinese===
“明天不成。不是我不肯，乔治（George）。是不成，就这么回事。”她的手缓缓抚过他的面颊。“这你知道。”

===Original===
He kissed her fingertips. “Day after, then, Tuesday. That’s flat, and no two ways about it. I have to go back to the ranch Wednesday; ought to be going back now. You can manage; I think you can manage anything you want. Understand? Tuesday.”

===Chinese===
他吻了吻她的指尖。“那就后天，星期二。就这么说定了，没有二话。星期三我就得回牧场去；本来这会儿就该走的。你有的是办法；我想你想要办成什么都办得成。明白吗？星期二。”

===Original===
She studied him. He was difficult, this man. Now you had him, now you didn’t. There were different kinds of Americans; this one came from the East; he was easy, and he was hard. Well, she could manage almost anything.

===Chinese===
她掂量着他。这男人难弄。一会儿你攥住了他，一会儿又没了。美国人有各式各样；这一个是从东部来的；他好说话，又难说话。哼，她差不多样样都对付得了。

===Original===
“All right, dat will be nice, I tink. I’ll be glad to come den. So you go get me two bottle of wiskey now, to take home, den I fix it. Tuesday.”

===Chinese===
“好吧，那敢情好，我想。到时候我高高兴兴地来。这会儿你就去给我拿两瓶威士忌来，带回家去，我好把事情办妥。星期二。”

===Original===
“That old souse! I wish he’d fall over a cliff and break his damned neck.”

===Chinese===
“那个老酒缸！真恨不得他跌下悬崖，摔断他那条该死的脖子。”

===Original===
She smiled at him. “I wish dat too, sometimes. But he ain’t a bad man, dat one. He has been good for me.”

===Chinese===
她朝他一笑。“有时候我也这么盼。可他不是个坏人，那一个。他一向待我不坏。”

===Original===
“I suppose so.”

===Chinese===
“也是。”

===Original===
“Now get de wiskey.”

===Chinese===
“这就去拿威士忌。”

===Original===
“Kiss me first.”

===Chinese===
“先亲我一口。”


### II / II

===Original===
She thought hard on the way home. The difficulties are beginning already. My path is beset with ambushes. And this is hard. Four days﻿—Monday, Tuesday, Wednesday, Thursday, too late. Oh, no, Laughing Boy, I must bend you. I cannot be robbed of this, I have a right to it. Now I’ve got to manage, I, Came With War. I have earned this, I think. I am afraid of you, Sings Before Spears; I do not like to be afraid. I shall conquer you, or else I’ll herd sheep. I cannot be conquered. God give me help﻿—hmph, that God! Well, I know how. I make my own trail of beauty. I know what to do. I am strong, Laughing Boy, Laughing Boy.

===Chinese===
回家的路上她苦苦地想。难关这就开始了。我的路上处处埋着伏兵。这一桩又硬。四天——星期一，星期二，星期三，星期四，就太迟了。哦，不成，笑娃，我必须把你拗过来。这件事我不能让人夺了去，我对它是有权利的。这下得使手段了，我，携战而来（Came With War）。这是我挣下的，我想。我怕你，矛前放歌（Sings Before Spears）；我不喜欢害怕。我要降服你，不然我就去放羊。我是不能被降服的。神啊，帮帮我——呸，那位神！哼，我知道怎么着。我自己踏出我自己的美之踪迹。我知道该做什么。我是强的，笑娃，笑娃。

===Original===
She was dressed as a Navajo again when he returned from Natahnetinn. He inspected the jeweller’s tools which she spread out for him, praising them, while she set to preparing food.

===Chinese===
他从纳塔内廷（Natahnetinn）回来时，她又换上了纳瓦霍人的装束。她把首饰匠的工具在他跟前摊开，他一件件端详，赞不绝口，她则动手预备吃食。

===Original===
“They were talking about you in the town today,” she told him.

===Chinese===
“今儿镇上有人谈论你。”她告诉他。

===Original===
“How was that? I do not understand.”

===Chinese===
“怎么讲的？我不明白。”

===Original===
“That Pah-Ute, the arrow went in farther than you thought. He went to Nahki Zhil trading post; there he bled to death. He told them about you. Now American Chief has made an order to put you in jail.”

===Chinese===
“那个派尤特人（Pah-Ute），箭进去得比你以为的深。他到了纳基日尔（Nahki Zhil）交易站，在那里流血流死了。他把你的事说了出去。如今美国酋长（American Chief）已经下了令，要把你关进牢里。”

===Original===
“Perhaps we had better go away from here, then.”

===Chinese===
“那兴许咱们还是离开这儿好。”

===Original===
“No, they will not do anything; it was only a Pah-Ute, they say. Only if you come into town and they see you, some policeman will take you then.”

===Chinese===
“不，他们不会怎么样的；不过是个派尤特人，他们说。只要你进了镇、叫他们撞见，那时才有警察来捉你。”

===Original===
“I am sorry. I should have liked to see that place. However, some day they may forget.”

===Chinese===
“可惜了。我本想看看那个地方。不过，总有一天他们会忘的。”

===Original===
“People have long memories for some things.”

===Chinese===
“有些个事，人的记性长着呢。”

===Original===
Yellow Singer and his wife came just at the end of the afternoon. He watched them walking, with their long shadows rippling over the unevenness of the ground and the occasional bushes.

===Chinese===
正当午后快尽的时候，黄颂唱师和他的婆娘来了。他望着他们一路走来，两条长长的影子在地面的高高低低间、在一丛丛偶尔出现的灌木上荡漾开去。

===Original===
“What are those coming? They look like Pah-Utes, perhaps. They look like Hunger People! What rags!”

===Chinese===
“走来的那两个是什么人？瞧着像派尤特人，兴许。倒像饥饿之民（Hunger People）！多么破烂的衣裳！”

===Original===
“They are Navajos; that is Yellow Singer. They look like that because they are poor, that’s all. He has come to sing over us.”

===Chinese===
“他们是纳瓦霍人；那是黄颂唱师。他们那副样子是因为穷，就这样。他是来给我们颂唱的。”

===Original===
There was something about those two faces that made Laughing Boy uncomfortable, as though a black veil had been pulled in front of them. They were people who would have unpleasant laughter. Both of them looked at him with open curiosity and an expression of understanding that bothered him. As they exchanged formal greetings, these two seemed to be extending to him a sympathy which he did not want. Then Slim Girl came out of the house dressed in her richest costume, and they were expressionless. Then, too, Laughing Boy was not concerned any more.

===Chinese===
那两张脸上有种什么东西，叫笑娃浑身不自在，仿佛有一幅黑纱幔蒙在了他们跟前。那是笑起来会笑得叫人不受用的人。两个都带着毫不遮掩的好奇打量他，那副了然于心的神情扰得他难受。彼此见礼的时候，这两人像是在朝他递过来一种同情，而他不要这种同情。随后瘦姑娘（Slim Girl）穿着她最华贵的一身衣裳从屋里出来，两人顿时没了表情。这么一来，笑娃也就不放在心上了。

===Original===
Yellow Singer’s wife handed a medicine basket to Slim Girl, which she filled with the corn mush she had prepared. The singer placed it in the correct place on the floor of the house. Laughing Boy entered carefully. He was thinking hard about what he was doing; he was putting forth every effort to make it good and beautiful. He thought about the gods, about Slim Girl, about the future. It was all confused, because he was excited. He wished House God would come to stand over them; he thought Hunting Goddess would be a good one for her, or Young Goddess, or White Shell Woman. Now Yellow Singer’s wife was leading her in. Under his breath, “Oh, beautiful!”

===Chinese===
黄颂唱师的婆娘把一只药篮递给瘦姑娘，篮里盛满她预备好的玉米糊。颂唱师把它安放在屋里地上那个该在的位置。笑娃小心翼翼地走进来。他正使劲想着自己在做的事；他拿出全部的心力，要把它做得又好又美。他想到诸神，想到瘦姑娘，想到往后。全都乱作一团，因为他太兴奋了。他但愿家宅神（House God）会来站在他们上方；他想狩猎女神（Hunting Goddess）对她是个好的，或是年轻女神（Young Goddess），或是白贝壳女人（White Shell Woman）。这时黄颂唱师的婆娘正领着她进来。他低声叹道：“啊，真美！”

===Original===
She was thinking of many things at once; her excitement was deep down like a desert river under the sands. Now I really have a husband in my house. When this is over, it will be a test between us. I ought to feel all sorts of things, marrying like this now. You are handsome, Sings Before Spears. You do not know how much you are mine.

===Chinese===
她同时想着许多事；她的兴奋深埋在底下，像沙底的漠地暗河。这下我屋里真真有了一个男人。这一场过去，便是我们俩的一场较量。像这样眼下就成亲，我原该百般滋味涌上心来才是。你生得俊，矛前放歌。你不知道你已是何等地是我的人。

===Original===
She sat down on the rug beside him. Yellow Singer divided the mush in four directions. Now he was praying for them. Laughing Boy concentrated his thought. Unreasonably, the girl was terrified lest something might happen. She was madly impatient. Now they partook of the yellow corn, ceremonially, and now it was Laughing Boy’s turn to make a prayer. He sang the prayer to House God with solemn emphasis:

===Chinese===
她在他身旁的毯子上坐下。黄颂唱师把玉米糊朝四个方向分开。此刻他正为他们祈祷。笑娃凝神定虑。没来由地，那姑娘恐慌起来，生怕要出什么事。她急得要疯。现在两人依着仪节同食黄玉米，现在轮到笑娃献祷了。他庄重着力，唱起献给家宅神的祷歌：

===Original===
“House made of dawn light,
						

						House made of evening light,
						

						House made of dark cloud,
						

						House made of he-rain,
						

						House made of dark mist,
						

						House made of pollen﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
“黎明之光筑成的屋宇，
暮色之光筑成的屋宇，
黑云筑成的屋宇，
雄雨筑成的屋宇，
黑雾筑成的屋宇，
花粉筑成的屋宇……”

===Original===
Yellow Singer’s wife, fat and sleepy, sat in the corner. She was vaguely sorry for that young man. She rolled a cigarette, wishing he had not chosen so long a prayer.

===Chinese===
黄颂唱师的婆娘，胖胖的、困困的，坐在角落里。她隐隐地替那年轻人觉着委屈，卷了一支烟，心里只嫌他挑了这么长的一段祷文。

===Original===
“In beauty it is finished,
						

						In beauty it is finished.”

===Chinese===
“在美之中告成，
在美之中告成。”

===Original===
The confident, solemn voice ceased. He looked at Slim Girl. Now they were married “in a beautiful way.” It might seem a little furtive, that ceremony without relatives, almost without guests, but now the gods had married them. Slim Girl was staring back at him with wide eyes.

===Chinese===
那笃定而庄严的嗓音停了。他望着瘦姑娘。这下他们是“以美的方式”成亲了。这样一场没有亲族、几乎连宾客也没有的仪式，看着未免有几分偷偷摸摸，可如今，是诸神把他们结成了夫妻。瘦姑娘睁大眼睛，回望着他。

===Original===
Yellow Singer’s wife stretched bulkily. “Now let us feast.”

===Chinese===
黄颂唱师的婆娘伸展了一下肥大的身子。“现在咱们开席吧。”

===Original===
The old singer grinned. Slim Girl regarded them dreamily until her husband spoke to her,

===Chinese===
老颂唱师咧嘴笑了。瘦姑娘恍恍惚惚地望着他们，直到她男人对她说话，

===Original===
“The guests must feast.”

===Chinese===
“客人是要宴乐的。”

===Original===
She brought forth a banquet﻿—canned tomatoes, pears, plums, beans, candies, pop, white bread. She heated the coffee, and set it out with plenty of sugar, and cups for all. On one side she put down a bottle of what looked like water.

===Chinese===
她端出一场席面——番茄、梨、李子、豆子，尽是罐头，另有糖果、汽水、白面包。她把咖啡煨热，端出来，糖给得足足的，杯子人人有一只。在一旁，她放下了一瓶看着像清水的东西。

===Original===
“It is a feast,” said Yellow Singer.

===Chinese===
“这真是席面。”黄颂唱师说。

===Original===
They all started eating. The old man took some of the clear liquid, and passed it to his wife, who drank and offered it to Laughing Boy.

===Chinese===
众人都吃了起来。老头子舀了些那清亮的液体，递给他的婆娘；婆娘喝了，把它敬到笑娃面前。

===Original===
“What is it?”

===Chinese===
“这是什么？”

===Original===
“Whiskey,” Slim Girl spoke quickly; “he does not know how to drink it.”

===Chinese===
“威士忌，”瘦姑娘接口很快；“他不会喝这个。”

===Original===
“I should like to try it.”

===Chinese===
“我想尝尝。”

===Original===
“It is good; you should take it, Grandfather. We all like it.” Yellow Singer chuckled.

===Chinese===
“它是好东西；你该喝的，老爷爷。我们都喜欢它。”黄颂唱师呵呵地笑。

===Original===
“I do not think he can stand it,” Slim Girl said thoughtfully.

===Chinese===
“我看他顶不住。”瘦姑娘若有所思地说。

===Original===
“Let me try it. It is right that I should find out about these things. Give me the bottle.”

===Chinese===
“让我试试。这些事我原该弄个明白。把瓶子给我。”

===Original===
He saw that her eyes, watching him, were speculative, with something hidden in back of them. He thought she was measuring him.

===Chinese===
他看见她望着自己的那双眼睛带着掂量，眼底后头还藏着点东西。他想，她是在称他的分量。

===Original===
“Give it to me.”

===Chinese===
“给我。”

===Original===
“Wait. You are not used to it; let me fix it for you. I can make it taste good.”

===Chinese===
“等等。你吃不惯它；让我来给你调配。我能把它调出好味道来。”

===Original===
She took out an orange and a lemon, that she had had hidden behind the water jar.

===Chinese===
她摸出一只橙子和一只柠檬来——那是她早早藏在水罐后头的。

===Original===
“What are those?”

===Chinese===
“那是什么？”

===Original===
“That is called ‘lanch.’ This yellow one is ‘lemon’; there is no word for it in Navajo. They are American fruits; they grow on trees, like peaches.”

===Chinese===
“那个叫作‘lanch’（橙子）。这个黄的是‘lemon’（柠檬）；纳瓦霍话里没有它的词。它们是美国水果；树上长的，跟桃子一样。”

===Original===
She made a stiffish drink in a tin cup, sweetened with a great deal of sugar.

===Chinese===
她在一只铁皮杯里调了杯颇有劲头的酒，搁了许许多多的糖。

===Original===
“You never did that for me,” Yellow Singer muttered to his wife.

===Chinese===
“你可从来没为我这么调过。”黄颂唱师冲他婆娘咕哝。

===Original===
“I shall do it for you, that thing﻿—when you are as young and handsome as he is.”

===Chinese===
“我会给你调的，那件事——等你也像他这么年轻、这么俊的时候。”

===Original===
They watched him with grinning interest while he tried the drink. Slim Girl was inattentive.

===Chinese===
他试着呷那杯酒，两人咧着嘴，看得津津有味。瘦姑娘却心不在焉。

===Original===
“But this is good! This is better than that red boiling water, I think.” He nodded towards his bottle of pop. It began to glow in his stomach. “What is it doing to me?”

===Chinese===
“可这真好！这比那种滚腾腾的红水强，我想。”他朝自己那瓶汽水点点头。他肚子里开始发起热来，亮堂堂的。“它这是在把我怎么样？”

===Original===
“It is beginning to do its good, little brother.” The medicine man pulled at the bottle in sympathy. “By and by you will love it, that feeling.” His wife reached for her share.

===Chinese===
“它开始行它的好了，兄弟。”那巫医感同身受，就着瓶子抿了一口。“往后你会爱上它的，那种滋味。”他的婆娘伸手来接她那一份。

===Original===
Slim Girl stood up. “The sun is setting. If you take much more of that now, you will not find your way home. Here is a bottle for you to take with you.”

===Chinese===
瘦姑娘站起身。“太阳落了。你这会儿再多吃上一些，就摸不着回家的路了。这儿有一瓶，你们带着走。”

===Original===
Inside the house it was half dark, and the doorway framed the clay bluffs opposite, painted with sunset. She rearranged the blankets and sheepskins for reclining against the wall, and there relaxed, smoking a cigarette. He finished his drink, liking the flavoured sugar in the bottom of it.

===Chinese===
屋里已经半黑，门洞把对面的泥岩峭壁框成一幅画，涂满了落日的颜色。她把毯子和羊皮重新铺排好，好倚着墙半躺，便在那里松快下来，抽着一支烟。他喝完了自己那杯，很爱那沉在杯底的带味的糖。

===Original===
“Sit over here, and let us talk a little now.”

===Chinese===
“坐到这边来，眼下咱们说一会儿话。”

===Original===
He placed himself half-sitting, half-lying beside her.

===Chinese===
他在她身边安顿下来，半坐半卧。

===Original===
“That is queer, that drink. I feel queer here,” touching the hinge of his jaw, “as if something were squeezing my teeth. But it is good; make me some more of it.”

===Chinese===
“那酒真古怪。我这儿觉得怪怪的，”他摸着腮帮骨的关节处，“像有什么东西在挤我的牙。可它是好的；再给我调一些。”

===Original===
“Not right away.”

===Chinese===
“先不忙。”

===Original===
“It would be good to sing, I think. Let us sing some very beautiful prayers together. Everything is good now.”

===Chinese===
“这会儿唱歌才好呢，我想。咱们一块儿唱几支顶顶美的祷歌吧。如今样样都好了。”

===Original===
“Let us just think for a little bit, now we are married.”

===Chinese===
“咱们且静静地待一小会儿，咱们成亲了。”

===Original===
“Now we are married. Why are you looking at me like that? I do not understand you all the time, what your face means.”

===Chinese===
“咱们成亲了。你怎么那样望着我？我不是总能懂你——你那张脸是什么意思。”

===Original===
“I like to look at you, Sings Before Spears.” Her hand fell into his, he felt her beside him. Something told him that that was only half an answer. He touched her face with his fingertips. She was studying him intently.

===Chinese===
“我喜欢看你，矛前放歌。”她的手落进他的手里，他觉出她在自己身旁。有什么告诉他，那只是半个回答。他用指尖抚了抚她的脸。她正一心一意地端详他。

===Original===
Then she kissed him. He did not understand it; her face suddenly near his, against his, distorted so close to his eyes, her eyes run together. He was held tightly, and something wet, at once hot and cool was against his mouth, with a tiny, fierce imprint of teeth. Vaguely he remembered hearing that Americans did this. He did not understand it; he had a feeling of messiness and disgust. He tried to move away, but she held him; he was pressed against the wall and the sheepskins. She was fastened onto him; he could feel all her body, it was entering into him. There was something uncontrolled, indecent about this. Everything became confused. A little flame ran along his veins. The world melted away from under him, his body became water floating in air, all his life was in his lips, mouth to mouth and breath against his face. He shut his eyes. His arms were around her. Now, almost unwittingly, he began to return her kisses.

===Chinese===
随后她吻了他。他不懂这个；她的脸忽然凑近他的脸，贴着他的脸，离他的眼睛那么近，都变了形，她的两只眼睛并成了一处。他被紧紧搂住，一样湿的东西，又热又凉，贴在他的嘴上，还带着牙齿细细的、凶凶的一啮。他模模糊糊记得听说过，美国人是这么做的。他不懂这个；他有一种乱糟糟的、叫人恶心兮兮的感觉。他想挪开，可她抱住他；他被挤在墙和羊皮中间。她整个人钉在他身上；他觉出她整个的身体，它正进到他的身体里来。这里头有种失了管束的、不成体统的东西。一切都乱作一团。一小股火苗顺着血管窜起来。世界从他脚底下化没了，他的身子变成浮在空气里的水，他整条性命都聚在唇上，嘴对着嘴，呼吸扑在脸上。他闭上了眼睛。他的胳膊搂住了她。这时，几乎是不由自主地，他开始回吻她。


### III / III

===Original===
Slim Girl was asleep. Laughing Boy was very tired, but there was no rest for him. It was black inside the house; here no night wind blew through the leaves of the hogan wall, no stars looked down through a smoke-hole. He found his tobacco and stole outside.

===Chinese===
