瘦姑娘睡着了。笑娃累得很，可他得不到歇息。屋里黑漆漆的；这里没有夜风穿过霍根屋（hogan，纳瓦霍人泥木筑的小屋）墙上的枝叶，没有星星从烟洞里俯望下来。他寻出自己的烟丝，悄悄溜到外头去。

===Original===
The cold night wind blew against his skin. His eyes rested among the shadowy forms of the buttes; he looked up at the thick-gathered, cool stars. It was like laying a cold knife-blade against a burnt finger. It was right that they gave women names about war; he understood that now.

===Chinese===
冰冷的夜风吹在他皮肤上。他的目光落在那些方山的黑影轮廓中间；又仰起头，望那攒得密密匝匝、清凉清凉的星星。就像把一片凉刀刃贴上烫了的手指头。他们给女人起带战争的名字，起得对；这层他如今懂了。

===Original===
His feet took him up onto the high place above the spring, where he had slept before. There he made himself comfortable with his back against a piñón trunk, smoking. Everything was whirling within him: it was necessary to put his thoughts in order. He had never imagined it was anything like this. He had lived the intimate life of the *hogan*, he knew the campfire jokes; but there was never a hint of this kind of thing. It might be just American tricks, but he thought not. No, it was she, her power. She was stronger than iron or fire. That drink was medicine, but she was not medicine; it was just she herself. That blade of grass of a girl, that little Slim Girl, she could make his belly turn over inside of him, she could make his interior dissolve. He sat wondering at her and at himself. So very uncontrolled, at moments he felt ashamed, but mostly it was wonder. That girl was like one of the Divine People. One should not forget one’s self, but this was a beautiful thing.

===Chinese===
他的一双脚把他带上了泉眼上头那处高地，他先前睡过的地方。他在那里背靠一株皮尼翁松（piñón）的树干安顿舒服了，抽着烟。他心里样样都在打转：非得把心思理出个条理来不可。他从没想象过事情竟是这样。霍根屋里的亲热日子他过过，营火边的浑话他也懂；可从来没有这一路事的半点影子。兴许不过是美国人的花巧，可他想不是。不，是她，是她的力量。她比铁还硬，比火还烈。那杯酒是药，可她不是药；那就只是她自己。那样一茎草叶似的姑娘，那样一个小小的瘦姑娘，她能叫他肚肠在肚子里翻个个儿，能叫他一腹五脏都化掉。他坐在那儿，对她、对他自己惊叹不已。那么地失于检点，有几刻他觉得羞臊，可多半是惊叹。那姑娘就像神圣之民（Divine People）中的一位。人是不该忘了自己的，可这是一件美的东西。

===Original===
He had fought, and sung, and raced horses, and known the uplift of the great dances; he was old friends with hunger, cold, fatigue, and suddenly contrasted feasting and comfort; he had lived out to the tips of his fingers, but this was something that made everything else turn thin and shadowy. That was her magic, perhaps, and it was wonderful, it altered life: that she gave complete fulfilment, where everything else was partial. Her house was better than other Indians’ houses, her food was richer than other Indians ate, and she fed one’s spirit with a perfection that only she could give. Hunger was dead where she was. She was not like The People; life with her would have to be different, but the trail was beautiful.

===Chinese===
他打过仗，唱过歌，赛过马，领略过大舞令人飞扬的时刻；他同饥饿、寒冷、疲乏是老交情，也识得骤然对照之下的宴乐与安逸；他一直活到了指尖上，可这是另一路东西，它叫其余的一切都变得单薄、变得影子似的。那是她的巫术吧，兴许，它神妙，它改换了生活：她给的是十足的圆满，其余一切都只给半份。她的屋子比别的印第安人的屋子好，她的吃食比别的印第安人吃的丰厚，她还拿唯有她才给得出的完美，喂养人的性灵。她在哪儿，饥饿就在哪儿死了。她跟迪内人不一样；跟她过日子必定要不一样，可这条路是美的。

===Original===
His cigarette was long dead. Boy Chasing His Arrow had moved far across the sky. Drowsiness mingled itself with his thoughts, blurring them, while the night wind blew upon him. His doubts and wonderments faded and thickened into sleep. Sleeping, he dreamed a dream. Perhaps the sight of the ragged, wretched singer and his wife had put it into his head. It was not much of a dream, not elaborate.

===Chinese===
他的烟卷早就熄透了。追箭少年（Boy Chasing His Arrow）已经在天上移出去老远。睡意掺进他的思绪，把它们搅得模糊，夜风一直吹在他身上。他的疑惑和惊叹褪下去、稠起来，凝成了睡眠。睡着以后，他做了一个梦。兴许是那个衣衫褴褛、可怜巴巴的颂唱师和他婆娘的样子，把这梦塞进了他脑子。梦不算什么，不繁复。

===Original===
He sat by the hogan fire, and his uncle was telling the end of the Coming Up Story. He did not really see much, but he knew it was his uncle, and he felt angry with him for what he had said about Slim Girl. He knew that the other children were there, and that the snow coming through the smoke-hole melted in the air, and fell on the fire with little hisses. It was his uncle speaking, it was Wind God speaking in his mind; they were the same. None of that was clear; only the old, familiar words came to him, very definitely, spoken with emphasis, as when one wants to impress a lesson upon children.

===Chinese===
他坐在霍根屋的火边，伯父正讲到上升传说（Coming Up Story）的结尾。他其实看不大真切，但他知道那是伯父，他还在为伯父说瘦姑娘的那些话生他的气。他知道别的孩子们也在跟前，知道从烟洞里飘进来的雪花在半空中就化了，落在火上，嘶嘶地轻响。是伯父在开口，也是风神（Wind God）在他心里开口；两个本是同一个。这些都不清楚；只有那些老的、熟悉的话来到他耳边，字字分明，带着着重的语气，像要把一个道理深深印进孩子们心里去时那样。

===Original===
“Slayer of Enemy Gods came to the Hunger People, they say﻿ ﻿… He said, ‘Now I am going to kill you, because you are bad for my people.’ Hunger Chief said, ‘If you kill us, nobody will be hungry any more, nobody will care about feasting. They will not go out and hunt for good food, venison, and fat prairie-dog. Nobody will want to go hunting,’ he said,” they say.

===Chinese===
“诛敌神者（Slayer of Enemy Gods）来到饥饿之民那里，他们说……他说：‘这会儿我要杀了你们，因为你们害我的人民。’饥饿首领（Hunger Chief）说：‘你若杀了我们，就再没有人挨饿，再没有人把宴乐放在心上。他们不会出去寻好东西吃了，鹿肉，肥肥的草原犬鼠。谁都不想再打猎了，’他说，”他们说。

===Original===
“Slayer of Enemy Gods said, ‘Then I shall let you live,’ they say.”

===Chinese===
“诛敌神者说：‘那我就许你们活下去，’他们说。”

===Original===
He heard Talking God call in the distance, “Wu, wu, wu, wu!” Then the god called again, nearer. He knew he was asleep, and tried to wake, to meet the god coming. He was terrified lest he should be asleep when the god called for the fourth time. He had an idea that he had put himself asleep deliberately, against the god’s will. The call sounded for a third time, just outside the hogan. Talking God was out there, standing on a bent rainbow, in company with many other Divine Ones. He had shut himself up in a pitch-black place with no entrance; he had locked himself away from the gods under a blanket of dark clouds.

===Chinese===
他听见说话神（Talking God）在远处呼唤：“呜，呜，呜，呜！”随后神又唤了，近了些。他知道自己睡着，拼命要醒过来，好迎那来到的神。他怕极了，生怕神唤到第四声时他还睡着。他有一种感觉：他是故意把自己弄睡着的，逆着神的意愿。呼唤第三回响起来，就在霍根屋外头。说话神就在那儿，立在一道弯弯的虹上，同着许许多多别的神圣之灵（Divine Ones）。他把自己关进了一个漆黑的、没有入口的地方；他把自己锁在了众神外面，压在一床黑云的毯子底下。

===Original===
With a desperate effort he woke, sitting up, gasping with relief. His eyes drank in the dawn, the open space, the tree, the clay buttes, things apart from his dream. Over across, a turtledove called, “*Hoo, hoo, hoo-hoo-hoo.*”

===Chinese===
他拼死一挣，醒了过来，坐起身，松了口气，直喘。他的眼睛饮着黎明、空旷、那棵树、那些泥岩方山——这些都在梦的外头。对过，一只斑鸠叫起来：“咕，咕，咕咕咕。”

## Chapter VIII / 第八章

### I / I

===Original===
She was still asleep inside the house. He stood looking down upon her in the half-light. She seemed frail, childish, and sweet, with the shadow under the eyelids, her mouth faintly drooping, her figure reduced to almost nothing beneath the blanket. He thought of that drama of strength and weakness, of conquering and being conquered, fitting it to this small person, soft in sleep. Now that he was looking at her, he had no reservations; it only seemed a miracle that she should be his. He wondered at the mere chance it was; Slender Hair speaking to him of the dance and the racing, coming to Tsé Lani, this little incident and that, until out of nowhere that which might never have been entered and became the core of his life.

===Chinese===
她还睡在屋里。他站在半明半暗的光里，低头望着她。她显得纤弱、稚气而甜美，眼睑下罩着一层暗影，嘴角微微下垂，毯子底下的身形几乎缩得若有若无。他想起那出关于强与弱、关于征服与被征服的戏文，把它套在这个睡得软软的小人儿身上。此刻这样望着她，他心里再无半点保留；只觉得她竟能归他所有，这本身就像个奇迹。他惊叹这纯乎是偶然：细发（Slender Hair）同他谈起舞会和赛马，来到特塞拉尼（Tsé Lani），这一桩小事，那一桩小事，直到那件原本也许永远不会成真的事凭空而至，闯了进来，成了他生命的核心。

===Original===
The sun would be up soon. He went to meet it.

===Chinese===
太阳快升起来了。他出去迎接它。

===Original===
“Dawn Boy, little chief,
						

						May all be beautiful before me as I wander﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
“黎明少年（Dawn Boy），小首领，
						

						愿我一路浪游，眼前皆在美中……”

===Original===
She woke happy, watching him under lazy eyelids as he stood outside the door, naked save for his breechclout, with the level sunlight touching the edges of his flanks and ribs, making a golden reflection where his upraised arms bunched the muscles at his shoulders. She thanked God and the gods indiscriminately. Whatever happened now, this could not be taken away. She shifted the blanket, closed her eyes, and assumed sleep.

===Chinese===
她带着欢喜醒来，惺忪的眼帘底下望着他：他站在门外，只系一条遮裆布，周身赤裸，平直的阳光抚着他侧腰和肋骨的棱线，高举的双臂把肩头的肌肉束起来，映出一层金光。她不分彼此，上帝和诸神一概谢过了。往后无论出什么事，这个是夺不走的了。她动了动毯子，闭上眼睛，装出睡着的样子。

===Original===
He sat down beside her, a little nervous about her awakening. Her eyelids quivered, she yawned deliciously, she stretched her arms like a kitten playing. She sat up and smiled at him, seeing his face brighten as he responded.

===Chinese===
他在她身旁坐下，想到她就要醒来，心里微微发紧。她的眼皮颤了颤，惬意地打个哈欠，又像只玩耍的小猫似的伸了伸胳膊。她坐起身来朝他微笑，看见他回应时，脸上亮堂起来。

===Original===
“Have I slept so late? I shall get your breakfast as soon as I have fixed your hair. You should see it.”

===Chinese===
“我睡到这么晚了？等我把你的头发拾掇好，就去弄你的早饭。你真该瞧瞧它现在什么样。”

===Original===
He felt of its disarray, with the queue hanging lopsided, then he grinned at her. “Your own is just as bad; go look at yourself in the spring.”

===Chinese===
他摸了摸那头乱发，辫子歪挂在一旁，随即朝她咧嘴一笑。“你自己的也好不到哪儿去；上泉水边照照你自己去。”

===Original===
She reached over to a shelf and took down a small mirror, which she handed to him. He looked at himself in it; this was fascinating but a little disappointing. Finally she took it from him.

===Chinese===
她探身到架子那边，取下一面小镜子递给他。他对着镜子照自己；这东西迷人，却又有点儿叫人失望。末了她把它从他手里收了回去。

===Original===
“Come, now, dress, and do up my hair.”

===Chinese===
“来吧，快些，穿上衣裳，给我把头发编起来。”

===Original===
He had often exchanged that service with his brothers and sisters; it was a pleasant and friendly act. He had watched his mother and father together at it, one leaning against the other’s knees, laughing when the brush pulled too hard, and he had seen that they extracted some pleasure from it which he did not know. Now he understood that, and the sheer domesticity of it delighted him. He felt really married, settled, a man who would soon have children, and speak as one of established position, no more a boy.

===Chinese===
这种互相梳头的活计，他从前常和弟弟妹妹们换着做；那是一桩愉快而亲热的举动。他见过母亲和父亲在一起梳头，一个倚着另一个的膝头，刷子拽得太重时便笑出声来，他那时便看出来，两人从里头得了某种他所不知道的乐趣。如今他懂了，这份十足的居家过日子的滋味叫他心花怒放。他觉得自己真正成了亲、落了户，是个不久就会有孩子的人了，开口也像个立住了地位的人，再不是个孩子。

===Original===
Breakfast was welcome when it came.

===Chinese===
早饭端上来时，正合心意。

===Original===
“Today you must get me the wood for a loom,” she told him. “It is a long time since I have woven, but I have beautiful blankets in my mind. I shall weave and you shall make jewelry.”

===Chinese===
“今天你得给我砍织机用的木料来，”她告诉他。“我很久没织布了，可我心里存着几床好看的毯子。我来织布，你来打银饰。”

===Original===
“No, today, before we do anything, we must make a sweat-bath. This is all different, here. We are starting off new. We must make ourselves clean, we must make a fresh start. And, besides, how can you weave? You have no sheep.”

===Chinese===
“不行，今天，咱们别的什么都先不干，先做个汗浴。这儿的一切都不一样了。咱们是重新起头。咱们得把自己弄干净，得来个崭新的开始。再说，你怎么织布？你连羊都没有。”

===Original===
“I shall buy wool in the store in town. They sell good wool there; the people round here bring it in.”

===Chinese===
“我上镇里的铺子买羊毛去。那儿卖好羊毛；这一带的人都往那儿送。”

===Original===
“Why do we not raise sheep? I have some in my mother’s flock I could get.”

===Chinese===
“咱们干吗不养羊？我母亲的羊群里有几只，我可以讨来。”

===Original===
“Who will keep them? You will have your horses and your jewelry; that will be plenty. I must always be going in to work for that missionary woman.”

===Chinese===
“谁来经管它们？你有你的马、你的银饰活计；那尽够了。我还得三天两头往镇里跑，给那个女传教士做工。”

===Original===
“I do not like your working in there.”

===Chinese===
“我不喜欢你上那儿做工。”

===Original===
“Why, have you some bad thought?”

===Chinese===
“怎么，你存了什么坏心思？”

===Original===
“No, I have no bad thought. But a house is empty when the woman is away.”

===Chinese===
“没有，我没存坏心思。可是婆娘一走，屋子就空了。”

===Original===
“I used to work for her every day; now I only work sometimes. She gives me good money. It is because of her that you will have silver to work with. Tomorrow, when I go in, I shall bring you Mexican silver. And those days you can go tend to the horses. By and by, when we have made much money with your jewelry and horses and my weaving and work, we shall go back to your country. We shall go back rich. Is it not a good plan?”

===Chinese===
“我从前天天都给她做工；如今只是偶尔去做。她给的钱不薄。你往后有银子打东西，托的正是她的福。明天我进城，就给你捎墨西哥银回来。那几天你正好去照看马。往后，等咱们靠你的银饰和马、我的织布和做工攒下许多钱，咱们就回你的家乡去。咱们风风光光地回去。这计划还不好么？”

===Original===
“You have spoken well. Here, I do not know about these things, but you know, and your words are good. It is enough for me that I have you. It is not just that we are married, but we are married all through; there is not any part of us left out.”

===Chinese===
“你说得在理。这些事在这儿我不懂，可你懂，你的话是好的。有你在，我尽够了。不光是咱们成了亲，咱们是从头到脚、彻里彻外结的亲；咱们俩，没有哪一星半点是落在外头的。”

===Original===
“No; there is not any part of us left out. And you do not want a second wife, Laughing Boy?”

===Chinese===
“是啊；咱们俩没有哪一星半点是落在外头的。你可不想再讨一房婆娘吧，笑娃（Laughing Boy）？”

===Original===
“No﻿—no!” The extra-emphatic, three syllable negative, “*É-do-ta!*” long-drawn-out, with the decisive sign of the right hand sweeping away. “Have you a sister you want me to take? If you want help here, in the hogan, I will get one, but she will not be for me. She will be in the way. You are enough for me; perhaps you are too much for me, I think.”

===Chinese===
“不——不！”那个加重语气、三个音节的否定词，“埃-多-塔（É-do-ta）！”拖得长长的，右手横空一挥，作出斩钉截铁的手势。“你有想让我娶的姐妹么？要是你想在这儿寻个帮手，在这霍根屋里，我可以去弄一个来，可她不是给我的人。她只会碍手碍脚。有你我就尽够了；兴许你对我是太过了些，我想。”

===Original===
Quickly she kissed him. He felt embarrassed, and loved it.

===Chinese===
她飞快地吻了他一下。他觉着难为情，又偏偏受用。

===Original===
He had to teach her the ritual and song of purification, but, with faint childhood memories to aid her, she was quick to learn. Her close attention pleased him, and it was a pleasure to hear her sing. He was only sorry for her, that she had for so long been denied these things, and angry and puzzled at the schools and the American life that had forbidden them. In the end, he taught her songs that she had no business knowing, quite aware of what he did, as a kind of unavowed tribute and token of the special quality he felt in her.

===Chinese===
洁净的仪轨和歌，他得一样样教她，好在有儿时淡薄的记忆帮衬着，她学得快。她那份专注叫他欢喜，听她唱歌也是一桩享受。他只是替她难受，这些东西竟被隔绝了这么久；对禁止这些东西的学堂和美国生活，他又气又困惑。到后来，他索性把一些她按理无份知晓的歌也教给了她——他对自己做的事心知肚明——算是一份未尝说出口的敬意，也是一枚凭信，记着他在她身上觉出的那种特别的品性。

### II / II

===Original===
After the steam bath and the water and the foaming yucca-suds, it was good to lie with hair spread out, drying, and talk vaguely of things to be done, and now and again to touch her. Great achievements completed themselves in a phrase. He drew the design of a bracelet in the sand; he braided his hair and mimicked the nasal speech of a Ute. They fell to talking of the ways of different tribes, the old wars, and the present semi-hostility between the Navajo and the Pah-Utes.

===Chinese===
蒸气浴、清水，再浇上泡沫腾腾的丝兰皂液，洗过之后，摊开头发躺着晾干，有一搭没一搭地商量要办的事，时不时碰一碰她，真是好滋味。宏图大业，一言半语间便自己成了。他在沙地上画手镯的图样；他编起辫子，学尤特人（Ute）那鼻音浓重的腔调说话。两人渐渐谈起各部族的习性、昔年的战事，还有眼下纳瓦霍人和派尤特人（Pah-Utes）之间那种半敌对的形势。

===Original===
“There is not often trouble with them,” he told her, “but we do not like them. They live wild up in that country beyond Oljeto, where they are hard to catch, and they steal things. Mostly they trouble the Mormons; the Mormons are afraid of them, they say. Since I was a little boy, only once we had real trouble with them. Then one time we went on the warpath for them. I went on the warpath that time.”

===Chinese===
“跟他们不常起纠纷，”他告诉她，“可我们不喜欢他们。他们在奥尔赫托（Oljeto）再过去那片地界里撒野过活，那儿难逮着他们，他们又手脚不干净。他们多半去搅扰摩门人（Mormons）；摩门人怕他们，据说是这样。打我是个小娃娃起，同他们真刀真枪的乱子只闹过一回。就是那一回，我们为他们出了征。那回出征我也在数。”

===Original===
He felt proud of the part he had played, and wanted to tell her about it.

===Chinese===
他为自己当时出的力感到自豪，想把那件事讲给她听。

===Original===
“That was three years ago; I was just about full grown. Blunt Nose, he made the trouble, that one. He was chief of a band of them; he lived up beyond Naesjé canyon, near Tsé Nanaazh. That is wild country, almost in the mountains. He was bad.

===Chinese===
“那是三年前；我刚差不多长足了个子。钝鼻子（Blunt Nose），惹祸的就是那一个。他是他们一伙人的头目；住在纳埃谢（Naesjé）峡谷再上去、特塞纳纳兹（Tsé Nanaazh）近旁。那是片荒僻地界，差不多算进山里了。那家伙坏。

===Original===
“He used to kill Mormon cattle all the time, a cow, and a cow, and a cow, here and there. He needed to keep no sheep. He did the way he pleased. He wore two pistols, and had a gun on his saddle, they say. He would ride down the middle of the trail, and not turn out for anyone.

===Chinese===
“他一年到头宰摩门人的牲口，一头牛，又一头牛，又一头牛，东一头西一头。他犯不着养羊。他爱怎么干就怎么干。他身上挎两支手枪，鞍子上还架着一杆枪，据说这样。他常打小路正当中骑过去，给谁也不让道。

===Original===
“One time he heard the Mormons had sent for soldiers, so he left their country alone. Then he sat quiet for a while, but his people got hungry. A Pah-Ute will eat almost anything, but there is very little up there. That is why the Navajos leave them alone; there is nothing in that country but a few Pah-Utes and a few antelopes. You cannot make anything out of the skins of either, so we let them alone.

===Chinese===
“有一回他听说摩门人派人去叫大兵了，便不去动他们的地界了。他消停了一阵，可他的人饿慌了。派尤特人差不多什么都吃得下，可那上头实在出产寥寥。所以纳瓦霍人不招惹他们；那片地界除了不多几个派尤特人、不多几只羚羊，什么也没有。这两样的皮子哪样也做不成物件，所以我们由他们去。

===Original===
“Well, now Blunt Nose decided to try mutton. He came down by Jahai Spring where Hungry Man lived. He had all his braves with him. They started to run off Hungry Man’s sheep. Then The Doer came along. You have heard of him? He is the one who killed those two Americans; his father was Generous Chief, the one who never was captured when The People went into exile. So The Doer came along; he saw that man’s wife, where she came running. He rode up, he started shooting at those men. They shot back at him. They were too many for him; he rode away and they chased him. But he killed one.

===Chinese===
“好，这下钝鼻子打定主意尝尝羊肉。他下到贾海泉（Jahai Spring）那一带，饿汉（Hungry Man）就住在那儿。他把手下的勇士全带了来。他们动手赶跑饿汉的羊。恰巧干事汉（The Doer）打那儿经过。你听说过他吧？就是杀了那两个美国人的那一个；他父亲是慷慨酋长（Generous Chief），迪内（The People）遭流放那年月始终没被拿住的那一个。就这样，干事汉打那儿经过；他望见那人的婆娘奔跑而来。他策马赶上去，朝那些人开了火。他们回敬他。他们人多，他吃不消；他拨马便走，他们追他。可他撂倒了一个。

===Original===
“Then Blunt Nose was angry. He killed Hungry Man, and his two children. He ran off the sheep. He went back into the Naesjé country.

===Chinese===
“钝鼻子这就恼了。他杀了饿汉，还有他的两个孩子。他把羊赶跑了。他回纳埃谢地界去了。

===Original===
“The People around there gathered together to hunt him, but the trader at Oljeto told them not to. He told them to wait while he wrote a paper to American Chief at T’o Nanasdési. So they did that. Meantime Blunt Nose was talking. He learned that there were no soldiers in the Mormon country, but he said he liked mutton. He said no Coyote could kill one of his braves and not be punished; he said the Coyote People would pay for what they had done. He talked like this all around; he talked brave, calling us that name.

===Chinese===
“那一带的迪内聚起来要去猎他，奥尔赫托的交易商叫他们先别动。他说等一等，容他写一纸文书，送给托纳纳斯代西（T’o Nanasdési）的美国酋长（American Chief）。大家便照办了。这当口钝鼻子在外面放话。他打听到摩门人的地界并没有大兵，可他说他好羊肉这一口。他说，哪条郊狼（Coyote）也别想杀了他一名勇士而不受罚；他说郊狼人（Coyote People）要为他们干下的事付出代价。他四处这么放话；话说得凶，还拿那个名号称呼我们。

===Original===
“In a little while he came down again. But they had men on watch, they made smoke signals. A lot of people came together and went after him, so that he had to make a big circle, around by Oljeto, to get back. When he went past the trading post, he shot into it. He did not hurt anyone, but he spoiled some tin cans and broke some windows. Then, on his way home, he crossed the bridge over T’o Atsisi Creek. It is a big wooden bridge that you can ride a horse over; Washindon had it built. They sent two Americans to show how it should be built.

===Chinese===
“没过多久他又下来了。可这边有人望风，打起了烟讯号。许多人聚齐去追他，逼得他兜了个大圈子，绕道奥尔赫托那一边，才脱身回去。他打交易站前经过时，朝站里开枪。没伤着人，可打坏了些铁皮罐头，震碎了些窗玻璃。随后在回家路上，他过了特奥·阿齐西（T’o Atsisi）溪上的桥。那是座大木桥，骑马也过得去；是瓦辛顿（Washindon）叫人修的。他们派了两个美国人来指点该怎么修。

===Original===
“Well, he came by this bridge. He was angry, so he burnt it, that man. And he got some sheep, and went home.

===Chinese===
“好嘛，他打这桥上过。他正一肚子火，就把它烧了，那家伙。他又捎上些羊，回家去了。

===Original===
“Then the trader wrote another paper, a strong one, and sent it to American Chief. Meantime The Doer was getting up a war-party. The People were angry; they wanted to kill them, all those Pah-Utes. So American Chief wrote a paper saying that he was getting up a war-party of Americans who would do the fighting. He said to have good horses ready for them, and that the Navajos must not start fighting or there would be soldiers. That was what he said.

===Chinese===
“随后交易商又写了一纸文书，一纸口气硬的，送给美国酋长。这当口干事汉正在纠合一支出征队。迪内人怒了；他们要把那些派尤特人一个不留全杀了。美国酋长便回文说，他要纠合一支美国人的出征队，由他们来动刀兵。他说要给他们备好脚力好的马，还说纳瓦霍人不许先开衅，不然就要开来大兵。他是这么说的。

===Original===
“He tried to talk to Dokoslid over the talking wires, to get the war-party, but a man down by Besh Nanaazh had cut some of the wires to mend his wagon, so he had to wait a day to fix them. Meantime he sent a rider with this paper.

===Chinese===
“他想通过传话线同多科斯利德（Dokoslid）讲话，去调那支出征队，可是贝什纳纳兹（Besh Nanaazh）那头低处一个人铰了几股线去修他的大车，他只得等上一天，等把线接好。这当口他派出一骑，带着这纸文书上路。

===Original===
“The rider came by Gomulli T’o trading post. His horse was lame and he was tired. I was there with a good horse, a roan. Yellow Mustache was the trader there; he told me to carry the paper. He told me to ride hard. He said I would be paid.

===Chinese===
“那骑手打戈穆利·特奥（Gomulli T’o）交易站经过。他的马瘸了，人也乏透了。我正在那儿，带着一匹好马，一匹沙毛马。黄胡子（Yellow Mustache）是那儿的交易商；他叫我把文书带上去。他叫我豁出命赶路。他说会有赏钱。

===Original===
“I rode all day. I rode at night until the moon set. I rode after it was all dark, but I was afraid of the spirits. Then I made camp. The next morning I saw a fresh horse, so I caught it and rode on. The sun was about halfway up when I saw Oljeto. And right then the Pah-Utes saw me. They started after me, and I went as hard as I could for the trading post.

===Chinese===
“我跑了一整天。夜里也跑，跑到月亮落山。天全黑之后我又跑了一程，可我怕鬼。这才扎营歇下。第二天清早我望见一匹闲散的壮马，便捉住它接着赶路。太阳升到一半高的光景，我望见了奥尔赫托。也正在那时，派尤特人瞧见了我。他们追了上来，我豁出一切朝交易站飞奔。

===Original===
“I could hear them shooting at me; I could hear their bullets. I was very much afraid, but there was nothing to do except ride hard.

===Chinese===
“我听得见他们朝我放松；听得见子弹飕飕地过。我怕极了，可没有别的法子，只有拼命地跑。

===Original===
“Then I felt something hit me. It made a dull thump; it did not hurt. I thought, ‘I have been hit in the bottom of my spine. In a minute it will hurt. Probably I shall die, I do not want to die.’ That way I thought.

===Chinese===
“接着我觉得什么东西撞上了我。闷闷一声钝响；并不疼。我想：‘我脊梁根上中了弹。过一分钟就要疼起来。多半我要死了；我不想死。’我那样一路想着。

===Original===
“I thought all that all at once, then I felt behind, but there was nothing. Then people began shooting from the windows of the trading post, and the Pah-Utes went away. I rode up to the door and got off, wondering if I should fall. But I was all right. There was a bullet in the cantle of my saddle; that was what I had felt.

===Chinese===
“这些念头一眨眼全过了一遍，我往身后摸去，却什么也没有。这时交易站的窗口里有人开起枪来，派尤特人便撤了。我骑到门口下马，心里拿不准自己会不会栽倒。可我毫发无损。马鞍后桥里嵌着一颗子弹；我方才觉着的就是它。

===Original===
“We waited five days for the American war-party; meantime they got some good horses together. Blunt Nose was around all the time. People gathered together in groups of ten to twelve families, or more, like in the days when we were always at war. Then those men came.

===Chinese===
“我们等了五天，等那支美国人的出征队；这其间大家凑齐了几匹好马。钝鼻子那阵子一直在左近出没。人们十个、十二个家户一伙地聚拢，也有更多的，就像从前我们长年交兵的那些年月。后来那些人到了。

===Original===
“There were eight of them. They all had badges on their shirts, like policemen, only not quite the same. One of them was a fat man; we did not see why they brought him.

===Chinese===
“他们一共八个。衬衫上都别着徽章，像警察，只是不大一样。其中有一个胖子；我们看不出带他来做什么用。

===Original===
“They said they wanted four Navajos to be trackers. The Doer was to be head tracker. There were two other young men, and then they took me. They said they would pay me a dollar a day. That was a new idea to me, to be paid for hunting Pah-Utes. I thought you just hunted them.

===Chinese===
“他们说要用四个纳瓦霍人当追踪手。干事汉领追踪手。另有两位年轻人，随后又挑中了我。他们说一天付我一块钱。追猎派尤特人还能拿钱，这在我是个新道理。我还道追猎他们就只是追猎罢了。

===Original===
“We tracked them for three days without seeing them. They tried to make us go at night, but we pretended not to be able to follow the trail. When The Doer told them that, they believed him. It was not an easy trail. Their horses were not shod, and they went a great deal over bald rock, they turned and doubled, they dragged branches behind them.

===Chinese===
“我们缀着踪迹走了三天，没见着他们的人。他们要我们夜里也赶路，我们只装作缀不住踪迹。干事汉替我们一说，他们便信了。那踪迹也实在难跟。他们的马没钉蹄铁，净走光秃秃的石面，又绕圈子又折返，还在身后拖着树枝扫踪。

===Original===
“We did not get much to eat, we could not make a fire. Those Americans brought a brown, sweet candy, and a little, dried-up black candy in boxes, something sticky. These we ate; you could go a long time on them. Then we had some dried corn. We started at first light; we went till it was quite dark. During the day it was hot among those rocks. We were hungry. It did not seem that we were following Pah-Utes; we were just following tracks in the sand, or little scratches on the rock, and some day we should come to the end of them, and something important would happen. Now the only thing in the world was those marks. When you saw one where you had not expected it, it seemed to shout at you.

===Chinese===
“我们没什么进嘴的，又生不得火。那些美国人带来一种褐色的甜糖块，还有小盒里一种干缩发黑、黏糊糊的糖。我们就吃这些；吃了能顶好一程。后来我们又有些干玉米。天刚放亮就动身；走到天黑透才歇。白天在那片石头窝里热得厉害。我们饿着。倒不像是在追什么派尤特人；只是在追沙地上的蹄印，或是石面上细细的刮痕，总有一天我们会追到这些印子的尽头，那时便要出一件大事。这会儿世上独一无二的东西，就是那些印子。在你意想不到的地方猛见着一个，它就像冲你叫喊。

===Original===
“But we were gaining on them. On the fourth day they were much fresher, those tracks. We were close to them. Then we saw a couple of them, on lame horses, and we chased them. They had to cross a deep arroyo; when they went down into it we raced, hoping to catch them at the bottom of it. But they got up the other side; they just came up that side when we got to the near edge. Right away they started shooting. They shot at that fat man.

===Chinese===
“可我们步步逼近了。到第四天，那些蹄印新鲜多了。我们离他们近了。这时我们望见他们两个，骑着瘸马，我们便追。他们非穿过一条很深的旱谷不可；他们下谷的时候我们放马疾驰，指望在谷底截住他们。可他们上了对岸；我们赶到近沿时，他们恰从那一边冒上头来。立刻就开了火。他们冲那胖子打。

===Original===
“Right away he fell off his horse. He landed on his stomach, and as soon as he landed he began to shoot. He shot between his horse’s legs. He hit one Pah-Ute in the leg and one in the arm. Then we knew why he had been brought along, that fat man.

===Chinese===
“他应声落马。他摔了个嘴啃地，一落地就开了火。他从马腿中间打枪。他打中一个派尤特人的腿，又打中一个的胳膊。这下我们明白带那胖子来是干什么用的了。

===Original===
“We wanted to kill them, but the chief American said that they had to go to T’o Nanasdési to be punished. Their horses were no good, so they put them on those two young men’s horses, and sent them back with them and one American to Oljeto. That left me and The Doer. I had never seen him before, that man, but everybody has heard about him. I was anxious to do well in his eyes.

===Chinese===
“我们想杀了他们，可那领头的美国人说，他们得押到托纳纳斯代西去伏法。他们那两匹马不中用了，便把俘虏安在那两个年轻人的马上，打发他们同一个美国人押送回奥尔赫托。这就剩下我和干事汉。那个人我从前没见过，可谁没听说过他。我一心想在他眼里挣个好看。

===Original===
“In the afternoon we came to the mouth of Yotatséyi Canyon. The trail was fresh and clear. The Doer told them that the other end of the canyon was halfway up Napani Mountain, to our right. It went in a big curve, he said. If they got out there, we could never catch them, he said. So he told them to send three men with him, and he would take them straight across there. He would reach it by nightfall, he said. Then, in the morning, we could start in from both ends and catch them, he said. So they did that. I stayed with the four at the lower end. I thought about there being still ten Pah-Utes, but I did not say anything. I did not want to seem afraid, if The Doer was not.

===Chinese===
“下午我们到了约塔塞伊（Yotatséyi）峡谷口。踪迹又新又清楚。干事汉告诉他们，峡谷那一端在纳帕尼山（Napani Mountain）半腰，在我们右手。那峡谷兜一个大弯，他说。他们若打那头出去，我们就永远逮不着了，他说。于是他说，拨三个人跟他走，他带他们照直横插过去。天黑前就能赶到那头，他说。然后明儿一早，我们两头一齐往里攻，就能堵住他们，他说。他们便照办了。我同那四个人留在下头这端。我心里念着派尤特人还剩十个呢，可我一声不吭。干事汉都不显怕，我也不想显出怕来。

===Original===
“We went a little way into the canyon and made camp. The chief American lent me a pair of magic tubes that he had, that you put to your eyes, and they made everything far away look near. I watched with them until I saw The Doer’s smoke signals. He signalled that they had had a fight, and the enemy were coming towards us. So we watched, but we saw no sign of them. It was dark soon after. We watched all night.

===Chinese===
“我们往峡谷里走了一小截，扎下营。那领头的美国人借给我一副神筒，他随身的物件，举到眼前，远处的东西便看着近了。我拿它瞭望，直到望见干事汉的烟讯号。他报的信是：他们交过手了，敌人正朝我们这边来。我们便警戒起来，却没见他们一点影子。天不久就黑透了。我们瞭望了一整夜。

===Original===
“When it was getting near dawn, but no light as yet, we started on foot. As soon as there was a little light, they sent me along the top of the talus along one wall, to look out for ambushes. The canyon was about five hundred paces wide; the walls were at least that high. The chief American watched me with those tubes. I would wave my hand, as a sign that I saw no one, and they would come forward, strung out across the canyon.

===Chinese===
“天临近破晓、还没亮的时候，我们步行出发。刚见一丝亮，他们就派我沿着一面崖壁下碎石坡的坡顶走，提防埋伏。峡谷约有五百步宽；两壁至少也有那么高。那领头的美国人拿神筒望着我。我挥一挥手，表示没见着人，他们便排开一线，横贯峡谷朝前推进。

===Original===
“It was full light when we got near where the canyon turned the corner. I went on ahead again, very slowly. I could look down into the sand and see the tracks that we had been following so long. I felt very much alone. I knew that man was watching me as I crept; that made me feel a little better. My father told me that the warpath was like stalking mountain sheep, only more exciting. I thought that there was more to it than that. Mountain sheep do not shoot back at you: mountain sheep are not people who are thinking about you just as hard as you are thinking about them.

===Chinese===
“大天亮时，我们挨近了峡谷拐角的地方。我又赶到前头去，走得极慢。我低头便望得见沙地，望见我们缀了这么久的那些蹄印。我感到说不出的孤单。我知道那个人正望着我潜行；这教我好受了些。我父亲说过，走征途就像潜近大角羊，只是更教人心跳。我想这里头的名堂还不止这些。大角羊不会朝你还枪：大角羊不是那样的人，一面苦苦算计你，正如你苦苦算计着他。

===Original===
“Then I saw them. They must have made camp up in the middle of Yotatséyi; now they were coming to try to get out this way. There were nine of them, in single file, riding carefully and watching. So I signalled to the Americans, and saw them take cover. I made myself as hard to see as I could. Suddenly I thought that I was enjoying myself a great deal.

===Chinese===
“接着我望见他们了。他们准是在约塔塞伊峡谷中段扎了营；这会儿正要打这一头夺路出去。他们一共九个，一路纵队，骑得小心，四下里张望。我便向美国人发信号，望见他们各自隐伏。我把身子藏得教人尽量瞧不见。忽然间我想：这滋味真是受用极了。

===Original===
“They came around the corner; now I looked right down on them, from behind a bush. One of them, looking around, looked straight at my bush. He was searching for people hiding. I thought that that bush was not nearly thick enough. His eyes passed on, and for just a moment I felt weak.

===Chinese===
“他们绕过拐角；这下我正从一丛灌木后头，居高临下望着他们。其中一个四下张望的，目光直直落在我这丛灌木上。他正在搜寻藏匿的人。我心想那丛灌木实在算不得密。他的目光移了过去，有一霎我只觉浑身发软。

===Original===
“Then the Americans started shooting. So I stood up and gave the war-cry and started. As I was shouting, I thought, they will hear the Coyote howl and know he’s a Wolf. There was a brave just below me in a buckskin shirt with beadwork on it, and a hat with a silver hatband. I noticed all about him while I shot at him. I wanted to kill him; I wanted it to be known that I had killed one. I saw my arrow in the air a long time; I saw it strike. He fell off his horse. Then I saw that he was not dead, and I was glad. I do not know why.

===Chinese===
“这时美国人开火了。我便站起身，发出战吼，冲了出去。我一面喊，一面想：他们听见郊狼嗥叫，就会知道那是一条狼。我下方正有一个勇士，穿一件缀珠绣的鹿皮衬衫，帽子上箍着银帽箍。我朝他放箭的当口，把他周身打量了个透。我要杀他；我要叫人知道我杀过一个。我的箭在空中飞了好久好久；我看见它中的。他从马上栽了下去。后来我见他没死，我心里高兴。我不知道为什么。

===Original===
“There were five of them down now, and the others put up their hands, so the Americans took them prisoners. One had been killed over in the other end of the canyon.

===Chinese===
“这时他们倒下了五个，其余的举起手来，美国人便把他们俘了。还有一个，是在峡谷那一头被打死的。

===Original===
“We went back to Oljeto, and they paid us as they said. Then we had ourselves purified from the blood, and we spent several dollars on canned food. I bought a big paper bag, as big as a hat, full of all different kinds of candy, and went home, and we all ate it.

===Chinese===
“我们回到奥尔赫托，他们如数付了钱。随后我们行了洁净礼，涤去血污，又花了几块钱买罐头吃。我买了一大纸袋糖果，帽子那么大的一袋，各式各样都有，带回家去，大家一块儿分着吃了。

===Original===
“When I was telling about what happened, I got to the part about thinking I was shot, and I started to laugh. I could not stop, I just laughed. So they started calling me by that name. That is all.”

===Chinese===
“每逢我讲起这段事，讲到自以为中了枪那一段，我就笑起来。止也止不住，光是笑。他们便拿那个名字叫起我来。就是这么回事。”

===Original===
Slim Girl said, “I am glad you are a man like that.” She thought to herself, he is a warrior. That was worth ten thousand schools. He must have been about eighteen. Where is basketball against that?

===Chinese===
瘦姑娘（Slim Girl）说：“我真高兴，你是这样一条汉子。”她心里想：他是个战士。单这一条，抵得上一万个学堂。他那时想必十八岁上下。拿篮球跟这个比，又算得了什么？

### III / III

===Original===
Before supper there was the well-mixed drink again, with its attendant elation and the curious feeling at the back of his teeth. He finished the brew.

===Chinese===
晚饭前，又有了那调得匀匀的饮子，带着随之而来的兴奋劲儿，还有牙床后头那古怪的感觉。他把那剂饮子喝尽了。

===Original===
“I should like to take some more of that.”

===Chinese===
“我还想再喝点儿那个。”

===Original===
“That is not a good thing to do.”

===Chinese===
“那可不是件好事。”

===Original===
“Why not?”

===Chinese===
“怎么不好？”

===Original===
“If you take too much you become foolish. You grow old before your time.”

===Chinese===
“喝得太多，人会变糊涂。不到岁数就先见老。”

===Original===
“That would not be good. Perhaps it would be better not to take any. You do not take it.”

===Chinese===
“那可不好。兴许一点儿不沾才对。你就不喝。”

===Original===
“It is not everyone who is able to drink it. It is not meant for women, that drink, it is for men. If I take it, it makes me sick quickly. It is all right for you, you are strong. It just makes you feel well, doesn’t it? You like it, I think?”

===Chinese===
“这东西不是人人都喝得的。它不是给女人预备的，那饮子，它是给男人的。我要是喝，很快就难受。你喝不要紧，你身板结实。它不过叫你觉着舒坦，不是么？你喜欢它，我想？”

===Original===
“Yes, I like it. I think it is good for me.”

===Chinese===
“是，我喜欢。我想它于我有益。”

===Original===
“It is good for you.” And she told herself, “I shall tie you with it. It is another hobble around your feet, so that you will not go away from me.”


===Chinese===
“它于你有益。”她心里却对自己说：“我要拿它拴住你。这是又一条绊在你脚上的索子，好教你再不能离开我。”

## Chapter IX / 第九章

### I / I

===Original===
Time passing and corn growing cannot be seen; one can notice only that the moon has become so much older, the corn so much higher. With a new life almost more regular than the old, yet far more thrilling, with a rich supply of silver and choice turquoise, with horses to trade and a cornfield to care for, and all the world made over new, time for Laughing Boy went like a swift, quiet river under cottonwood trees. For him, life﻿—which had never been a problem﻿—was solved and perfected, with none of Slim Girl’s complication of feeling that such happiness was too good to last. Had he sat down at T’o Tlakai to compose a song of perfection, he could not have imagined anything approaching this.

===Chinese===
时光流逝、玉米生长，都是眼睛看不见的；人能察觉的，只是月亮老了这么多，玉米高了这么多。新的日子几乎比旧的日子还要规律，却远远更动人心魄：手头银子宽裕，绿松石皆是上选，有马可以交易，有玉米田要照料，整个世界像重新造过一遍，对笑娃来说，时光就像三叶杨树下一条湍急而无声的河。在他，生活——从来就不曾是个难题——如今解开了、圆满了，全没有瘦姑娘那种缠夹的心思，总觉得这样的幸福好得过了头，怕不长久。倘若他曾坐在特奥·特莱凯，要编一支圆满之歌，他也想不出有什么能及得上眼前这一切。

===Original===
It had always been a pleasure to him to work in the corn, to help make the green shafts shoot up, to watch them dance, and contrast their deep, full green with the harsh, faded desert. Among his people corn was a living thing; to make a field beautiful was not so far from making a fine bracelet, and far more useful. He drew the precious water into his field thriftily. At its corners he planted the four sacred plants.

===Chinese===
在玉米地里做活计，向来是他的乐事：帮着那些青绿的秆子往上蹿，看它们迎风起舞，把那深浓饱满的绿，同荒漠的粗粝褪色两相映照。在他的族人里，玉米是活物；把一块田弄美了，同打造一只好手镯相去不远，还更有用得多。他把金贵的水省着引进田里，又在田的四角种下四种神圣的植物。

===Original===
Slim Girl did not understand it at first; she had rather wanted to bar it as entailing unnecessary labour, but decided not to say anything. He saw that she thought it dull, drudging work. He did not try to explain it to her directly, but told her the story of Natinesthani and the origin of corn, and taught her the songs about the tall plant growing. When the stalks were past waist-high, he took her to the field at evening, while sunset brought the drab clay bluffs to life with red, and a soft breeze made the leaves swing and whisper. He made her see the whole field in contrast, and the individual hills, the slender plants and their promise, talking to her of Corn Maiden and Pollen Boy, and of how First Man and First Woman were made from corn. Her eyes were opened to it then, as much through understanding how he felt as through what she objectively beheld. After that she worked with him a little, to please him, although she never cared for the backbreaking toil in itself.

===Chinese===
瘦姑娘起初不明白这个；她本想拦下不许，觉得这平添无谓的辛劳，终究还是决定什么都不说。他看得出，她觉得这是乏味的、苦熬的活计。他不曾想法子直接向她解释，只讲给她听纳蒂内斯塔尼的故事、玉米的来历，教她唱那高高的植株生长的歌。等到秆子高过了腰，他趁一个傍晚带她到田里去，那时落日把灰扑扑的泥岩陡崖照得红彤彤地活了过来，软风里叶片摇曳低语。他让她看整块田的对照，也看一座座土丘、一株株细弱的苗和它们的指望，同她讲玉米少女（Corn Maiden）和花粉少年（Pollen Boy），讲第一男人（First Man）和第一女人（First Woman）怎样由玉米做成。她的眼睛这才睁开了——一半是懂得了他心里怎么感受，一半是眼前实见的东西。打那以后她也陪他做一点活，为的讨他喜欢，虽说这份累断腰的苦工本身，她从来喜欢不起来。

===Original===
His silver sold well. His craftsmanship was fine, his invention lively, and his taste in turquoise most exacting. It was strong, pure stuff, real Northern Navajo work, untouched by European influence. Other Indians would buy it in the store, and its barbaric quality caught the tourist’s eye. Slim Girl got in touch with the Harvey agent, finding him a ready buyer at good prices. She liked to think, then, of the many places along the railroad in which strangers were paying for her husband’s work.

===Chinese===
他的银器卖得好。手艺精，构思活，挑绿松石的眼力又顶顶苛刻。那是结实、纯正的东西，地道的北纳瓦霍货色，一丝一毫没沾欧洲影响。别的印第安人会在铺子里买它，它那股蛮野之气又正入游客的眼。瘦姑娘搭上了哈维公司的采办（Harvey agent），发现那是个现成的好买主，价钱也公道。她那时喜欢想：铁路沿线多少地方，外乡人正付钱买她丈夫的活计呢。

===Original===
She had learned not to care much for general opinion of herself, and was surprised to find that this tangible evidence of her mythical husband’s existence, this visible means of support, made a pleasant difference in the trader’s attitude towards her, and eventually in the looks she received from men throughout the town. There was a surprised feeling that she must have been telling the truth about herself, and a grateful decrease in attempts to scrape unwelcome acquaintance with her. As for George Hartshorn, her American, he developed an increased jealousy that she knew how to use.

===Chinese===
她早已学会不大在乎旁人对她的公论，却惊讶地发现：她那子虚乌有的丈夫如今有了这样摸得着的凭据，有了这样看得见的营生，那商人待她的态度便有了可喜的变化，到后来，全城男人望她的眼光也变了。人们惊觉她从前讲自己的话想必竟是实情，那些硬要来同她攀扯、惹她厌烦的招数，也令她感激地少下去。至于乔治·哈茨霍恩（George Hartshorn），她那个美国人，他的醋劲愈发大了，而她正晓得怎么使唤这股醋劲。

===Original===
To complete her idyll, she wanted to weave, and she found it harder than she had expected. She had been taken to school young, before she had become skilled, and now it was almost all forgotten. Laughing Boy even had to teach her the names of her tools. She wondered, as he watched her struggling with the stubborn warp, if he were laughing at her inside himself, if she seemed ridiculous to him. Many times she would have given up had it not been for her natural determination of character, and for knowing how anomalous and incomplete to him was the house in which the woman could not make a blanket. She dearly longed to reconstruct that scene, but after just a little her back would ache, her forearms grow heavy, and in the backs of her hands would be sharp pains, while the threads were like demons to outwit her. The patient, monotonous spinning was pure torture, and she knew little or nothing of dyes.

===Chinese===
为着成全她这牧歌，她要织毯，却发觉比她料想的难得多。她小小年纪就被带进学堂，还没学得手熟，如今几乎忘了个干净。连家什的名字，笑娃都得一一教她。他望着她跟那倔强的经线较劲，她心里犯嘀咕：他肚内是不是在笑她，她在他眼里是不是显得可笑。多少回她都想撂开手，若不是她天生那股不服输的性子，若不是她明白：一个女人织不出毯子的人家，在他看来是何等反常、何等残缺。她一心想把当年那一幕重新拾回来，可是只做一点点，背就酸了，小臂就沉了，手背上一阵阵刺痛，那些线又像捉弄她的鬼魅。那桩磨性子的、单调的纺线活是纯粹的苦刑，至于染料，她几乎一窍不通。

===Original===
Of course, her first blanket was an ambitious one, elaborately designed. The conception was simplified in the making, and the finished product was a quarter of the originally intended size. When she cut the sorry object from the loom, and looked at it, all crooked, irregular, and full of holes, she could have cried. She hid it from him. Many of her later attempts, not fit to go under a saddle or be sold, she destroyed, but this was the first thing she had made. It was a sad failure, but she could see what it was meant to be, and she kept it.

===Chinese===
自然，她的头一条毯子就野心勃勃，图样设计得繁复讲究。织着织着，构思一简再简，织成的成品只有原先打算的四分之一大。等她把那寒碜物件从织机上割下来一看，歪的扭的、七高八低、净是窟窿，她简直要哭出来。她把它藏起来，不给他瞧。后来那些不成功的尝试，垫马鞍不合用、又卖不出去的，她都毁了；只有这头一件亲手做出来的东西——虽是一桩伤心的失败，她却看得出它本要成为什么样子——她把它留下了。

===Original===
She wove perfectly plain strips that might serve to be sat on, and even many of these were hopeless. At times, despite her husband’s encouragement, in his absence she would curse fluently in English and yank at the strings. Few things could make her lose control of herself thus; she wondered at herself for continuing. It was an offering to her beloved and, unconsciously, an expiation for a guilt she had not admitted.

===Chinese===
她也织些全然素净的条毯，好歹能拿来坐的东西；可就连这些，许多也织得没了指望。有时候，全不理会丈夫的鼓励，趁他不在，她会用英语一连串流利地咒骂，狠命扯那些线绳。能叫她这样失态的事本来没有几桩；她自己也纳闷，怎么竟一直撑了下去。这是献给她心上人的祭品，也是——她自己没有觉出来——为一桩不曾认下的罪过做的赎偿。

### II / II

===Original===
On a day when the corn was nearly ripe, she went to work in the field. Tiring, she sat down to rest where she could watch two stalks, with their silk just showing against the sky. Low on the horizon the beginnings of a storm darkened the blue. She called Laughing Boy.

===Chinese===
玉米快熟的一天，她到田里做活。做累了，便拣个地方坐下歇息，正好望得见两棵秆子，须绒刚刚顶出来，衬着天。地平线低处，一场风暴的先头把蓝色压暗了。她唤笑娃。

===Original===
“Show me how they draw the corn in the sand-pictures.”

===Chinese===
“教教我，沙画里他们是怎样画玉米的。”

===Original===
“I do not think I should show you that. You are a woman, and you have never seen the true gods in the Night Chant.”

===Chinese===
“我看这不该画给你看。你是女人，又从没在夜颂仪（Night Chant）里见过真神。”

===Original===
“Perhaps you are right.”

===Chinese===
“也许你说得对。”

===Original===
He was making a decision.

===Chinese===
他正在拿定一个主意。

===Original===
“I shall show you.” He drew in the sand. “We do it like this. Here is blue, here yellow. Here are the tassels, the silk.”

===Chinese===
“我就画给你看。”他在沙地上画起来。“我们是这样画的。这里是蓝，这里是黄。这儿是穗，这儿是须。”

===Original===
“Why do you show me?”

===Chinese===
“你为什么肯画给我看？”

===Original===
“You are not like ordinary people, you have a strength of your own. I do not think any harm will come to you.”

===Chinese===
“你不比寻常人，你自己有一股力量。我想不会有什么祸事落到你身上。”

===Original===
She looked from the conventionalization to the growing stalks; she divided the threatening sky into a design. Her first, elaborate blanket had been a built-up, borrowed idea, her later ones were uninteresting accidents. Now she saw her work complete, loving it and the task of making it. Now she really had something to tell her loom.

===Chinese===
她把目光从那程式化的图形移到生长的秆子上；又把那步步逼来的阴天，分割成了一幅图样。她头一条毯子那种繁复设计，原是堆砌起来的、借来的意思；后来那些，又不过是乏味的偶然。如今她看见自己的作品完完整整，爱它，也爱把它织成的那桩事。这会儿，她才真有东西好讲给她的织机听了。

===Original===
She was impatiently patient with the dyeing and spinning, needlessly afraid that she would lose her inspiration. When she was ready, she worked so steadily that Laughing Boy warned her of the fate of women who wove too much, and forced her to let a day go by. Her muscles were much tougher now, and her fingers had grown clever and hard among the strands.

===Chinese===
染色、纺线，她心急火燎地耐着性子，没来由地怕丢了灵感。齐备之后，她织得那样不住手，笑娃只得拿织得太狠的女人会落下的下场告诫她，硬逼她放掉一天。如今她的筋肉结实多了，手指在线缕中间也磨得又巧又硬。

===Original===
She managed for him to be away with his horses during the last two days, when she finished it. He had not yet returned when, a little despondently, she locked in the selvage, unrolled it on the frame, and sat back on her heels to smoke and look. She did not see what she had conceived. She did not see a living design, balanced and simple, with mated colours. She saw thin, messy workmanship, irregular lines, blunders, coarsenesses. At one place she had forgotten to lock the blue into the green weft, sunlight showed through. The counting of stitches was uneven. The blanket was not even a rectangle.

===Chinese===
最后两天，她设法让他带着马去了外头，就在这两天里，她把它织完了。等她有些没精打采地锁好织边，把毯子在架上展开，退后坐在脚跟上，点起烟来端详，他还没有回来。她看不出自己构思的东西。她看不出一幅活的图样，匀称、简净、颜色两两相配。她看见的是单薄邋遢的活计，歪斜的线条、错处、粗陋。有一处她忘了把蓝锁进绿纬里，阳光从那里透过来。针脚数得不匀。这条毯子甚至不是个长方形。

===Original===
She went quickly away from the house, walking hurriedly and smoking fiercely.

===Chinese===
她快步离开屋子，走得急，烟也抽得凶。

===Original===
“I am not a Navajo; it is not given to me to do these things. Mother was happy when she wove, she was beautiful then. I cannot make anything, and he is gifted. He will despise me in the end. Being able to make something beautiful is important to him. He will feel his house empty without the sound of weaving. They said I was gifted, that man who came that time. ‘My child, just stick to the things of your people and you will do something. You have it.’ ‘Mr. Waters is a very famous artist, you must pay attention to what he says, my dear. He expressed himself much pleased with your pictures. I am sure the whole class is proud of Lillian today.’ Those crayons were easy. Perhaps he would like that. But I want to weave. There is nothing the matter with me. No use. *God damn it to hell! God damn me!* *Chindi, mai, shash, Jee-Cri!* Well, let’s go and look at it. There’s his pony in front of the house. Come on.”

===Chinese===
“我不是纳瓦霍人；这些事没有分给我。妈妈织毯的时候是快活的，那时候她美。我什么都做不出来，他却是天生的巧手。他到头来会看轻我的。能亲手做出美的东西，对他是顶要紧的事。屋子里没有织机的响声，他会觉得这个家是空的。他们倒说过我有天分，当年来过的那个人。‘孩子，只管守着你本族的东西，你会做出名堂来的。你有这个。’‘沃特斯先生（Mr. Waters）是位大名鼎鼎的艺术家，亲爱的，他的话你可要好好听着。他对你那些画表示十分满意。我敢说，今天全班都为莉莉（Lillian）骄傲。’蜡笔画有什么难的。没准儿他会喜欢那个。可我想织毯。我又没有什么毛病。没用。该死，真真该死！叫我下地狱去吧，连我一块儿咒死吧！Chindi, mai, shash, Jee-Cri!（鬼魂、郊狼、熊、耶稣基督！）好了，去瞧瞧它吧。屋前头拴着他的小马呢。走吧。”

===Original===
He had taken down the blanket and was pegging it out carefully. All he said was,

===Chinese===
他已经把毯子取了下来，正细心地用木钉把它张平。他只说了一句，

===Original===
“Where are your wool cards?”

===Chinese===
“你的梳毛板（wool cards）呢？”

===Original===
She brought them, two implements like very sharp currycombs, used to prepare the wool for spinning. She sat down to watch him, thinking, Perhaps I shall get very drunk. That might help.

===Chinese===
她把家什取来——两件像磨得飞快的马梳似的东西，是纺线前用来理羊毛的。她坐下看他做，心里想：也许我该大醉一场。没准儿管用。

===Original===
He carded the face of the blanket energetically, so roughly that it seemed a gratuitous insult even to her poor work. The very coarseness of her spinning served his purpose, as the sharp teeth scraped and tore across the design. She wondered if he were trying to efface it. He stood up.

===Chinese===
他起劲地梳刷毯面，下手那么重，纵然对她这份可怜活计，也像是一记无缘无故的羞辱。偏是她纺线的粗陋成全了他的用意：锋利的齿刮过去、撕过去，横扫整幅图样。她疑心他是想把它刮没。他站起身。

===Original===
“Now come here and look.” He put his arm over her shoulder. “You have thought well. The picture is beautiful.”

===Chinese===
“现在过来看。”他把胳膊搭上她的肩。“你想得好。这画很美。”

===Original===
The scraping had torn loose a long wool nap, almost a fur, fluffy and fine, that covered all the errors of the weaving. The sharp edges were lost, but the lovely combination she had dreamed of was there, soft and blurred, as though one saw it through tears. She could see how good her conception had been, how true and sure. She had made a beautiful thing. She looked and looked.

===Chinese===
那一番刮刷，刮起了一层长长的羊毛绒，几乎像一层皮毛，蓬松细软，把织造上一切的错处都盖没了。凌厉的边线不见了，可她梦寐以求的那点可爱的配合还在那里，柔和、氤氲，像隔着一汪泪水望见它。她看得出自己的构思有多好，多么真切、多么笃定。她做出了一件美的东西。她看了又看。

===Original===
He loosened the pegs and turned up the untouched side. As he turned it, he jerked at the corners, throwing the uncertain weave out of shape. It looked like a child’s work.

===Chinese===
他拔松木钉，把没动过的那一面翻上来。翻的时候他扯动四角，把那松浮不稳的织物扯得走了样。它看上去像小孩子的活计了。

===Original===
“I am not telling you a lot of things. I am just letting you see something. I think you understand it.”

===Chinese===
“我不跟你说许多道理。我只让你自己看一样东西。我想你是懂的。”

===Original===
“I understand. You will be able to put my next blanket under your saddle, and be proud of it. Thank you.”

===Chinese===
“我懂。往后我的毯子，你尽管垫在马鞍底下，还能为它得意呢。谢谢你。”

### III / III

===Original===
After that there were long, flawless days when they were at home together, he at his forge, she at her loom, time passing with the thump of the batten, the ring of the hammer or rasp of the file. There were chatter and laughter, songs, and long, rich silences. Work then was all love and inspiration.

===Chinese===
打那以后，是一长串圆满无瑕的日子，两人一道守在家里，他守着他的铁匠炉，她守着她的织机，时光随着打纬刀的咚咚、铁锤的叮当或锉刀的沙沙流过去。有闲话，有笑，有歌，也有长长的、饱满的沉默。那时候，劳作全是爱和灵感。

===Original===
She had known a good many different kinds of pleasure, but this was a new richness, something that did not exhaust itself, but grew, a sharing of achievement, designs, colours; fingers, hands, and brain creating, overcoming. There was the talk and hummed songs. There was a great deal to be silent about. It came to her as she was weaving the coloured threads to her intent, Why was this not enough?

===Chinese===
各样各色的快乐，她领略过好许多，可这是一种新的丰盈，一种不会耗尽、只会生长的东西：共享成就、图样、颜色；手指、手和脑子在创造，在攻克。有絮语，有哼着的歌。也有许多尽在沉默里的事。正当她照着自己的心思织那些彩线，一个念头浮上来：这样的日子，怎么还不够呢？

===Original===
This is it. This is the thing I have always wanted. There is nothing better; why endanger it. Why not let that man go now? Why not just do this?

===Chinese===
就是这个了。这就是我向来要的东西。再没有比这更好的了；为什么还要拿它去冒险。何不趁现在放那个男人走？何不就只这样过下去？

===Original===
The batten thumping down on the weft, the hammer ringing on white metal.

===Chinese===
打纬刀咚地砸在纬线上，铁锤在白亮的金属上敲得叮当响。

===Original===
As long as I keep on my way, there is danger. I could never go back to what used to be now. This is what is worth while. A hogan in the Northern desert would be beautiful now.

===Chinese===
只要照我这条路走下去，就有危险。如今我再也回不到从前那样了。这才是值得的。北边荒漠里的一座霍根屋，如今想来该有多美。

===Original===
Sure fingers interlocking dark blue and black, driving the toothed stick down over the juncture.

===Chinese===
稳当的手指把深蓝和黑互相扣紧，把带齿的打纬杆从交界处打下去。

===Original===
I cannot stop halfway now. I am making a new trail of beauty. When I get through, it will be wonderful. Nothing will ever have been like our life.

===Chinese===
事到如今，我停不得一半。我在开出一条新的美之踪迹。等我走通了，那会是了不起的。到那时，再没有什么日子比得上我们这一份。

===Original===
Lifting the treadle to let a line of crimson follow the shuttle through the design.

===Chinese===
抬起踏板，放一线绯红跟着梭子穿过图样。

===Original===
We shall command money, money will command everything. I have herded sheep, their dust in their lungs, hot, a little girl howling at the sheep. We shall be above that. *Aigisi hogan hojoni.* A little girl watching old Light Man drive by to his summer camp in a buckboard behind two spanking pintos.

===Chinese===
我们要驾驭钱，钱驾驭一切。我放过羊，羊群的尘土呛在肺里，热，一个小姑娘冲着羊群吼。我们要高过那个。Aigisi hogan hojoni（霍根屋里才有美）。一个小姑娘望着老亮汉子（Light Man）赶着两匹神气的花斑马、驾着轻便四轮马车，往他的夏营地去。

===Original===
A tiny touch of white brings the red meander to life, and deepens the thunderous background.

===Chinese===
极小的一点白，让那条红色回纹活了过来，把那雷鸣般的底色也压得更深了。

===Original===
Navajo women are growing old when Americans are just getting really strong. I am not going to turn into a fat old squaw. My dear, my dear, will you be gay when you are old? Your silver is beautiful. Is anything in the world worth the risk of separating your forge and my loom?

===Chinese===
纳瓦霍女人正当美国人才真正强壮起来的年纪，就见老了。我可不打算变成个肥肥胖老的婆娘。亲爱的，亲爱的，你老了还会快活吗？你打的银子是美的。这世上可有什么，值得冒把你的铁匠炉同我的织机拆散的险？

===Original===
The blue shuttle goes under six warp strands, the black, coming under two, meets it. A close weave looks like a true diagonal.

===Chinese===
蓝梭从六根经线底下过去，黑梭从两根底下过来，同它会合。织得密了，看上去就像一道真正的斜纹。

===Original===
Are you afraid now, Came With War? I can handle these men. I make my own trail, and I do not stop halfway. I shall make something perfect, that nobody else has made. If I stop now, I might as well stop work on this blanket, after all it cost me to learn to make it. I shall pay myself back for everything that has been.

===Chinese===
这会儿你怕了吗，携战而来？这些男人，我拿得住。我自己的路自己开，而且从不在半道上停。我要做出一件十全十美的、旁人谁也没做出来的东西。倘若现在停下，倒不如索性撂下这条毯子——毕竟学织它费了我偌大本钱。从前的一切，我都要连本带利偿回自身。

===Original===
A single weft strand has no thickness at all, and a blanket is long. It needs patience to finish it, and to make it beautiful, one must not be afraid of the colours.

===Chinese===
一根纬线几乎没有厚度，而一条毯子很长。要织完它须得耐性；要叫它美，就不能怕那些颜色。

===Original===
Laughing Boy, having done his thinking and made up his mind, did not mull over his decision, any more than when he had started a bracelet; he worried whether it ought to have been a necklace. If he did think of other forms, it was only in reflecting that after this was done he would make more, and always more.

===Chinese===
笑娃想过了、拿定了主意，便不再反刍这个决定，正如他起手打一只手镯时，从不操心它原该是条项链。若说他还想旁的样式，那也只是想着：这件做完，还要做更多，永远更多。

===Original===
You make your dies out of iron files, you get some small piece of iron from a trader for your anvil. In a hard wooden board you cut depressions for hammering out bosses and conchos and hemispheres for beads. When you have bought or made your tools, and have your skill, you go ahead. You make many things, rings, bracelets, bow-guards, necklaces, pendants, belts, bridles, buttons, hatbands. No two are alike, but they are all of the silver, or of silver and turquoise.

===Chinese===
冲模拿铁锉刀改，铁砧跟商人讨一小块铁来做。在坚硬的木板上凿出凹窝，好锤打出圆凸饰、康乔银扣（conchos）和做珠子用的半球。家什置办齐了、也自己做得了，手艺在身，就只管做下去。做的东西多得很：戒指、手镯、弓护腕、项链、坠饰、腰带、辔头、纽扣、帽带。没有两件是相同的，可件件都是银的，或是银和绿松石的。

===Original===
Having what he had, he went ahead with living. There were many days, all different, some of high emotion, some of mere happiness, but they were all made of the same stuff; there was one element beautified in all of them. So he worked, content.

===Chinese===
既有了他所有的一切，他便只管把日子过下去。日子多得很，各各不同，有的高昂，有的只是快活，可都是同一种料子做成的；每一个日子里，都有同一样东西点化成了美。他这样劳作着，心满意足。

===Original===
When he had made something that had truly satisfied him, he would give it to her, saying, “That is for you. There is no use selling that to Americans, they do not understand.”

===Chinese===
每当他做出一件真正让自己满意的东西，便把它送给她，说：“这是给你的。这东西卖给美国人没用，他们不懂。”

===Original===
It always pleased her, but she would appraise the jewelry carefully, checking it against their mutual profits, his sales and horse trades, her blankets, and what she brought from the town. If it could be paid for, she put it away, otherwise she required that it be earned. Her primitive banking won his astonished admiration. For her, it was a happy symbol that their fortune, however earned, should be stored in things of beauty.

===Chinese===
这总叫她欢喜，可她还是要细细给首饰估价，对着两人共有的进项盘算：他卖银器的钱和马匹生意，她织的毯子，还有她从镇上带回来的东西。账上付得起的，她便把它收起来；付不起，她就要它被挣回来。她这套原始的银钱法子，赢得他惊讶的钦佩。在她，这是一桩快活的象征：他们的家财，不论怎么挣来的，都存放进美的东西里。

===Original===
And every day, at the end, the sun went down and the harsh horizons dimmed. Then there was the magic drink ready for him, and after that a banquet. They spoke dreamily in the firelight, side by side, and knew a great intimacy. They were not two individuals, but two parts who together made a whole, and there was no cleavage between them.

===Chinese===
而每一天，到了头，太阳落下去，粗粝的地平线暗下来。那时节，给他备好的那杯神奇饮料在等着，随后是一桌盛宴。他们并排在火光里梦一般地说着话，享有一种深深的亲昵。他们不是两个个人，是合起来成一个整体的两半，中间没有一丝裂缝。

## Chapter X / 第十章

### I / I

===Original===
The first time Laughing Boy rode away to Natahnetinn with the horses, he rebelled against the need to leave their tiny valley, and against the prospect of recurring trips, some of several days’ duration. But very shortly he found that, no matter how much in love, a man needs both time to himself, alone, and in general periods of being away from the sphere which is permeated by the influence of a woman. He had a use for these days alone. After all, at the end of a day the sun always set, and it was less than half the time that a strayed animal, a bit of trading, or the need of moving his herd kept him away overnight.

===Chinese===
头一回赶着马群上纳塔内廷（Natahnetinn）去的时候，笑娃（Laughing Boy）对非离开他们那条小山谷不可心怀抵触，对往后还要一趟一趟反反复复地出远门——有的一趟就是好几天——更是抵触。可没过多久他便发现，一个男人不管情陷得多深，都需要有独自一人的时光，而且总得隔三差五离开那个被女人的气息浸透了的世界。这些独自在外的日子自有它们的用处。说到底，一天到了头，太阳总要落山；真因为寻一头走失的牲口、做点买卖，或是得把马群赶去别处而在外头过夜的，十回里头不到一半。

===Original===
Here he could ride the range and sing. Here it was that he thought of the best designs for his silver. It was beautiful, too, watching the long-maned ponies in the good grass, or coming down to water. Then there was the trading, meat and drink to a Navajo﻿—patience, bluff, deception, penetration. It was so pleasant to sit down with another Indian for a long morning of smokes, gossip, and business, learning all the news and driving a close bargain. Very few of his people ever came by his house, and those were mostly specimens like Yellow Singer. He did not want anyone there; that was a place apart, just as here he always had the feeling of a secret knowledge he could not share, something beyond the comprehension of the men he encountered.

===Chinese===
在这里，他可以纵马来回巡看，放声歌唱。正是在这里，他想出了他那些银器上最好的图样。看长鬃的小马立在丰美的草里，或是下到水边去饮水，也是一桩美事。再有就是做买卖——对纳瓦霍人来说，这跟饭食一样是要紧的——凭的是耐心、唬人、使诈和看透人心。跟另一个印第安人对坐一上午，抽烟、闲磕牙、谈生意，把各样新闻听个遍，再把价钱抠得死紧，实在叫人愉快。他的族人极少走到他家那一带去，去的也多半是黄颂唱师（Yellow Singer）那一流的人物。他不愿意任何人在那里；那是一方别样的天地，正如在这里，他心里总揣着一种没法与人分享的隐秘见识，一样他遇上的那些男人无从理解的东西。

===Original===
He listened to the gossip, jokes, and talk about women that was frank enough, seeing in it all that they had no idea of what he knew. He did not try to speak of his wife, knowing that he could never tell them about her, nor yet make a pretense of speaking as if she were just a wife, as they did. Few ever asked after her, and then in a tone of a certain constraint, though their words were formal enough. He had expected something of the sort, after what his uncle had said; she broke the rules and upset things. If they knew her, she was troublesome to them. Of course they resented the disturbance of their minds, and called it bad, with tales that grew in telling. So he sat, as it were, on the edge of their domestic discussions. When it was a matter of horses, he came to be listened to with respect. Everyone agreed that he knew horses, and that he was an excellent trader; when he was speaking about a horse he was trying to sell at the moment, nothing he said was believed.

===Chinese===
他听着那些闲话、笑话，还有那些够直露的关于女人的谈论，看得出他们对他知道的种种一无所知。他不设法说起自己的妻子，因为他知道自己永远没法把她讲给他们听，也没法装模作样，照他们的口气说起她，好像她只是寻常一个老婆似的。极少有人问起她，问起时话音里也带着几分拘束，尽管字面上足够客气。舅舅说过那些话之后，他就料到会有这般光景；她坏了规矩，搅乱了章法。他们若是认得她，她便是给他们惹麻烦的人。他们自然恼恨心里这份不自在，便说那是邪性，传来传去，越传越邪。于是每逢他们谈家事，他就像坐在圈子边上的人。可一说到马，大家便都恭恭敬敬听他讲。人人都承认他懂马，是个顶好的买卖人；只是每当他夸起自己眼下正要出手的那匹马，他说的任何一个字都没人信。

===Original===
Trading was brisk and profitable. His own people were active enough in it. Hopis came down that way, and occasionally a Zuni would pass by. A tourist company in Los Palos was having a good season; they found it convenient to tell Slim Girl that they wanted so many ponies delivered on such a day. They often got fearful cat-meat, but always sound, and profitable for all with the Easterners paying two dollars a day.

===Chinese===
买卖做得红火，进项可观。他自家族人在这一带做得够勤快的。霍皮人（Hopi）也往这边来，偶尔还有祖尼人（Zuni）路过。洛斯帕洛斯一家做游客生意的公司正赶上好时节；他们图省事，便告诉瘦姑娘（Slim Girl）某天要送多少匹小马过去。他们收到的常是些破得吓人的劣马——活脱脱喂猫的货色——倒都个个结实；东部人一天出两块钱，两头都有得赚。

===Original===
His profits went back into the herd. One by one he was getting himself animals that satisfied him, that made him happy to touch and proud to ride them. When the day came that they went back to T’o Tlakai, they would bring fine blankets and much jewelry on splendid horses. He made a pair of brass-mounted saddles, and began, little by little as he earned the silver, a squaw’s bridle that should be envied from the San Juan to the Little Colorado.

===Chinese===
赚来的钱又都投回马群里。一匹接一匹，他给自己置办下称心的牲口，摸着欢喜，骑着自豪。等回特奥·特莱凯（T’o Tlakai）的日子到来，他们要骑着骏马，带上上好的毯子和许多银首饰。他打了两副镶黄铜的马鞍，又随着银钱一笔笔挣进来，一点点动手打造一副婆娘用的笼头——从圣胡安河到小科罗拉多河，要教人人见了都眼红的一副。

===Original===
Those days afield ceased to be penalties. As he settled in the saddle at dawn, it was rather like reentering the old, familiar life into which he carried the enchanted quality of the new. The trail to Natahnetinn was still cool; he loped and enjoyed himself. There would be the action of rounding up a loose pony, the pleasure of feeling a neat-footed horse under one, chance meetings, talk, and trade.

===Chinese===
那些在外的日子不再是苦刑。黎明时分跨进马鞍，倒像是重新走进旧日熟悉的生活，又把新生活的魔力随身带了进去。去纳塔内廷的路上还凉着；他大步跑着马，自得其乐。总会有围拢一匹散马的忙活，有胯下一匹踏步利落的马带来的痛快，有偶遇，有闲谈，有买卖。

===Original===
Almost best of all was to sit on a knoll, smoking and watch the animals feed. One never sees a horse so well as when he is grazing close by, intent upon the grass, oblivious of the man. Then one sees how he moves his ears, how he blows through his soft nostrils, how his casual movements are made. He moves from clump to clump, making his selections by standards of his own, never still, yet entirely free of the restlessness of a stalled horse. It is the essence of pastoral life. Cigarette smoke rises lazily in the hot air, the sun is comfortable upon one’s bones, the gently moving animals make peace.

===Chinese===
差不多顶好的，要数坐在一座小丘上，抽着烟看牲口吃草了。马在近旁专心吃草、把人忘在脑后的时候，人才把马看得最真切。那时你才看清它怎样转动耳朵，怎样从柔软的鼻孔里喷气，那些随随便便的动作是怎么做出来的。它从一丛草挪到另一丛草，按自己的标准挑挑拣拣，从没有一刻静止，却又全然没有圈在栏里的马那股焦躁。这便是牧人生活的真味。纸烟的烟在热空气里懒懒地升起，太阳晒在骨头上暖洋洋的，轻轻走动着的牲口叫人心里一片安宁。

===Original===
He did his thinking then, detached from his emotions, mildly introspective, reflective. He would weigh each thing and value it, go back, retrace, and balance. It was one thing to have made up his mind, another to know exactly where he was﻿—the difference between setting out on a new trail and marking down all the landmarks of the discovered country. The horse shifted from clump to clump, making soft noises, hooves in sand, and crunching. Cigarette smoke wavered and turned with breezes too soft to feel, the movement of the heat in the air. Thoughts became pictures, changing slowly.

===Chinese===
他就在那时想事情，把情绪撇在一旁，带着几分内省，几分回味。他会把每一件事掂一掂、估个值，再回头、重走、求得平衡。拿定主意是一回事，确切知道自己身在何处是另一回事——就像动身走一条新路，和把探明白的那片地界一一记下界标，是两回事。那匹马从一丛草挪到另一丛草，弄出细碎的声响，蹄子踏在沙里，嘴里咯吱咯吱地嚼。纸烟的烟随着拂不动人皮肤的微风摇曳、打转，那是空气里热气的流动。念头化成了图画，缓缓变换。

===Original===
He had accepted Slim Girl’s difference and unconventionality, but for some time still she occasionally startled him. He wanted to understand her; he thought he was sure of what she was, but yet admitted that there were things about her that were beyond him. And for some reason, he always resented the idea of her working in the town. Not that it was a novelty for Navajos to work for Americans, or that he had any means of taking an attitude towards menial labour. His people had owned slaves in the old days; a few still survived, but he had no particular idea of the position of a servant. Yet he wished she would not go there. Then again, he sympathized entirely with her idea of amassing a fortune. Perhaps it was just because the town and its Americanism were part of an unknown world, perhaps because when she returned from there she seemed so tired, and once or twice he had surprised in her eyes a puzzling look, a look of a man who has just killed and scalped a hated enemy. But it was no use his trying to form an opinion. He did not know his way here; with only his people’s judgments and measures, he could decide nothing. He certainly could not expect everything to be the same. As well expect, when one had ridden beyond Old Age River, into the Mormon country, to turn and still see Chiz-na Hozolchi on the eastern horizon.

===Chinese===
他早已接受了瘦姑娘的与众不同和不守常规，可此后好一阵子，她仍时不时叫他吃惊。他想理解她；他自以为对她的为人有了把握，却也承认她身上有些东西他参不透。而且不知为什么，一想到她在镇上做工，他心里总不痛快。倒不是说纳瓦霍人替美国人做工算什么新鲜事，也不是他对低贱的营生有什么架子好摆。他的族人在旧日里蓄过奴；有几个到如今还在，可他对仆役的位分并没有什么特别的成见。然而他不愿她往那儿去。可另一面，她那份攒下一份家业的念想，他又全然赞同。也许只因为那镇子和它那套美国做派属于一个未知的世界，也许因为她从那里回来时总显得那么疲惫，有一两回，他还在她眼里撞见过一种叫人费解的神情——像一个刚杀了仇敌、割下头皮的男人会有的神情。可他想下个断语也是白想。在这上头他不认得路；单凭他族人的判断和尺度，他什么也断不了。他自然不能指望样样都和原先一样。那就好比指望一个人骑马过了暮年河（Old Age River）、进了摩门人的地界，回头还望得见东边地平线上的 Chiz-na Hozolchi。

===Original===
On those few occasions when she warned him that the missionary’s wife would want her to stay overnight, he did not like to come home. He tried it once, and found that the house without her was a long song of emptiness. Usually he would stay with some friends on the reservation, feeling a little patronizing towards their family life, slightly disturbed only by the presence of their children. Those nights he missed his drink, finding himself with but a poor appetite for supper, and with little desire for talk. Their food seemed coarse to him nowadays.

===Chinese===
有那么几回，她提醒他说，女传教士会要她留宿过夜，他就不愿意回家。他试过一回，发觉没有她的房子是一支绵长而空落的歌。他通常便留在保留地上哪个朋友家里，对他们的家庭生活怀着几分俯就，只有他们孩子的在场叫他微微不安。那些夜晚他惦记自己那口咖啡，晚饭没了胃口，也提不起说话的兴致。如今他们家的饭食，在他嘴里显得粗了。

===Original===
Aside from all other things, going away was worth while for the sake of coming back, well tired, to be greeted at the house. It was so different from coming back to T’o Tlakai. There was a thrill in riding up to the door, particularly when he came on a newly traded, yet finer horse. Or it was a real source of pleasure to bring in a string for the tourist company, whooping at them as they debouched from the narrow place between the bluffs, herding and mastering them at a run, into the corral, conscious of Slim Girl leaning in the doorway, delightfully aware of her admiration. There would be news, talk, and all the magic when the sun began to set. Quite often he was first home. He would amuse himself by arranging things for supper, piling wood, drawing water. He learned to handle the can-opener. Then she would come through the opening; he would see her pace quicken as she noticed his horse in the corral, and he would sit back, smiling, to receive her smile.

===Chinese===
撇开旁的不谈，单为着回家这一桩，出门就值得——带着一身痛快的疲惫，到家时有人迎接。这跟回特奥·特莱凯是完全两样的滋味。骑马朝门口走去有一股兴奋劲儿，尤其是骑着一匹新换来的、更神气的马的时候。给游客公司赶一串小马回来也是一桩真正的乐事：看它们从断崖间那道窄口里涌出来，朝它们吆喝，奔跑着圈拢它们、降住它们，赶进围栏，一边知道瘦姑娘正倚在门口，得意地察觉到她眼里的赞叹。接着便有新闻，有闲谈，还有太阳西沉时分的种种魔法。常常是他先到家。他便自己寻乐子，张罗晚饭，码柴，打水。他学会了使开罐器。随后她便从门洞里进来；他看见她一眼望见围栏里他的马，脚步便快起来，他向后一靠，微笑着，去接她的微笑。

### II / II

===Original===
One day he raced home before a thunderstorm that caught him just at the end, first a fine spray, then such a drenching as one might get from buckets, then the spray again, and a pale sun that had no warmth. The valley was all in shadow when he reached the house; he was wet and cold. She had not arrived yet. He built up the fire, and then, searching for coffee, came across the bottle. That was just the thing. But no, he decided, it is she who understands that, and went on looking for coffee. He found the package, empty. Well, he would try a little whiskey.

===Chinese===
有一天，他赶在一场雷雨前一路奔回家，雨恰恰在末了追上他：先是一层细密的水雾，接着兜头一顿猛浇，像一桶桶水泼下来，然后又是水雾，再往后，是一轮没有热气的白淡淡太阳。他到家时，整条山谷都罩在暗影里；他浑身湿透，发冷。她还没有回来。他把火拨旺，随后找咖啡，撞见了那瓶酒。那正合适。可是不，他拿定主意，这东西要她才懂，便接着找咖啡。他找到纸包，空的。好吧，那就尝一点威士忌。

===Original===
There was no fruit in the house, so he poured himself about half a mug of clear liquor. Bah! It was filthy-tasting stuff.

===Chinese===
家里没有果子，他便给自己倒了约莫半缸子的净酒。呸！这东西的味道脏得很。

===Original===
“Mule’s water!” he said.

===Chinese===
“骡子喝的水！”他说。

===Original===
But even that little taste was warming. He sugared the whiskey, held his nose, and bolted it. First he felt sick to his stomach, then he began to feel better. *Ei-yei!* There was a fire in the middle of him, he was warm all over. He was walking on air. He rolled and lit a cigarette. He began to feel so well! He sang,

===Chinese===
可就那么一点沾唇的暖意也上来了。他在威士忌里搁了糖，捏住鼻子，一口灌了下去。起初他胃里发恶心，跟着便开始觉得好受了。咿-耶！他身体正中烧起一团火，浑身都暖了。他脚下像踩着云。他卷了一支烟，点上。他开始觉得浑身舒坦！他唱起来，

===Original===
“Now with a god I walk,
						

						Striding the mountaintops﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
“如今我与一位神明同行，
						

						大步跨过群山的峰顶……”

===Original===
That was the way to take it, it was fine stuff. He wished Slim Girl would come soon. He thought of many things to say to her. He would make her see how he felt about her, how beautifully he understood her. She must know what wonderful things he knew how to say, how perceptive he was. She must stop thinking about all those things she was always thinking about, and drink some of this, and sing with him. There would be such love as never had been in all the world before. Tomorrow he would bring his horses and they would ride to T’o Tlakai, and if that missionary’s wife said anything about it, he would shoot her and tie her scalp on his bridle. It was foolish working for her, when his jewelry and his horses were so entirely sufficient. Life in T’o Tlakai would be a dream. He could see just how beautiful it would be. A little more whiskey would be good.

===Chinese===
这么喝才对路，这是好东西。他巴不得瘦姑娘快些回来。他想起许多要对她说的话。他要让她看清他对她怀着怎样的心，把她懂得多么美。她必须知道他有多少妙话会说，他的眼力有多好。她不该再想她那些成天想不完的事，该喝上一点这个，跟他一起唱。那将是开天辟地以来不曾有过的恩爱。明天他就把马带来，他们骑马回特奥·特莱凯去；那女传教士要是敢多一句嘴，他就一枪崩了她，把她的头皮拴在笼头上。替她做工是犯傻，他有银饰手艺，有马群，尽够了。特奥·特莱凯的日子会是一场好梦。那日子会有多美，他看得清清楚楚。再来一点威士忌才好。

===Original===
It went down more easily, a second half-mug, nearer full than the last, on his empty stomach.

===Chinese===
这一回下去便容易多了，又是半缸子，比上一回满些，灌进他空空的肚肠里。

===Original===
“T’o Tlakai and children.” He said out loud, “I want some children.” And began to feel sorry for himself. Then he began to feel sick. He felt very sick. Everything was dark and whirling, and he was miserable. He fell upon the floor, hiding his eyes to see if things wouldn’t stay still. Immediately the floor began to rise on end, higher and higher; soon he would be pitched against the wall. He opened his eyes, the floor went back to level, but the whole business span. Then he was racked. The world heaved and bucked, waters roared in his ears. Then he went out completely.

===Chinese===
“特奥·特莱凯，还有孩子。”他出了声，“我想要几个孩子。”随即顾影自怜起来。跟着他开始觉得难受。他难受极了。四下里一片黑，天旋地转，他苦不堪言。他扑倒在地上，掩起眼睛，试试东西能不能安分待着不动。地板立刻竖了起来，越竖越高；眼看就要把他甩到墙上去了。他睁开眼，地板放平了，可这整个乾坤还在打转。随后他整个人被掏空。世界掀起又颠跳，轰鸣的水声灌满两耳。然后他便彻底人事不省了。

===Original===
She, too, was tired when she came in, having been kept back by the storm. She looked down on him, heaved a sigh, and then smiled as a mother might whose child had done something forbidden and hurt himself just enough to learn a lesson. Very gently, she pulled him so that his head lay in the ashes. One arm fell across his spew. She put the uncorked bottle beside him, where he would smell the stuff when he came to. She nodded to herself. It was well enough, lucky to have happened just like this. It would teach him. The place did not smell very well. She took some food and a blanket, and went up to the tree on top of the bluff.

===Chinese===
她进屋时也累了，是被暴风雨耽搁下的。她低头看他，长叹一声，随即笑了，像一个母亲那样笑——孩子做了犯禁的事，又恰好把自己磕碰得足以长个记性。她轻轻地拉他，让他的头枕进灰烬里。他一条胳膊搭在自己的呕吐物上。她把开了塞子的瓶子放在他身旁，等他醒来，一闻便知是怎么回事。她自顾自点了点头。这样也够了，事情偏偏就这样发生，算运气好。这会教他学个乖。这地方的气味不太好闻。她拿了些吃食和一条毯子，上崖顶那棵树底下去了。

### III / III

===Original===
The corn matured and was harvested. Seedling peaches that he had set out began to lose their leaves. First frost appeared in the night. The season of thunderstorms had passed; now was the time when one might say the names of the gods. Laughing Boy, riding herd, felt the tang in the air and touched his bow. This was good hunting weather, if one could go to the mountains. Down here there was nothing save the usual prairie dogs, coyotes, and jack rabbits. He began to feel restless.

===Chinese===
玉米熟了，收进了家。他栽下的桃树苗开始落叶。初霜在夜里降临。雷雨的季节过去了；如今是可以称呼诸神名字的时候了。笑娃骑马看管马群，觉出空气里那股凛冽，摸了摸他的弓。这正是打猎的好天气，若是能上得山去。这一带低处却什么也没有，只有照旧的草原犬鼠、郊狼和长耳兔。他心里开始发起躁来。

===Original===
One day he met two braves dressed in all their best and fanciest, one on a roan and one on a pinto. His own horse, freshly caught, was prancing as he rode up to them; theirs were lively.

===Chinese===
有一天他遇见两个勇士，浑身上下穿戴着自己最好最花哨的行头，一个骑着沙毛马，一个骑着花斑马。他自己那匹刚逮来的马，朝他们走过去时一路扬着碎步；他们那两匹也精神。

===Original===
“*Ahalani!*”

===Chinese===
“阿哈拉尼（Ahalani）！”

===Original===
“*Ahalani*, Grandfather!”

===Chinese===
“阿哈拉尼，老爷爷！”

===Original===
“Where are you going?”

===Chinese===
“你们上哪儿去？”

===Original===
“To dance at Chilbito. And you, say?”

===Chinese===
“去奇尔比托（Chilbito）跳舞。你呢？”

===Original===
“Just riding around. I have horses here.”

===Chinese===
“随便转转。我的马在这一带。”

===Original===
“You have a good horse.”

===Chinese===
“你有一匹好马。”

===Original===
“He is pretty good. I got him from a Hopi. Let us race.”

===Chinese===
“它还算不错。我从一个霍皮人手里弄来的。咱们赛一场吧。”

===Original===
“Good. How much will you bet?”

===Chinese===
“好。你押多少？”

===Original===
“Five dollars.”

===Chinese===
“五块。”

===Original===
“That is too much for us; bet three.”

===Chinese===
“这数对我们太大了；押三块吧。”

===Original===
“Good. To that tree there?”

===Chinese===
“好。就跑到那边那棵树？”

===Original===
“All right.”

===Chinese===
“行。”

===Original===
*Hé!* His horse did well. Too bad he didn’t have the bay. “*E-é-é-ya!* Come on now, my horse, come on, Grandfather!” Three horses tearing neck and neck, three men bent over their manes, urging. The pinto was nosing ahead. Laughing Boy pressed in his heels, his belly drew tight with the thrill of motion.

===Chinese===
嘿（Hé）！他的马跑得好。可惜他的枣红马没带来。“咿-欸-欸-呀！这就上吧，我的马，跑起来，老爷爷！”三匹马撕着风并颈狂奔，三个人伏在马鬃上催赶。花斑马的鼻子渐渐抢到了前头。笑娃把脚跟往马肚上一磕，肚皮随着飞驰的快意绷紧了。

===Original===
They hurtled past the tree, the pinto slightly ahead, and drew rein, laughing.

===Chinese===
他们飞掠过那棵树，花斑马稍稍在前，三人勒缰，大笑。

===Original===
“You win, Grandfather.”

===Chinese===
“你赢了，老爷爷。”

===Original===
The man received his money.

===Chinese===
那人收下了赢来的钱。

===Original===
“I am sorry I did not have my bay horse here. He is much faster than this one.”

===Chinese===
“可惜我的枣红马不在。它比这匹快得多。”

===Original===
“Bring your bay horse to the dance. There may be some racing, I think.”

===Chinese===
“把你的枣红马带到舞会上来。兴许会有几场赛马，我想。”

===Original===
“What dance is it?”

===Chinese===
“那是什么舞？”

===Original===
“A Night Chant. Wind Singer is leading it.”

===Chinese===
“一场夜颂仪（Night Chant）。领仪的是风颂唱师（Wind Singer）。”

===Original===
“I shall think about coming.”

===Chinese===
“我来不来，再想想。”

===Original===
“It is only a five-day dance. It is for Twice Brave; he has not much money, they say. You had better come soon.”

===Chinese===
“这不过是一场五天的舞。是为双勇（Twice Brave）办的；他没有多少钱，他们说。你最好早点来。”

===Original===
“What made him sick?”

===Chinese===
“他是怎么得病的？”

===Original===
“He looked at his mother-in-law; he spoke to her, they say.”

===Chinese===
“他看了他的岳母；还跟她说话了，他们说。”

===Original===
“*Ei-yei!* How did that happen?”

===Chinese===
“咿-耶！那怎么闹成那样的？”

===Original===
“They lived near each other. When his wife was away, she got his food for him, they say. He came too soon and saw her. She covered her face, but he spoke to her, they say.”

===Chinese===
“他们两家住得近。他老婆不在家的时候，岳母给他张罗饭食，他们说。他回来得早了，撞见了她。她把脸遮上，可他还是跟她说话了，他们说。”

===Original===
“He spoke to her! He is crazy, I think.”

===Chinese===
“他跟她说话了！他是疯子，我想。”

===Original===
“Perhaps he is; he does strange things. When the missionary at Tsé Tlchi used to serve beans, a lot of us went to hear him. He held a sing every seven days, and afterwards there were beans, but there was no dancing. We followed the Jesus Road until he stopped giving us beans. Then Twice Brave went back and stole a lot of red *t’oghlepai* that he had, it was something to do with his religion. It was good. But when he had drunk a lot of it, he went and made his horse drink it. He put the bottle down its mouth and made it take it, the way he had seen an American do. He made his horse crazy, just like a man. I saw it. It couldn’t walk straight. And now he has spoken to his mother-in-law, they say. So he has a bad toothache. You should come to the dance.”

===Chinese===
“兴许是；他尽做稀奇事。特塞特奇（Tsé Tlchi）那个传教士从前分豆子的时候，我们好些人都去听他讲。他每七天行一场颂仪，完了就分豆子，只是没有跳舞。我们跟着走了耶稣之路（Jesus Road），直到他不再分豆子。后来双勇又回去，偷了他许多红色的 t’oghlepai，说是跟他那教门有些关系的物件。那东西好。可他灌下许多之后，又跑去叫他的马也喝。他把瓶子塞进马嘴，逼它咽下去，那架势是照着一个美国人学的。他把马灌疯了，疯得跟人一模一样。我亲眼看见的。那马连直路都走不成。如今他又跟岳母说了话，他们说。所以他牙疼得厉害。你该来这场舞。”

===Original===
“*Ei!* I should like to see that man. I shall come if I can.”

===Chinese===
“咿！我倒想见见这个人。能来我就来。”

===Original===
He was glad that the season of the great dances was returning. As he rode home, he thought that it would be good to see the gods once more, perhaps to know the holy fear and exaltation when one swallowed the sacred arrows inside the Dark Circle of Branches. He loved the gatherings of people, the huge fires, and the holy things. There was religious experience and high thought, and then there was sociability on a large scale. Sometimes there were horse-races or a chicken-pull or gambling.

===Chinese===
大舞会的季节回来了，他心里高兴。骑马回家的路上，他想，若能再见一见诸神该多好，或许还能再度知道那种圣洁的敬畏与昂扬——在暗枝圈（Dark Circle of Branches）里把圣箭吞下去时的滋味。他爱众人的聚会，爱冲天的大火堆，爱那些圣物。那里有宗教的体验和高远的思致，此外还有大规模的欢聚交游。有时候还有赛马、抢鸡赛或者赌钱。

===Original===
He had not thought about these things for so long, or at least he had thought of them distantly, himself apart. As a blanket and its design before dawn is seen, but has no colours, then with clear light grows vivid in red and green and yellow, so the feeling of his tribe swept over him. It was exhilarating.

===Chinese===
这些事他许久不曾想过了，或者至少只是远远地想着，自己置身事外。就像天亮以前的一条毯子和它上面的花样，看得见，却没有颜色，待天光大亮，才在红、绿、黄里鲜亮起来——他部族的情感就这样漫过他的全身。这教人振奋。

### IV / IV

===Original===
He spoke about it to her after supper. “There is to be a Night Chant over at Chilbito, by Tseye Buckho. There may be racing.”

===Chinese===
晚饭后，他跟她提起这事。“奇尔比托那边要办一场夜颂仪，就在特塞耶·巴克霍（Tseye Buckho）近旁。兴许会有赛马。”

===Original===
“How long will the song last?” There was no reason in the world why they should not go. She was searching in her mind, and found only that she dreaded it.

===Chinese===
“歌要唱多久？”天底下没有任何缘由他们不该去。她在心里搜寻，找到的却只有惧怕。

===Original===
“Only five nights. It is for Twice Brave; he is not very rich, they say.”

===Chinese===
“只唱五夜。是为双勇办的；他不很阔，他们说。”

===Original===
“We do not want to go, I think. Let us wait for a complete one.”

===Chinese===
“我看咱们还是不去。等一场齐全的再说。”

===Original===
She understood herself as she spoke; she was jealous of his people, of something they had in common which she could not share.

===Chinese===
说话间她明白了自己；她是在妒忌他的族人，妒忌他们共有的一样东西——她无从分享的那样东西。

===Original===
He looked at her inquiringly, catching a tone of earnestness in her voice. She had no reasons, yet very much did not want to go. He saw that it mattered to her.

===Chinese===
他探询地望着她，从她话音里听出一缕郑重来。她说不出道理，却非常不想去。他看得出这事对她要紧。

===Original===
“Perhaps you are right. We shall wait.”

===Chinese===
“也许你是对的。我们就等。”

===Original===
“I think that is better.”

===Chinese===
“我想这样更好。”

===Original===
Both understood.

===Chinese===
两人都明白了。

===Original===
It was puzzling, though. He wondered about it as they sat there. He wanted to understand her. He told himself: “If she wants me to know, she will tell me. I do not think she knows herself. I have made up my mind, there is no use hesitating on the trail. I make her my life, let her be my life. I do not know why she does this thing, but I know what I think of her. If I knew just what was in her mind, it would be worth thinking about, it would tell me something about her. Now there is so much of her I do not understand. I know what I want, that is enough.”

===Chinese===
不过这事终究叫人纳闷。他们坐在那里，他心里琢磨。他想理解她。他对自己说：“她要是想让我知道，自会告诉我。我看她自己也不知道。我主意已定，在道上踌躇没有用处。我拿她做我的性命，就让她做我的性命。我不知道她为什么做这件事，可我知道我怎么看待她。我若知道她心里究竟装着什么，那倒值得想一想，那会告诉我一些她的为人。如今她身上我不懂的地方还有这么多。我知道我要什么，这就够了。”

===Original===
He watched her in the firelight, her slender lines, her oval face of sleeping fires. The trail of beauty lay within this house; not all the songs and horses in the world were worth this minute.

===Chinese===
他望着火光里的她，那纤秀的身段，那张鹅蛋脸，覆着一层沉睡的火光。美之踪迹就躺在这座屋子里；普天之下所有的歌和马，也换不来这一刻。

## Chapter XI / 第十一章

### I / I

===Original===
The life apart enclosed him again. If some encounter with Indians bound for a dance, some reminiscent incident, brought on a momentary restlessness, he did not have to deal with it. It simply expressed itself in the smug feeling that what he had was so vastly superior to anything in their philosophy. He was a little sorry for those people. When he felt like that, he would stir his pony to a lope, with his head high, uplifted, thinking of Slim Girl, of some little thing to say or do for her. He was a young man very much in love, a young man with his mind made up to love.
===Chinese===
那种离群索居的生活，又把他围拢在当中了。偶尔遇上几个赶去跳舞的印第安人，或是撞见什么勾起旧忆的事端，惹起一阵子的心绪不宁，他也用不着去对付。那份不安自会化解，化成一股自得之气，只觉得自己怀抱里的东西，比他们那套人生道理里的任何货色都高出不知多少。他有点可怜那些人。每逢这种心绪，他便催动小马放开大步跑，昂着头，意气轩昂，心里想着瘦姑娘（Slim Girl），想着能为她说句什么话、做件什么小事。他是个一往情深的年轻人，一个打定了主意要去爱的年轻人。

===Original===
At the beginning of the month of Little Snow, he surprised her by bringing an Indian home with him. She was disturbed and uneasy as she prepared the extra food. There was no reason to be bothered; just because something had never happened before did not make it a bad sign. Underneath all her self-confidence was a feeling which she refused to recognize, that this life of theirs hung by threads. Really, in her heart of hearts, she was surprised that everything ran so smoothly. Little things upset her.
===Chinese===
小雪月（Little Snow）月初，他领了个印第安人回家来，叫她吃了一惊。她张罗着添做饭食，心里七上八下。其实犯不着不安；一件事从前不曾有过，并不因此便成了凶兆。她那一腔自信底下，压着一股她不肯认账的感觉——他们这份日子，全悬在几缕细线上。说真的，在她心底最深处，她一直暗暗诧异，这一切竟过得这般顺当。芝麻绿豆的小事都能搅得她心慌意乱。

===Original===
Long-haired and hatless, the man’s pure Navajo costume, the heavy look of his jewelry, indicated the Northern country. Laughing Boy called him cousin, and questioned him about people and things at T’o Tlakai and all the Gyende district.
